## I / 第一章

===Original===
I Go to Pittsburg

McKnight is gradually taking over the criminal end of the business. I never liked it, and since the strange case of the man in lower ten, I have been a bit squeamish. Given a case like that, where you can build up a network of clues that absolutely incriminate three entirely different people, only one of whom can be guilty, and your faith in circumstantial evidence dies of overcrowding. I never see a shivering, white-faced wretch in the prisoners’ dock that I do not hark back with shuddering horror to the strange events on the Pullman car Ontario, between Washington and Pittsburg, on the night of September ninth, last.

McKnight could tell the story a great deal better than I, although he can not spell three consecutive words correctly. But, while he has imagination and humor, he is lazy.

“It didn’t happen to me, anyhow,” he protested, when I put it up to him. “And nobody cares for secondhand thrills. Besides, you want the unvarnished and ungarnished truth, and I’m no hand for that. I’m a lawyer.”

===Chinese===
我去匹兹堡

麦克奈特（McKnight）正逐步接手我们事务所的刑事案子。我向来不喜欢这行当，而且自打那桩下铺十号的怪案之后，我变得有点神经兮兮了。试想你手头有这么一桩案子：你能编织出一张铁证如山的线索网，把三个截然不同的人统统牵连进去，可他们中间只有一个是有罪的——你对间接证据的信念就会因证据过多而窒息而死。每当我看见被告席上那个瑟瑟发抖、面色惨白的可怜虫，我就忍不住回想起去年九月九日夜晚，在华盛顿与匹兹堡之间的普尔曼车厢"安大略号"（Pullman car Ontario）上发生的那桩离奇事件，至今仍不寒而栗。

麦克奈特讲这个故事会比我在行得多，尽管他连着三个词都拼不对。不过，他虽然有想象力和幽默感，人却懒得很。

"反正这事没摊到我头上，"我跟他提的时候，他这样抗议道。"没人爱听二手惊悚故事。再说了，你要的是不加修饰、毫无美化的真相，我可干不了那个。我是个律师。"

===Original===
So am I, although there have been times when my assumption in that particular has been disputed. I am unmarried, and just old enough to dance with the grown-up little sisters of the girls I used to know. I am fond of outdoors, prefer horses to the aforesaid grown-up little sisters, am without sentiment1 and completely ruled and frequently routed by my housekeeper, an elderly widow.

In fact, of all the men of my acquaintance, I was probably the most prosaic, the least adventurous, the one man in a hundred who would be likely to go without a deviation from the normal through the orderly procession of the seasons, summer suits to winter flannels, golf to bridge.

So it was a queer freak of the demons of chance to perch on my unsusceptible thirty-year-old chest, tie me up with a crime, ticket me with a love affair, and start me on that sensational and not always respectable journey that ended so surprisingly less than three weeks later in the firm’s private office. It had been the most remarkable period of my life. I would neither give it up nor live it again under any inducement, and yet all that I lost was some twenty yards off my drive!

===Chinese===
我也是个律师，尽管我的这个身份时不时会被人质疑。我未婚，年纪刚好能跟以前认识的那些姑娘的成年小妹们跳舞。我喜好户外活动，喜欢马胜过前述那些成年小妹，毫无浪漫情怀，被我的管家——一位上了年纪的寡妇——完全管得死死的，还常常被她杀得片甲不留。

事实上，在我认识的所有男人中，我大概是最乏味、最缺乏冒险精神的那个——一百个人里头唯一可能循规蹈矩、按部就班地从夏天的西装换到冬天的法兰绒、从高尔夫换到桥牌，四季更替中毫无偏离常规的那一个。

所以，命运之魔居然选中了我这副对一切刺激都无动于衷的三十岁胸膛，把我跟一桩犯罪拴在一起，给我贴上一张风流韵事的标签，让我踏上那场惊世骇俗、有失体面的旅程——不到三周后在事务所的私人办公室里画上了一个令人意想不到的句号。那是我一生中最离奇的一段日子。我既不愿放弃它，也绝不愿再重来一次，而我为之付出的代价不过是开球时少了二十码而已！

===Original===
It was really McKnight’s turn to make the next journey. I had a tournament at Chevy Chase for Saturday, and a short yacht cruise planned for Sunday, and when a man has been grinding at statute law for a week, he needs relaxation. But McKnight begged off. It was not the first time he had shirked that summer in order to run down to Richmond, and I was surly about it. But this time he had a new excuse. “I wouldn’t be able to look after the business if I did go,” he said. He has a sort of wide-eyed frankness that makes one ashamed to doubt him. “I’m always car sick crossing the mountains. It’s a fact, Lollie. Seesawing over the peaks does it. Why, crossing the Alleghany Mountains has the Gulf Stream to Bermuda beaten to a frazzle.”

So I gave him up finally and went home to pack. He came later in the evening with his machine, the Cannonball, to take me to the station, and he brought the forged notes in the Bronson case.

===Chinese===
其实下次出差本来就该轮到麦克奈特了。我周六在切维蔡斯有场锦标赛，周日还安排了短途游艇巡航，况且一个人在成文法里埋头苦干了一周，总该放松放松。但麦克奈特又推脱了。整个夏天他为了跑去里士满已经不止一次开溜，我对此态度很不友善。不过这次他有了新借口。"我要是去了，谁来看管生意？"他说。他有种瞪大眼睛的坦诚劲儿，让人不好意思怀疑他。"我翻山越岭的时候总是晕车。这是真的，洛利（Lollie）。在山峰之间来回颠簸最要命了。嘿，翻阿勒格尼山脉比坐船走墨西哥湾流去百慕大还让人受不了。"

于是我最终放弃，回家收拾行李。当晚晚些时候，他开着他的车"炮弹号"（Cannonball）来送我去车站，还带来了布朗森（Bronson）案的那些伪造票据。

===Original===
“Guard them with your life,” he warned me. “They are more precious than honor. Sew them in your chest protector, or wherever people keep valuables. I never keep any. I’ll not be happy until I see Gentleman Andy doing the lockstep.”

He sat down on my clean collars, found my cigarettes and struck a match on the mahogany bed post with one movement.

“Where’s the Pirate?” he demanded. The Pirate is my housekeeper, Mrs. Klopton, a very worthy woman, so labeled—and libeled—because of a ferocious pair of eyes and what McKnight called a bucaneering nose. I quietly closed the door into the hall.

“Keep your voice down, Richey,” I said. “She is looking for the evening paper to see if it is going to rain. She has my raincoat and an umbrella waiting in the hall.”

===Chinese===
"拿命护着它们，"他警告我。"它们比荣誉还值钱。缝进你的护胸甲里头，或者随便你们律师把贵重东西塞哪儿。我从来不存什么贵重玩意儿。我非得看到绅士安迪（Gentleman Andy）踩着小碎步走正步，才能心满意足。"

他一屁股坐在我熨好的衣领上，找到我的烟盒，一只手在红木床柱上划了根火柴。

"海盗呢？"他问道。"海盗"指的是我的管家克洛普顿夫人（Mrs. Klopton），一位非常可敬的女士，之所以被这么称呼——而且是被冤枉了——是因为她有一双凶巴巴的眼睛和一个被麦克奈特形容为"海盗式"的鼻子。我悄悄把通往走廊的门关上了。

"小声点，里奇（Richey），"我说。"她在找晚报，看今晚会不会下雨。她已经把我的雨衣和雨伞搁在走廊里了。"

===Original===
The collars being damaged beyond repair, he left them and went to the window. He stood there for some time, staring at the blackness that represented the wall of the house next door.

“It’s raining now,” he said over his shoulder, and closed the window and the shutters. Something in his voice made me glance up, but he was watching me, his hands idly in his pockets.

“Who lives next door?” he inquired in a perfunctory tone, after a pause. I was packing my razor.

“House is empty,” I returned absently. “If the landlord would put it in some sort of shape—”

“Did you put those notes in your pocket?” he broke in.

===Chinese===
衣领已经被压得没法修复了，他丢下它们，走到窗前。他在那里站了好一阵子，盯着代表隔壁房子墙壁的那团漆黑。

"已经下雨了，"他回头说道，随即关上了窗户和百叶窗。他的语气里有什么东西让我抬起头来，可他正盯着我看，双手漫不经心地插在口袋里。

"隔壁住着谁？"他停顿了一下，用敷衍的语气问道。我正在收拾剃须刀。

"那房子空着，"我心不在焉地答道。"要是房东能把那房子收拾收拾——"

"你把那些票据放口袋里了？"他突然打断我。

===Original===
“Yes.” I was impatient. “Along with my certificates of registration, baptism and vaccination. Whoever wants them will have to steal my coat to get them.”

“Well, I would move them, if I were you. Somebody in the next house was confoundedly anxious to see where you put them. Somebody right at that window opposite.”

I scoffed at the idea, but nevertheless I moved the papers, putting them in my traveling-bag, well down at the bottom. McKnight watched me uneasily.

“I have a hunch that you are going to have trouble,” he said, as I locked the alligator bag. “Darned if I like starting anything important on Friday.”

“You have a congenital dislike to start anything on any old day,” I retorted, still sore from my lost Saturday. “And if you knew the owner of that house as I do you would know that if there was anyone at that window he is paying rent for the privilege.”

===Chinese===
"放了，"我不耐烦地说。"跟我的注册证、洗礼证明和疫苗接种证放一块儿。谁想要它们，得先偷走我的外套才行。"

"要我是你，我就会把它们挪个地方。隔壁房子里有个人正拼命想看清你把东西放哪儿了。就在对面那个窗户那儿。"

我对此嗤之以鼻，但还是把那些票据挪了位置，放进旅行箱，塞到最底下。麦克奈特不安地看着我。

"我有预感你会遇上麻烦，"我锁好鳄鱼皮箱的时候，他说。"真他妈不喜欢在星期五开始什么大事。"

"你是天生就不喜欢在随便哪一天开始做什么事，"我回嘴道，心里还在为失去的那个周六耿耿于怀。"你要是像我一样了解那房子的主人，你就会知道就算那窗户后面真有人，他付了房租才有权在那儿。"

===Original===
Mrs. Klopton rapped at the door and spoke discreetly from the hall.

“Did Mr. McKnight bring the evening paper?” she inquired.

“Sorry, but I didn’t, Mrs. Klopton,” McKnight called. “The Cubs won, three to nothing.” He listened, grinning, as she moved away with little irritated rustles of her black silk gown.

I finished my packing, changed my collar and was ready to go. Then very cautiously we put out the light and opened the shutters. The window across was merely a deeper black in the darkness. It was closed and dirty. And yet, probably owing to Richey’s suggestion, I had an uneasy sensation of eyes staring across at me. The next moment we were at the door, poised for flight.

“We’ll have to run for it,” I said in a whisper. “She’s down there with a package of some sort, sandwiches probably. And she’s threatened me with overshoes for a month. Ready now!”

===Chinese===
克洛普顿夫人敲了敲门，在走廊里谨慎地开口。

"麦克奈特先生带晚报来了吗？"她问道。

"不好意思，没带，克洛普顿夫人，"麦克奈特喊道。"小熊队赢了，三比零。"他站在那儿咧嘴笑着，听她拖着黑丝绸裙窸窸窣窣地走远了，带着一股恼火劲儿。

我收拾完行李，换了领子，准备出发。然后我们小心翼翼地熄了灯，打开百叶窗。对面那扇窗在黑暗中只是一个更深的黑影。窗户关着，很脏。可是，大概是因为里奇那番话的缘故，我总觉得有一双眼睛正隔着窗户盯着我。紧接着我们已到了门口，做好了冲刺的准备。

"我们得跑着出去，"我小声说。"她就在楼下，手里捧着个什么东西，大概是三明治。她已经威胁了我一个月，要给我套上套鞋。准备好了——冲！"

===Original===
I had a kaleidoscopic view of Mrs. Klopton in the lower hall, holding out an armful of such traveling impedimenta as she deemed essential, while beside her, Euphemia, the colored housemaid, grinned over a white-wrapped box.

“Awfully sorry—no time—back Sunday,” I panted over my shoulder. Then the door closed and the car was moving away.

McKnight bent forward and stared at the façade of the empty house next door as we passed. It was black, staring, mysterious, as empty buildings are apt to be.

“I’d like to hold a postmortem on that corpse of a house,” he said thoughtfully. “By George, I’ve a notion to get out and take a look.”

“Somebody after the brass pipes,” I scoffed. “House has been empty for a year.”

===Chinese===
我万花筒般地瞥见克洛普顿夫人站在楼下门厅里，怀里抱着一堆她认为必不可少的旅行用品，身旁的有色人种女仆尤菲米亚（Euphemia）正对着一个白纸裹着的盒子咧嘴笑。

"非常抱歉——没时间了——周日回来，"我气喘吁吁地回头喊道。门关上了，车子开了出去。

经过隔壁那栋空房子时，麦克奈特倾身盯着它的门面。它漆黑、冷峻、神秘，空房子往往都是这副模样。

"我真想对那栋死房子验验尸，"他若有所思地说。"天哪，我真有股冲动想下车看看。"

"有人在打黄铜管的主意，"我嘲弄道。"那房子空了一年了。"

===Original===
With one hand on the steering wheel McKnight held out the other for my cigarette case. “Perhaps,” he said; “but I don’t see what she would want with brass pipe.”

“A woman!” I laughed outright. “You have been looking too hard at the picture in the back of your watch, that’s all. There’s an experiment like that: if you stare long enough—”

But McKnight was growing sulky: he sat looking rigidly ahead, and he did not speak again until he brought the Cannonball to a stop at the station. Even then it was only a perfunctory remark. He went through the gate with me, and with five minutes to spare, we lounged and smoked in the train shed. My mind had slid away from my surroundings and had wandered to a polo pony that I couldn’t afford and intended to buy anyhow. Then McKnight shook off his taciturnurnity.

===Chinese===
麦克奈特一手扶着方向盘，另一只手伸过来要我的烟盒。"也许吧，"他说；"可我搞不懂她要黄铜管干什么。"

"女人！"我放声大笑。"你只是盯着你表壳背面的照片看太久了，就这么回事。据说有个实验说，如果你盯着一张照片看够久——"

但麦克奈特已经闷闷不乐了：他正襟危坐，目视前方，直到把"炮弹号"在车站前停稳，再也没说一句话。即便到了车站，他也只是敷衍了一句。他陪我过了检票口，离发车还有五分钟，我们就在候车棚里闲逛抽烟。我的心思早已从周围环境飘走了，飘到一匹我买不起却无论如何都想买下的马球矮种马身上。这时麦克奈特终于打破了沉默。

===Original===
“For heaven’s sake, don’t look so martyred,” he burst out; “I know you’ve done all the traveling this summer. I know you’re missing a game tomorrow. But don’t be a patient mother; confound it, I have to go to Richmond on Sunday. I—I want to see a girl.”

“Oh, don’t mind me,” I observed politely. “Personally, I wouldn’t change places with you. What’s her name—North? South?”

“West,” he snapped. “Don’t try to be funny. And all I have to say, Blakeley, is that if you ever fall in love I hope you make an egregious ass of yourself.”

In view of what followed, this came rather close to prophecy.

===Chinese===
"看在老天爷份上，别摆出那副殉道者的嘴脸，"他突然爆发道；"我知道整个夏天都是你在出差。我知道你明天有场比赛要缺席。但别像个忍辱负重的老妈子；该死的，我星期天非得去里士满不可。我——我想去见一个姑娘。"

"哦，别在意我，"我客客气气地说。"就我个人而言，我可不想跟你换位子。她叫什么名字——北？南？"

"韦斯特（West），"他厉声说。"别耍嘴皮子。我只有一句话，布莱克利（Blakeley）：你要是哪天坠入了爱河，我巴不得你当个彻头彻尾的大傻瓜。"

考虑到后来发生的事，这话简直算得上预言了。

===Original===
The trip west was without incident. I played bridge with a furniture dealer from Grand Rapids, a sales agent for a Pittsburg iron firm and a young professor from an eastern college. I won three rubbers out of four, finished what cigarettes McKnight had left me, and went to bed at one o’clock. It was growing cooler, and the rain had ceased. Once, toward morning, I wakened with a start, for no apparent reason, and sat bolt upright. I had an uneasy feeling that someone had been looking at me, the same sensation I had experienced earlier in the evening at the window. But I could feel the bag with the notes, between me and the window, and with my arm thrown over it for security, I lapsed again into slumber. Later, when I tried to piece together the fragments of that journey, I remembered that my coat, which had been folded and placed beyond my restless tossing, had been rescued in the morning from a heterogeneous jumble of blankets, evening papers and cravat, had been shaken out with profanity and donned with wrath. At the time, nothing occurred to me but the necessity of writing to the Pullman Company and asking them if they ever traveled in their own cars. I even formulated some of the letter.

===Chinese===
西行之旅平安无事。我跟一位来自大急流城的家具商、一位匹兹堡钢铁公司的销售代理以及一位东部大学的年轻教授打了几局桥牌。四局赢了三局，抽完了麦克奈特给我留下的所有香烟，一点钟上床睡觉。天越来越凉了，雨也停了。有一次，快到黎明时分，我突然惊醒——毫无来由地——猛地坐了起来。我有一种不安的感觉，好像有人在看着我，跟傍晚在窗前经历过的那种感觉一模一样。但我能摸到那个装着票据的箱子，就搁在我和窗户之间，我把胳膊搭在上面护着它，又沉沉睡去了。后来我试图拼凑那次旅程的碎片时，想起我的外套——它本来叠好放在那儿，远离我辗转反侧的范围——第二天早上却从一堆乱七八糟的毯子、晚报和领带中被解救出来，我一边骂骂咧咧地把它抖开，一边怒气冲冲地穿上。当时我脑子里只想着一件事：得给普尔曼公司写封信，问问他们自己坐自己的车厢时是不是也这么颠簸。我甚至在脑子里拟好了几行措辞。

===Original===
“If they are built to scale, why not take a man of ordinary stature as your unit?” I wrote mentally. “I can not fold together like the traveling cup with which I drink your abominable water.”

I was more cheerful after I had had a cup of coffee in the Union Station. It was too early to attend to business, and I lounged in the restaurant and hid behind the morning papers. As I had expected, they had got hold of my visit and its object. On the first page was a staring announcement that the forged papers in the Bronson case had been brought to Pittsburg. Underneath, a telegram from Washington stated that Lawrence Blakeley, of Blakeley and McKnight, had left for Pittsburg the night before, and that, owing to the approaching trial of the Bronson case and the illness of John Gilmore, the Pittsburg millionaire, who was the chief witness for the prosecution, it was supposed that the visit was intimately concerned with the trial.

===Chinese===
"你们的车厢要是按比例缩放的，为什么不拿一个普通身材的人当基准单位呢？"我在心里写道。"我没法像你们那个用来喝那难以下咽的水的旅行杯那样折叠起来。"

在联合车站喝了杯咖啡之后，我心情好多了。时间还太早，没法去办正事，我就在餐厅里闲坐，用晨报挡住自己。不出所料，他们已经知道了我的来访及其目的。头版上赫然登着一条消息：布朗森案的伪造票据已被带到匹兹堡。下面还有一则华盛顿的电讯称，布莱克利-麦克奈特事务所的劳伦斯·布莱克利（Lawrence Blakeley）已于前夜动身前往匹兹堡，鉴于布朗森案即将开庭审理，且控方主要证人——匹兹堡百万富翁约翰·吉尔摩（John Gilmore）身患疾病，此行被认为与案件审理密切相关。

===Original===
I looked around apprehensively. There were no reporters yet in sight, and thankful to have escaped notice I paid for my breakfast and left. At the cabstand I chose the least dilapidated hansom I could find, and giving the driver the address of the Gilmore residence, in the East End, I got in.

I was just in time. As the cab turned and rolled off, a slim young man in a straw hat separated himself from a little group of men and hurried toward us.

“Hey! Wait a minute there!” he called, breaking into a trot.

But the cabby did not hear, or perhaps did not care to. We jogged comfortably along, to my relief, leaving the young man far behind. I avoid reporters on principle, having learned long ago that I am an easy mark for a clever interviewer.

===Chinese===
我忐忑不安地环顾四周。还没看见记者的影子，我庆幸自己没被注意到，付了早餐钱就离开了。在出租车站，我挑了一辆还算过得去的出租马车，把吉尔摩宅邸在东区的地址给了车夫，上了车。

我来得正是时候。马车刚转弯驶出，一个戴草帽的瘦高个年轻人从一小群人中脱身出来，朝我们快步跑来。

"嘿！等一下！"他喊道，小跑着追了上来。

但车夫没有听见，或者也许装作没听见。马车小跑着稳稳向前，让我松了口气，把那个年轻人远远甩在了后面。我一向回避记者，因为我早就知道自己是聪明采访者最容易得手的目标。

===Original===
It was perhaps nine o’clock when I left the station. Our way was along the boulevard which hugged the side of one of the city’s great hills. Far below, to the left, lay the railroad tracks and the seventy times seven looming stacks of the mills. The white mist of the river, the grays and blacks of the smoke blended into a half-revealing haze, dotted here and there with fire. It was unlovely, tremendous. Whistler might have painted it with its pathos, its majesty, but he would have missed what made it infinitely suggestive—the rattle and roar of iron on iron, the rumble of wheels, the throbbing beat, against the ears, of fire and heat and brawn welding prosperity.

===Chinese===
我离开车站时大约是九点。我们的马车沿着一条环山大道行驶，一边紧贴着这座大城市的一座大山的山腰。远处下方，铁轨横陈，四面八方矗立着工厂七七四十九座高耸的烟囱。河水上升腾的白色雾气，与灰黑色的烟尘交织成一片若隐若现的薄霭，其间星星点点地散布着炉火。它丑陋，却又壮观。惠斯勒（Whistler）也许能画出它的悲怆和雄伟，但他会遗漏那令它蕴含无穷意味的东西——铁与铁碰撞的铿锵轰鸣、车轮的隆隆声响、在耳边搏动的节拍，那是烈火、热力与筋肉锻造繁荣的脉搏。

===Original===
Something of this I voiced to the grim old millionaire who was responsible for at least part of it. He was propped up in bed in his East End home, listening to the market reports read by a nurse, and he smiled a little at my enthusiasm.

“I can’t see much beauty in it myself,” he said. “But it’s our badge of prosperity. The full dinner pail here means a nose that looks like a flue. Pittsburg without smoke wouldn’t be Pittsburg, any more than New York without prohibition would be New York. Sit down for a few minutes, Mr. Blakeley. Now, Miss Gardner, Westinghouse Electric.”

The nurse resumed her reading in a monotonous voice. She read literally and without understanding, using initials and abbreviations as they came. But the shrewd old man followed her easily. Once, however, he stopped her.

“D‑o is ditto,” he said gently, “not *do*.”

===Chinese===
我把这些感受跟那位至少要为这片景象负一部分责任的、面容冷峻的老百万富翁说了几句。他在东区的家中卧床不起，听着护士念市场行情报告，对我的这番热情报以淡淡的一笑。

"我自己倒看不出它有什么美，"他说。"但它是我们繁荣的徽章。'饭碗满满的'在这儿的意思就是——鼻子长得像烟囱。没有烟的匹兹堡就不是匹兹堡，就像没有禁酒令的纽约就不是纽约。请坐一会儿，布莱克利先生。现在，加德纳小姐（Miss Gardner），念西屋电气（Westinghouse Electric）。"

护士用单调的声音继续念。她照本宣科，也不理解念的是什么，遇到首字母缩写和缩略词就原样念出。但这精明的老头子跟得毫不费力。不过有一次，他打断了她。

"D‑o 是'ditto'（同上），"他温和地说，不是"*do*"。

===Original===
As the nurse droned along, I found myself looking curiously at a photograph in a silver frame on the bedside table. It was the picture of a girl in white, with her hands clasped loosely before her. Against the dark background her figure stood out slim and young. Perhaps it was the rather grim environment, possibly it was my mood, but although as a general thing photographs of young girls make no appeal to me, this one did. I found my eyes straying back to it. By a little finesse I even made out the name written across the corner, “Alison.”

Mr. Gilmore lay back among his pillows and listened to the nurse’s listless voice. But he was watching me from under his heavy eyebrows, for when the reading was over, and we were alone, he indicated the picture with a gesture.

“I keep it there to remind myself that I am an old man,” he said. “That is my granddaughter, Alison West.”

===Chinese===
护士在那儿絮絮叨叨地念着，我的目光却被床头柜上一个银质相框里的照片吸引住了，自己都没察觉。那是一个穿白裙子的姑娘，双手松松地交握在身前。在深色背景的衬托下，她的身姿纤细而年轻。也许是周围环境过于压抑，也许是我当时的心境使然——通常来说，年轻姑娘的照片对我来说毫无吸引力，可这张不同。我发现自己一次又一次地把目光移向它。靠着一点小技巧，我甚至辨认出了写在角落里的名字："艾莉森（Alison）。"

吉尔摩先生靠在枕头堆里，听着护士有气无力的念读声。但他正从浓密的眉毛下注视着我，因为念完之后，我们单独相处时，他用一个手势指了指那张照片。

"我把它放在那儿，是为了提醒自己已经是个老头子了，"他说。"那是我的孙女，艾莉森·韦斯特（Alison West）。"

===Original===
I expressed the customary polite surprise, at which, finding me responsive, he told me his age with a chuckle of pride. More surprise, this time genuine. From that we went to what he ate for breakfast and did not eat for luncheon, and then to his reserve power, which at sixty-five becomes a matter for thought. And so, in a wide circle, back to where we started, the picture.

“Father was a rascal,” John Gilmore said, picking up the frame. “The happiest day of my life was when I knew he was safely dead in bed and not hanged. If the child had looked like him, I—well, she doesn’t. She’s a Gilmore, every inch. Supposed to look like me.”

“Very noticeably,” I agreed soberly.

===Chinese===
我照例表示了得体的惊讶，他见我反应积极，便带着几分自豪的轻笑告诉了我他的年龄。这次我的惊讶是真的了。从年龄聊到他早餐吃了什么、午餐没吃什么，又聊到六十五岁人的储备体力——这是个值得琢磨的话题。就这样兜了一大圈，我们又回到了起点——那张照片。

"她父亲是个无赖，"约翰·吉尔摩拿起相框说。"我这辈子最开心的一天，就是知道他平平安安死在床上，没有被绞死。要是这孩子长得像他，我——嗯，可她不像。她是个十足的吉尔摩家的人。大家都说她长得像我。"

"非常明显，"我认真地附和道。

===Original===
I had produced the notes by that time, and replacing the picture Mr. Gilmore gathered his spectacles from beside it. He went over the four notes methodically, examining each carefully and putting it down before he picked up the next. Then he leaned back and took off his glasses.

“They’re not so bad,” he said thoughtfully. “Not so bad. But I never saw them before. That’s my unofficial signature. I am inclined to think—he was speaking partly to himself—to think that he has got hold of a letter of mine, probably to Alison. Bronson was a friend of her rapscallion of a father.”

I took Mr. Gilmore’s deposition and put it into my traveling-bag with the forged notes. When I saw them again, almost three weeks later, they were unrecognizable, a mass of charred paper on a copper ashtray. In the interval other and bigger things had happened: the Bronson forgery case had shrunk beside the greater and more imminent mystery of the man in lower ten. And Alison West had come into the story and into my life.

===Chinese===
这时候我已经把票据拿出来了。吉尔摩先生放回照片，从旁边拿起他的眼镜。他按部就班地逐一审阅那四张票据，每张都仔细端详一番，放下后再拿起下一张。然后他靠回去，摘下了眼镜。

"它们还不算太糟，"他若有所思地说。"不算太糟。但我以前从没见过这些票据。这个是我的非正式签名。我倾向于认为——他说话有些像在自言自语——他是弄到了我的一封信，很可能是写给艾莉森的。布朗森是她那个不成器的父亲的朋友。"

我录下了吉尔摩先生的证词，连同那些伪造票据一起放进了我的旅行箱。当我再次见到它们时——将近三周之后——它们已经面目全非了，只是一堆铜烟灰缸里的焦黑纸片。在那段时间里，发生了其他更大的事情：布朗森伪造案在下铺十号男人那桩更重大、更迫在眉睫的谜案面前已黯然失色。而艾莉森·韦斯特，也走进了这个故事，走进了我的生命。

## II / 第二章

### A Torn Telegram / 一封撕碎的电报

===Original===
I lunched alone at the Gilmore house, and went back to the city at once. The sun had lifted the mists, and a fresh summer wind had cleared away the smoke pall. The boulevard was full of cars flying countryward for the Saturday half-holiday, toward golf and tennis, green fields and babbling girls. I gritted my teeth and thought of McKnight at Richmond, visiting the lady with the geographical name. And then, for the first time, I associated John Gilmore’s granddaughter with the “West” that McKnight had irritably flung at me.

===Chinese===
我独自在吉尔摩家吃了午饭，然后立刻返回了城里。阳光驱散了晨雾，一阵清新的夏日微风吹散了烟霾。林荫大道上满是朝着乡间疾驰的汽车，人们趁着周六半日假期去打高尔夫球和网球，前往绿油油的田野和叽叽喳喳的姑娘们中间。我咬牙切齿地想着里奇蒙的麦克奈特（McKnight），正去拜访那位名字带有地理特征的女士。接着，我第一次将约翰·吉尔摩（John Gilmore）的孙女与麦克奈特恼火地甩给我的那个“韦斯特（West）”联系了起来。

===Original===
I still carried my traveling-bag, for McKnight’s vision at the window of the empty house had not been without effect. I did not transfer the notes to my pocket, and, if I had, it would not have altered the situation later. Only the other day McKnight put this very thing up to me.

===Chinese===
我依然提着我的旅行包，因为麦克奈特在空房子窗口看到的景象并非毫无影响。我没有把那些笔记转移到口袋里，即使我这么做了，后来的情况也不会改变。就在前几天，麦克奈特还拿这事质问我。

===Original===
“I warned you,” he reminded me. “I told you there were queer things coming, and to be on your guard. You ought to have taken your revolver.”

===Chinese===
“我警告过你的，”他提醒我。“我告诉过你怪事会来的，让你提防着点。你该带上你的左轮手枪。”

===Original===
“It would have been of exactly as much use as a bucket of snow in Africa,” I retorted. “If I had never closed my eyes, or if I had kept my finger on the trigger of a six-shooter (which is novelesque for revolver), the result would have been the same. And the next time you want a little excitement with every variety of thrill thrown in, I can put you by way of you begin by getting the wrong berth in a Pullman car, and end—”

===Chinese===
“那玩意儿就像在非洲的一桶雪一样毫无用处，”我反驳道。“就算我从不闭眼，或者手指一直扣在六响枪（这是小说里对左轮手枪的称呼）的扳机上，结果也是一样。下次你要是想找点刺激，把各种惊险都经历一遍，我可以给你指条路：你先在普尔曼车厢里订错一个铺位，然后结局——”

===Original===
“Oh, I know how it ends,” he finished shortly. “Don’t you suppose the whole thing’s written on my spinal marrow?”

===Chinese===
“哦，我知道结局怎么样，”他简短地打断道。“你以为这事儿没写在我的脊椎骨髓上吗？”

===Original===
But I am wandering again. That is the difficulty with the unprofessional storyteller: he yaws back and forth and can’t keep in the wind; he drops his characters overboard when he hasn’t any further use for them and drowns them; he forgets the coffeepot and the frying-pan and all the other small essentials, and, if he carries a love affair, he mutters a fervent “Allah be praised” when he lands them, drenched with adventures, at the matrimonial dock at the end of the final chapter.

===Chinese===
但我又跑题了。这就是非专业讲故事的人的毛病：他左右摇摆，把握不住方向；当他觉得角色没用了，就把他们扔下船淹死；他忘了咖啡壶、煎锅和所有其他小必需品；如果他写爱情故事，当男女主角历经冒险终于抵达婚姻码头时，他会念叨一声热烈的“感谢真主安拉”。

===Original===
I put in a thoroughly unsatisfactory afternoon. Time dragged eternally. I dropped in at a summer vaudeville, and bought some ties at a haberdasher’s. I was bored but unexpectant; I had no premonition of what was to come. Nothing unusual had ever happened to me; friends of mine had sometimes sailed the high seas of adventure or skirted the coasts of chance, but all of the shipwrecks had occurred after a woman passenger had been taken on. “Ergo,” I had always said “no women!” I repeated it to myself that evening almost savagely, when I found my thoughts straying back to the picture of John Gilmore’s granddaughter. I even argued as I ate my solitary dinner at a downtown restaurant.

===Chinese===
那个下午过得极其不令人满意。时间像永恒一样难熬。我溜进一家夏日杂耍剧院，又在一家服饰用品店买了几条领带。我很无聊，但并不期待什么；我对即将发生的事毫无预感。我从未遇到过什么不寻常的事；我的朋友们有时会航行在冒险的汪洋大海，或是在机遇的海岸边徘徊，但所有的海难都发生在女乘客上船之后。“因此，”我总是说，“不要女人！”那天晚上，当我的思绪又飘回到约翰·吉尔摩孙女的形象时，我几乎是凶狠地对自己重复着这句话。我甚至在市中心餐馆吃着孤独的晚餐时，还在为此争论。

===Original===
“Haven’t you troubles enough,” I reflected, “without looking for more? Hasn’t Bad News gone lame, with a matinée race booked for next week? Otherwise aren’t you comfortable? Isn’t your house in order? Do you want to sell a pony in order to have the library done over in mission or the drawing-room in gold? Do you want somebody to count the empty cigarette boxes lying around every morning?”

===Chinese===
“你的麻烦还不够多吗，”我反思道，“还要自找麻烦？‘坏消息（Bad News）’不是瘸了吗，下周还有场日间赛马。除此之外，你难道不舒服吗？你的房子不是井井有条吗？你想卖掉一匹小马，好把书房改造成传教士风格，或者把客厅贴上金箔吗？你想每天早上让人数数到处乱扔的空烟盒吗？”

===Original===
Lay it to the long idle afternoon, to the new environment, to anything you like, but I began to think that perhaps I did. I was confoundedly lonely. For the first time in my life its even course began to waver: the needle registered warning marks on the matrimonial seismograph, lines vague enough, but lines.

===Chinese===
把这归咎于漫长无聊的下午，归咎于新的环境，随你归咎于什么都行，但我开始想，也许我确实想要。我他妈的太孤独了。我生命中第一次，那平稳的轨迹开始摇摆：婚姻地震仪的指针划出了警告的刻痕，线条虽然模糊，但终究是线条。

===Original===
My alligator bag lay at my feet, still locked. While I waited for my coffee I leaned back and surveyed the people incuriously. There were the usual couples intent on each other: my new state of mind made me regard them with tolerance. But at the next table, where a man and woman dined together, a different atmosphere prevailed. My attention was first caught by the woman’s face. She had been speaking earnestly across the table, her profile turned to me. I had noticed casually her earnest manner, her somber clothes, and the great mass of odd, bronze-colored hair on her neck. But suddenly she glanced toward me and the utter hopelessness—almost tragedy—of her expression struck me with a shock. She half closed her eyes and drew a long breath, then she turned again to the man across the table.

===Chinese===
我的鳄鱼皮包放在脚边，仍然锁着。等咖啡的时候，我向后靠去，漠不关心地打量着周围的人。有常见的几对情侣，彼此专注着：我新近的心境让我宽容地看待他们。但邻桌，一男一女共进晚餐，气氛却截然不同。最初吸引我注意力的是那个女人的脸。她正隔着桌子热切地说着什么，侧脸对着我。我曾不经意地注意到她认真的神态、深色的衣服，以及她颈后那一大绺奇特的、青铜色的头发。但突然她瞥了我一眼，她表情中那彻底的绝望——几乎是悲剧——让我震惊得心猛地一沉。她半闭着眼睛，长长地吸了一口气，然后又转向了对面的男人。

===Original===
Neither one was eating. He sat low in his chair, his chin on his chest, ugly folds of thick flesh protruding over his collar. He was probably fifty, bald, grotesque, sullen, and yet not without a suggestion of power. But he had been drinking; as I looked, he raised an unsteady hand and summoned a waiter with a wine list.

===Chinese===
两人都没在吃东西。他瘫坐在椅子里，下巴抵着胸口，衣领上方堆着难看的肥厚肉褶。他大概五十岁，秃顶，相貌丑陋，阴沉，但隐约透着一股力量。但他喝过酒了；我看着他抬起一只不稳的手，用酒单叫来一个侍者。

===Original===
The young woman bent across the table and spoke again quickly. She had unconsciously raised her voice. Not beautiful, in her earnestness and stress she rather interested me. I had an idle inclination to advise the waiter to remove the bottled temptation from the table. I wonder what would have happened if I had? Suppose Harrington had not been intoxicated when he entered the Pullman car Ontario that night!

===Chinese===
年轻女人俯身越过桌子，又急切地说了起来。她不自觉地提高了声音。她并不漂亮，但她那专注和焦灼的神情倒是引起了我的兴趣。我一时冲动，想建议侍者把那瓶诱惑从桌上拿开。我想知道如果我那样做了，会发生什么？假如那天晚上哈灵顿（Harrington）进入安大略号（Ontario）普尔曼车厢时没有醉酒呢！

===Original===
For they were about to make a journey, I gathered, and the young woman wished to go alone. I drank three cups of coffee, which accounted for my wakefulness later, and shamelessly watched the tableau before me. The woman’s protest evidently went for nothing: across the table the man grunted monosyllabic replies and grew more and more lowering and sullen. Once, during a brief unexpected pianissimo in the music, her voice came to me sharply:

===Chinese===
据我推测，他们正要开始一段旅程，而年轻女人希望独自前往。我喝了三杯咖啡（这解释了我后来为何清醒），然后厚颜无耻地观看着眼前的场景。女人的抗议显然毫无作用：桌子对面的男人用单音节词咕哝着回应，脸色越来越阴沉、越来越阴郁。有一次，音乐意外地短暂轻柔下来，她的声音尖锐地传到我耳中：

===Original===
“If I could only see him in time!” she was saying. “Oh, it’s terrible!”

===Chinese===
“但愿我能及时见到他！”她说。“哦，太可怕了！”

===Original===
In spite of my interest I would have forgotten the whole incident at once, erased it from my mind as one does the inessentials and clutterings of memory, had I not met them again, later that evening, in the Pennsylvania station. The situation between them had not visibly altered: the same dogged determination showed in the man’s face, but the young woman—daughter or wife? I wondered—had drawn down her veil and I could only suspect what white misery lay beneath.

===Chinese===
尽管我对此很感兴趣，但那天晚上稍后在宾夕法尼亚车站再次遇到他们之前，我本会立刻忘记整件事，就像抹去记忆中那些无关紧要的杂物一样。他们之间的状况似乎没有明显变化：男人脸上仍是那副固执的决绝，但年轻女人——女儿还是妻子？我思忖着——放下了面纱，我只能猜测那白色面纱下藏着何等苍白的痛苦。

===Original===
I bought my berth after waiting in a line of some eight or ten people. When, step by step, I had almost reached the window, a tall woman whom I had not noticed before spoke to me from my elbow. She had a ticket and money in her hand.

===Chinese===
我排队等候了八到十个人，才买到我的铺位。当我一步一步几乎快到窗口时，一个之前没注意到的高个子女人在我身旁开口说话了。她手里拿着一张票和钱。

===Original===
“Will you try to get me a lower when you buy yours?” she asked. “I have traveled for three nights in uppers.”

===Chinese===
“你买票时能帮我弄个下铺吗？”她问。“我已经在上铺坐了三个晚上了。”

===Original===
I consented, of course; beyond that I hardly noticed the woman. I had a vague impression of height and a certain amount of stateliness, but the crowd was pushing behind me, and someone was standing on my foot. I got two lowers easily and, turning with the change and berths, held out the tickets.

===Chinese===
我当然同意了；除此之外，我几乎没注意到这个女人。我模模糊糊觉得她很高，有几分威严，但人群在身后推搡，还有人踩着我的脚。我轻易地买到了两个下铺，然后拿着找零和铺位票转过身，递出车票。

===Original===
“Which will you have?” I asked. “Lower eleven or lower ten?”

===Chinese===
“您要哪个？”我问。“十一号下铺还是十号下铺？”

===Original===
“It makes no difference,” she said. “Thank you very indeed.”

===Chinese===
“哪个都行，”她说。“非常感谢。”

===Original===
At random I gave her lower eleven, and called a porter to help her with her luggage. I followed them leisurely to the train shed, and ten minutes more saw us under way.

===Chinese===
我随手把十一号下铺给了她，叫了一个脚夫来帮她搬行李。我慢悠悠地跟着他们走向车棚，十分钟后我们就出发了。

===Original===
I looked into my car, but it presented the peculiarly unattractive appearance common to sleepers. The berths were made up; the center aisle was a path between walls of dingy, breeze-repelling curtains, while the two seats at each end of the car were piled high with suitcases and umbrellas. The perspiring porter was trying to be six places at once: somebody has said that Pullman porters are black so they won’t show the dirt, but they certainly show the heat.

===Chinese===
我朝我的车厢里看了一眼，但它呈现出卧铺车厢那种特有的、毫无吸引力的样子。铺位已经铺好；中央过道是一条夹在灰暗、挡风的帘子墙壁之间的小径，车厢两端的两个座位上则高高堆满了手提箱和雨伞。那个汗流浃背的脚夫恨不得分身六处：有人说普尔曼的脚夫是黑色的，这样就看不出脏，但他们确实显得很热。

===Original===
Nine-fifteen was an outrageous hour to go to bed, especially since I sleep little or not at all on the train, so I made my way to the smoker and passed the time until nearly eleven with cigarettes and a magazine. The car was very close. It was a warm night, and before turning in I stood a short time in the vestibule. The train had been stopping at frequent intervals, and, finding the brakeman there, I asked the trouble.

===Chinese===
九点一刻就寝实在荒唐，尤其我在火车上几乎不睡或根本不睡，所以我去了吸烟车厢，抽着烟、翻着杂志消磨到将近十一点。车厢里很闷热。那是个温暖的夜晚，就寝前我在车厢连接处站了一会儿。火车频繁地停站，我在那儿遇到了制动员，便问发生了什么故障。

===Original===
It seemed that there was a hotbox on the next car, and that not only were we late, but we were delaying the second section, just behind. I was beginning to feel pleasantly drowsy, and the air was growing cooler as we got into the mountains. I said good night to the brakeman and went back to my berth. To my surprise, lower ten was already occupied—a suitcase projected from beneath, a pair of shoes stood on the floor, and from behind the curtains came the heavy, unmistakable breathing of deep sleep. I hunted out the porter and together we investigated.

===Chinese===
似乎是下一节车厢的轴箱过热了，我们不仅晚点了，还耽误了紧随其后的第二趟列车。我开始感到惬意的困倦，随着我们进入山区，空气也变得凉爽起来。我向制动员道了晚安，回到了我的铺位。令我惊讶的是，十号下铺已经被人占了——一只行李箱从下面探出来，一双鞋子摆在地板上，帘子后面传来深沉的、不容置疑的鼾睡声。我找出脚夫，我们一起查看了情况。

===Original===
“Are you asleep, sir?” asked the porter, leaning over deferentially. No answer forthcoming, he opened the curtains and looked in. Yes, the intruder was asleep—very much asleep—and an overwhelming odor of whisky proclaimed that he would probably remain asleep until morning. I was irritated. The car was full, and I was not disposed to take an upper in order to allow this drunken interloper to sleep comfortably in my berth.

===Chinese===
“先生，您睡着了吗？”脚夫毕恭毕敬地俯身问道。没有回答，他拉开帘子往里看。是的，那个不速之客睡着了——睡得很沉——一股浓烈的威士忌气味表明他大概会一直睡到天亮。我很恼火。车厢满员，我可不想为了这个醉醺醺的闯入者舒舒服服地睡在我的铺位，而去睡上铺。

===Original===
“You’ll have to get out of this,” I said, shaking him angrily. But he merely grunted and turned over. As he did so, I saw his features for the first time. It was the quarrelsome man of the restaurant.

===Chinese===
“你得从这儿出去，”我愤怒地摇着他说。但他只是咕哝了一声，翻了个身。就在他翻身时，我第一次看清了他的面貌。正是餐馆里那个好斗的男人。

===Original===
I was less disposed than ever to relinquish my claim, but the porter, after a little quiet investigation, offered a solution of the difficulty. “There’s no one in lower nine,” he suggested, pulling open the curtains just across. “It’s likely nine’s his berth, and he’s made a mistake, owing to his condition. You’d better take nine, sir.”

===Chinese===
我比以往任何时候都不想放弃我的权利，但脚夫经过一番小小的私下查看，提出了一个解决难题的办法。“九号下铺没人，”他建议道，拉开了对面的帘子。“很可能九号才是他的铺位，他弄错了，因为他那状况。您最好睡九号吧，先生。”

===Original===
I did, with a firm resolution that if nine’s rightful owner turned up later I should be just as unwakable as the man opposite. I undressed leisurely, making sure of the safety of the forged notes, and placing my grip as before between myself and the window.

===Chinese===
我照做了，心里打定主意，如果九号铺位的合法主人晚点出现，我就和对面那家伙一样装睡不醒。我不慌不忙地脱了衣服，确认了假钞的安全，然后像之前一样把手提包放在我和窗户之间。

===Original===
Being a man of systematic habits, I arranged my clothes carefully, putting my shoes out for the porter to polish, and stowing my collar and scarf in the little hammock swung for that purpose.

===Chinese===
作为一个有条理的人，我把衣服仔细摆放好，把鞋子拿出来让脚夫擦亮，把衣领和围巾收进专门悬挂的小吊床里。

===Original===
At last, with my pillows so arranged that I could see out comfortably, and with the unhygienic-looking blanket turned back—I have always a distrust of those much-used affairs—I prepared to wait gradually for sleep.

===Chinese===
最后，我把枕头摆得舒舒服服，以便能看清外面，把那条看起来不太卫生的毯子掀开——我向来不信任这种被反复使用的东西——准备慢慢等待睡意降临。

===Original===
But sleep did not visit me. The train came to frequent, grating stops, and I surmised the hot box again. I am not a nervous man, but there was something chilling in the thought of the second section pounding along behind us. Once, as I was dozing, our locomotive whistled a shrill warning—“You keep back where you belong,” it screamed to my drowsy ears, and from somewhere behind came a chastened “All-right-I-will.”

===Chinese===
但睡意没有眷顾我。火车频繁地、刺耳地停下，我猜想又是轴箱过热了。我并非神经质的人，但想到第二趟列车就在我们后面轰隆隆地跟着，就有点不寒而栗。有一次，我正打盹，我们的机车发出一声尖利的警告——“你在后面老实待着，”它对着我昏昏欲睡的耳朵尖叫，后面某处传来一声温顺的“好吧-我会的”。

===Original===
I grew more and more wide-awake. At Cresson I got up on my elbow and blinked out at the station lights. Some passengers boarded the train there and I heard a woman’s low tones, a southern voice, rich and full. Then quiet again. Every nerve was tense: time passed, perhaps ten minutes, possibly half an hour. Then, without the slightest warning, as the train rounded a curve, a heavy body was thrown into my berth. The incident, trivial as it seemed, was startling in its suddenness, for although my ears were painfully strained and awake, I had heard no step outside. The next instant the curtain hung limp again; still without a sound, my disturber had slipped away into the gloom and darkness. In a frenzy of wakefulness, I sat up, drew on a pair of slippers and fumbled for my bathrobe.

===Chinese===
我变得越来越清醒。在克雷森（Cresson），我用手肘撑起身子，眯着眼看站台的灯光。一些乘客在那里上了车，我听到一个女人低沉的声音，是南方口音，浑厚而饱满。然后又安静了。每根神经都紧绷着：时间过去了，也许十分钟，也许半小时。接着，在毫无预警的情况下，当火车转过一个弯时，一个沉重的身体被扔进了我的铺位。这件事看似微不足道，但其突然性却令人吃惊，因为尽管我的耳朵痛苦地警醒着，却没听到外面有脚步声。下一刻，帘子又无力地垂下了；我的打扰者仍悄无声息地溜进了昏暗的黑暗中。在极度的清醒中，我坐起身，套上拖鞋，摸索着我的浴袍。

===Original===
From a berth across, probably lower ten, came that particular aggravating snore which begins lightly, delicately, faintly soprano, goes down the scale a note with every breath, and, after keeping the listener tense with expectation, ends with an explosion that tears the very air. I was more and more irritable: I sat on the edge of the berth and hoped the snorer would choke to death. He had considerable vitality, however; he withstood one shock after another and survived to start again with new vigor. In desperation I found some cigarettes and one match, piled my blankets over my grip, and drawing the curtains together as though the berth were still occupied, I made my way to the vestibule of the car.

===Chinese===
从对面的铺位，大概是十号下铺，传来那种特别恼人的鼾声：开始时轻柔、细微、带着微弱的假声，随着每一次呼吸，音阶下降一个音符，在让听者紧张地期待之后，以一声撕裂空气的爆炸声结束。我越来越烦躁：我坐在铺位边上，希望打鼾的家伙窒息而死。然而，他生命力相当顽强；他经受住了一次又一次的冲击，活了下来，又以新的活力重新开始。绝望中，我找到几支烟和一根火柴，把毯子堆在我的手提包上，拉上帘子，装作铺位还有人，然后朝车厢连接处走去。

===Original===
I was not clad for dress parade. Is it because the male is so restricted to gloom in his everyday attire that he blossoms into gaudy colors in his pajamas and dressing-gowns? It would take a Turkish to feel at home before an audience in my red and yellow bathrobe, a Christmas remembrance from Mrs. Klopton, with slippers to match.

===Chinese===
我的穿着可不适合列队检阅。难道是因为男人在日常服装中如此局限于阴郁的色调，才会在睡衣和晨袍上绽放出艳丽的色彩吗？穿着克洛普顿夫人（Mrs. Klopton）送的这件红黄相间的圣诞礼物——一件浴袍和配套的拖鞋，只有土耳其人才能在观众面前感到自在。

===Original===
So, naturally, when I saw a feminine figure on the platform, my first instinct was to dodge. The woman, however, was quicker than I; she gave me a startled glance, wheeled and disappeared, with a flash of two bronze-colored braids, into the next car.

===Chinese===
所以，很自然地，当我在站台上看到一个女性身影时，我的第一反应就是躲开。然而，那个女人比我更快；她惊恐地瞥了我一眼，转身就消失在下一节车厢里，留下两根青铜色辫子的一闪。

===Original===
Cigarette box in one hand, match in the other, I leaned against the uncertain frame of the door and gazed after her vanished figure. The mountain air flapped my bathrobe around my bare ankles, my one match burned to the end and went out, and still I stared. For I had seen on her expressive face a haunting look that was horror, nothing less. Heaven knows, I am not psychological. Emotions have to be written large before I can read them. But a woman in trouble always appeals to me, and this woman was more than that. She was in deadly fear.

===Chinese===
我一手拿着烟盒，一手拿着火柴，靠在不牢靠的门框上，凝视着她消失的身影。山风把我的浴袍吹得在光脚踝周围扑打，我唯一的那根火柴烧到了尽头，熄灭了，我依然凝视着。因为我看到她那富有表情的脸上萦绕着一种表情，那正是恐惧，毫无疑问。天知道，我并不善于察言观色。情感必须表现得非常明显，我才能读懂。但陷入困境的女人总能打动我，而这个女人不止如此。她处于极度的恐惧之中。

===Original===
If I had not been afraid of being ridiculous, I would have followed her. But I fancied that the apparition of a man in a red and yellow bathrobe, with an unkempt thatch of hair, walking up to her and assuring her that he would protect her would probably put her into hysterics. I had done that once before, when burglars had tried to break into the house, and had startled the parlor maid into bed for a week. So I tried to assure myself that I had imagined the lady’s distress—or caused it, perhaps—and to dismiss her from my mind. Perhaps she was merely anxious about the unpleasant gentleman of the restaurant. I thought smugly that I could have told her all about him: that he was sleeping the sleep of the justified and the intoxicated in a berth that ought, by all that was fair and right, to have been mine, and that if I were tied to a man who snored like that I should have him anaesthetized and his soft palate put where it would never again flap like a loose sail in the wind.

===Chinese===
如果我不是害怕显得可笑，我本会跟上去。但我想象，一个穿着红黄浴袍、头发蓬乱的男人出现在她面前，向她保证会保护她，很可能会让她歇斯底里起来。我以前做过一次这种事，当时窃贼试图闯入房子，结果把客厅女仆吓得在床上躺了一个星期。所以我试图说服自己，我想象了那位女士的痛苦——或者是我造成的——然后把她从我的思绪中赶走。也许她只是在为餐馆里那位令人不快的绅士担心。我自鸣得意地想，我本可以把她的一切都告诉他：他正像个正义又醉醺醺的人一样，在一个本该属于我的铺位上酣睡，如果我跟一个打鼾像他那样的男人绑在一起，我会给他做麻醉，把他的软腭放到一个再也不会像松开的船帆在风中拍打的地方。

===Original===
We passed Harrisburg as I stood there. It was starlight, and the great crests of the Alleghanies had given way to low hills. At intervals we passed smudges of gray white, no doubt in daytime comfortable farms, which McKnight says is a good way of putting it, the farms being a lot more comfortable than the people on them.

===Chinese===
我站在那里时，我们驶过了哈里斯堡。星光点点，阿勒格尼山脉的雄伟峰顶让位给了低矮的山丘。我们不时经过一些灰白色的斑点，毫无疑问是白天舒适的农场，麦克奈特说这是一种很好的说法，因为农场比住在里面的人舒适多了。

===Original===
I was growing drowsy: the woman with the bronze hair and the horrified face was fading in retrospect. It was colder, too, and I turned with a shiver to go in. As I did so a bit of paper fluttered into the air and settled on my sleeve, like a butterfly on a gorgeous red and yellow blossom. I picked it up curiously and glanced at it. It was part of a telegram that had been torn into bits.

===Chinese===
我渐渐困倦了：那个青铜色头发、惊恐面容的女人正在回忆中淡去。天也冷了，我打了个寒颤，转身要进去。就在这时，一小片纸片飘到空中，落在我的袖子上，就像一只蝴蝶停在一朵艳丽的红黄花朵上。我好奇地捡起来，瞥了一眼。这是一封被撕成碎片的电报的一部分。

===Original===
There were only parts of four words on the scrap, but it left me puzzled and thoughtful. It read, “—ower ten, car seve—.”

===Chinese===
纸片上只有四个词的一部分，但它让我困惑和深思。上面写着：“——号下铺，七节车厢——。”

===Original===
“Lower ten, car seven,” was my berth—the one I had bought and found preempted.

===Chinese===
“十号下铺，七节车厢，”是我的铺位——那个我买了却发现被占用的铺位。

## III / 三、过道对面

===Original===
No solution offering itself, I went back to my berth. The snorer across had apparently strangled, or turned over, and so after a time I dropped asleep, to be awakened by the morning sunlight across my face.

I felt for my watch, yawning prodigiously. I reached under the pillow and failed to find it, but something scratched the back of my hand. I sat up irritably and nursed the wound, which was bleeding a little. Still drowsy, I felt more cautiously for what I supposed had been my scarf pin, but there was nothing there. Wide awake now, I reached for my traveling-bag, on the chance that I had put my watch in there. I had drawn the satchel to me and had my hand on the lock before I realized that it was not my own!

===Chinese===
没有头绪，我只好回到自己的铺位。对面那个打呼噜的家伙显然已经憋过去了，或者翻了个身，所以过了一阵子我又睡着了，直到早晨的阳光照在我脸上才醒来。

我摸索着找表，大大地打了个哈欠。我伸手到枕头下面，没找到表，但有什么东西刮破了我手背。我恼怒地坐起来，照料伤口，那儿微微渗着血。睡意未消，我更小心地摸索，以为是别针掉了，但什么也没摸到。这下完全清醒了，我伸手去拿旅行袋，说不定我把表放在了里面。我把袋子拖到跟前，手已经放在锁扣上，才意识到——这不是我的袋子！

===Original===
Mine was of alligator hide. I had killed the beast in Florida, after the expenditure of enough money to have bought a house and enough energy to have built one. The bag I held in my hand was a black one, sealskin, I think. The staggering thought of what the loss of my bag meant to me put my finger on the bell and kept it there until the porter came.

“Did you ring, sir?” he asked, poking his head through the curtains obsequiously. McKnight objects that nobody can poke his head through a curtain and be obsequious. But Pullman porters can and do.

“No,” I snapped. “It rang itself. What in thunder do you mean by exchanging my valise for this one? You’ll have to find it if you waken the entire car to do it. There are important papers in that grip.”

===Chinese===
我的旅行袋是鳄鱼皮的。我在佛罗里达亲手宰了那条鳄鱼，花了够买一栋房子的钱，费了够盖一栋房子的劲儿。而我手里的这只，是黑色的，我猜是海豹皮的。想到丢了旅行袋意味着什么，我简直头都晕了，手指按在铃铛上不放，直到列车员来。

“您按铃了吗，先生？”他问，毕恭毕敬地把头伸进帘子里。麦克奈特（McKnight）总说，没人能把头伸进帘子时还显得毕恭毕敬。但普尔曼列车员就能，而且总是这样。

“不是我按的，”我厉声说，“铃铛自己响的。你他妈到底为什么把我的皮箱换成了这只？你就是把整节车厢都吵醒也得给我找回来。那只箱子里有重要文件。”

===Original===
“Porter,” called a feminine voice from an upper berth nearby. “Porter, am I to dangle here all day?”

“Let her dangle,” I said savagely. “You find that bag of mine.”

The porter frowned. Then he looked at me with injured dignity. “I brought in your overcoat, sir. You carried your own valise.”

The fellow was right! In an excess of caution I had refused to relinquish my alligator bag, and had turned over my other traps to the porter. It was clear enough then. I was simply a victim of the usual sleeping-car robbery. I was in a lather of perspiration by that time: the lady down the car was still dangling and talking about it: still nearer a feminine voice was giving quick orders in French, presumably to a maid. The porter was on his knees, looking under the berth.

===Chinese===
“列车员，”附近上铺传来一个女人的声音，“列车员，我要在这儿晃荡一整天吗？”

“让她晃去，”我恶狠狠地说，“先给我找到箱子。”

列车员皱了皱眉。然后他带着受委屈的尊严看着我。“我把您的大衣拿进来了，先生。您自己的皮箱是自己提的。”

这家伙说得对！我过度小心，不肯松手我的鳄鱼皮袋，把其余行李都交给了列车员。那么事情很清楚了。我不过是那种常见的卧铺车厢失窃案的受害者。这时候我已经汗流浃背了：车厢那头那位女士还在晃荡着、念叨着；更近处一个女声正在用法语快速下令，大概是吩咐女仆。列车员跪在地下，查看铺位底下。

===Original===
“Not there, sir,” he said, dusting his knees. He was visibly more cheerful, having been absolved of responsibility. “Reckon it was taken while you was wanderin’ around the car last night.”

“I’ll give you fifty dollars if you find it,” I said. “A hundred. Reach up my shoes and I’ll—”

I stopped abruptly. My eyes were fixed in stupefied amazement on a coat that hung from a hook at the foot of my berth. From the coat they traveled, dazed, to the soft-bosomed shirt beside it, and from there to the collar and cravat in the net hammock across the windows.

“A hundred!” the porter repeated, showing his teeth. But I caught him by the arm and pointed to the foot of the berth.

“What—what color’s that coat?” I asked unsteadily.

“Gray, sir.” His tone was one of gentle reproof.

“And—the trousers?”

He reached over and held up one creased leg. “Gray, too,” he grinned.

===Chinese===
“不在那儿，先生，”他说着拍掉膝盖上的灰。他明显高兴起来，因为责任已经洗清了。“我猜是您昨晚在车厢里晃荡的时候被拿走了。”

“你找到箱子我给你五十块，”我说，“一百块。去把我的鞋拿下来，我——”

我突然住了口。我的眼睛死死盯着挂在我铺位末端钩子上的一件外套，惊得目瞪口呆。目光从外套移到旁边那件软胸衬衫，又移到网兜里那个衣领和领结。

“一百块！”列车员龇着牙重复道。但我抓住他的胳膊，指向铺位那头。

“那件——那件外套是什么颜色的？”我声音发颤地问。

“灰色的，先生。”他的语气温和地带着责备。

“那——裤子呢？”

他探过身去，拎起一条裤腿。“也是灰的，”他咧嘴笑道。

===Original===
“Gray!” I could not believe even his corroboration of my own eyes. “But my clothes were blue!” The porter was amused: he dived under the curtains and brought up a pair of shoes. “Your shoes, sir,” he said with a flourish. “Reckon you’ve been dreaming, sir.”

Now, there are two things I always avoid in my dress—possibly an idiosyncrasy of my bachelor existence. These tabooed articles are red neckties and tan shoes. And not only were the shoes the porter lifted from the floor of a gorgeous shade of yellow, but the scarf which was run through the turned over collar was a gaudy red. It took a full minute for the real import of things to penetrate my dazed intelligence. Then I gave a vindictive kick at the offending ensemble.

“They’re not mine, any of them,” I snarled. “They are some other fellow’s. I’ll sit here until I take root before I put them on.”

“They’re nice lookin’ clothes,” the porter put in, eying the red tie with appreciation. “Ain’t everybody would have left you anything.”

“Call the conductor,” I said shortly. Then a possible explanation occurred to me. “Oh, porter—what’s the number of this berth?”

“Seven, sir. If you cain’t wear those shoes—”

“Seven!” In my relief I almost shouted it. “Why, then, it’s simple enough. I’m in the wrong berth, that’s all. My berth is nine. Only—where the deuce is the man who belongs here?”

“Likely in nine, sir.” The darky was enjoying himself. “You and the other gentleman just got mixed in the night. That’s all, sir.” It was clear that he thought I had been drinking.

===Chinese===
“灰色！”我简直不敢相信自己的眼睛。“可我的衣服是蓝色的！”列车员觉得好笑：他钻进帘子底下，拎出一双鞋。“您的鞋，先生，”他得意洋洋地说，“我猜您是做梦了吧，先生。”

我穿衣有两个禁忌——也许是我单身生活养成的怪癖。这两样忌讳的东西是红领带和棕黄色皮鞋。而现在，列车员从地上拎起来的这双鞋，颜色是刺眼的亮黄，而且从翻折领口里穿出来的领巾居然是俗气的大红色。足足过了一分钟，事情的真正含义才穿透我晕乎乎的脑袋。然后我恶狠狠地踢了一脚那堆扎眼的行头。

“这些都不是我的，”我咆哮道，“是别人的。我就是坐在这儿长出根来，也不穿这玩意儿。”

“挺好看的衣服嘛，”列车员插嘴说，赞赏地盯着红领带，“别人可不一定给你留东西。”

“去叫列车长，”我简短地说。接着一个可能的解释闪过我的脑子。“哦，列车员——这是几号铺？”

“七号，先生。要是您穿不了那双鞋——”

“七号！”我如释重负，差点喊出来。“那就对了，其实很简单。我跑错铺位了，仅此而已。我的铺位是九号。只是——见鬼，这铺位原来的主人呢？”

“大概在九号，先生。”黑人列车员开心极了，“您和另一位先生夜里弄混了。就这点事，先生。”显然他觉得我是喝多了。

===Original===
I drew a long breath. Of course, that was the explanation. This was number seven’s berth, that was his soft hat, this his umbrella, his coat, his bag. My rage turned to irritation at myself.

The porter went to the next berth and I could hear his softly insinuating voice. “Time to get up, sir. Are you awake? Time to get up.”

There was no response from number nine. I guessed that he had opened the curtains and was looking in. Then he came back.

“Number nine’s empty,” he said.

“Empty! Do you mean my clothes aren’t there?” I demanded. “My valise? Why don’t you answer me?”

“You doan’ give me time,” he retorted. “There ain’t nothin’ there. But it’s been slept in.”

The disappointment was the greater for my few moments of hope. I sat up in a white fury and put on the clothes that had been left me. Then, still raging, I sat on the edge of the berth and put on the obnoxious tan shoes. The porter, called to his duties, made little excursions back to me, to offer assistance and to chuckle at my discomfiture. He stood by, outwardly decorous, but with little irritating grins of amusement around his mouth, when I finally emerged with the red tie in my hand.

“Bet the owner of those clothes didn’t become them any more than you do,” he said, as he plied the ubiquitous whisk broom.

“When I get the owner of these clothes,” I retorted grimly, “he will need a shroud. Where’s the conductor?”

===Chinese===
我长出一口气。当然，这就是解释了。这是七号铺的东西：那顶软帽是他的，那把伞是他的，大衣、旅行袋都是他的。我的怒火转向了对自己恼火。

列车员去了隔壁铺位，我听见他那温软诱导的声音：“该起床了，先生。您醒了吗？该起床了。”

九号铺没有应声。我猜他大概是掀开帘子往里看了看。然后他回来了。

“九号铺是空的，”他说。

“空的！你是说我的衣服不在那儿？”我追问，“我的皮箱呢？你为什么不回答我？”

“您没给我时间嘛，”他反驳道，“那儿什么都没有。不过有人睡过。”

刚刚升起的一点希望破灭，失望更甚。我怒气冲冲地坐起来，穿上那堆留给我衣服。然后，怒火未消，我坐在铺位边上，穿上那双讨厌的棕黄皮鞋。列车员被叫去干活，又抽空跑回来看我，一边帮忙一边暗暗笑话我的狼狈相。我终于出来了，手里攥着那条红领带，他站在旁边，表面上恭恭敬敬，嘴角却挂着一丝令人恼火的窃笑。

“我打赌这些衣服的主人穿上也不比你好看，”他说着，挥舞着那把无处不在的小扫帚。

“等我找到这些衣服的主人，”我恶狠狠地回道，“他就得用裹尸布了。列车长在哪儿？”

===Original===
The conductor was coming, he assured me; also that there was no bag answering the description of mine on the car. I slammed my way to the dressing-room, washed, choked my fifteen and a half neck into a fifteen collar, and was back again in less than five minutes. The car, as well as its occupants, was gradually taking on a daylight appearance. I hobbled in, for one of the shoes was abominably tight, and found myself facing a young woman in blue with an unforgettable face. (“Three women already.” McKnight says: “That’s going some, even if you don’t count the Gilmore nurse.”) She stood, half-turned toward me, one hand idly drooping, the other steadying her as she gazed out at the flying landscape. I had an instant impression that I had met her somewhere, under different circumstances, more cheerful ones, I thought, for the girl’s dejection now was evident. Beside her, sitting down, a small dark woman, considerably older, was talking in a rapid undertone. The girl nodded indifferent now and then. I fancied, although I was not sure, that my appearance brought a startled look into the young woman’s face. I sat down and, hands thrust deep into the other man’s pockets, stared ruefully at the other man’s shoes.

The stage was set. In a moment the curtain was going up on the first act of the play. And for a while we would all say our little speeches and sing our little songs, and I, the villain, would hold center stage while the gallery hissed.

The porter was standing beside lower ten. He had reached in and was knocking valiantly. But his efforts met with no response. He winked at me over his shoulder; then he unfastened the curtains and bent forward. Behind him, I saw him stiffen, heard his muttered exclamation, saw the bluish pallor that spread over his face and neck. As he retreated a step the interior of lower ten lay open to the day.

The man in it was on his back, the early morning sun striking full on his upturned face. But the light did not disturb him. A small stain of red dyed the front of his night clothes and trailed across the sheet; his half-open eyes were fixed, without seeing, on the shining wood above.

I grasped the porter’s shaking shoulders and stared down to where the train imparted to the body a grisly suggestion of motion. “Good Lord,” I gasped. “The man’s been murdered!”

===Chinese===
列车长马上就来，他向我保证；车厢里也没有符合我描述的皮箱。我砰砰地冲进盥洗室，洗了脸，把十五寸半的脖子硬塞进十五寸的衣领，不到五分钟就回来了。车厢以及车上的乘客都渐渐呈现出白天的样子。我一瘸一拐地走进去，因为有一只鞋紧得要命，结果迎面撞见一个穿蓝衣的年轻姑娘，长着一张让人过目难忘的脸。（“已经是第三个女人了。”麦克奈特说，“就算不算那个吉尔摩（Gilmore）护士，也算够数了。）她侧身半对着我站着，一只手闲闲地垂着，另一只手扶着自己，凝望着窗外飞驰的景色。我立刻觉得，我曾在某个不同的场合见过她——一个更快乐的场合，我想，因为这姑娘此刻的沮丧是显而易见的。坐在她身旁的，是一个个子矮小、肤色较深的妇人，年纪大得多，正压低声音急促地说着什么。姑娘偶尔心不在焉地点点头。我隐约觉得——虽然不确定——我的出现让那年轻姑娘脸上闪过一丝惊讶。我坐下来，双手深深插进另一个人的衣兜，愁眉苦脸地盯着另一个人的鞋子。

舞台已搭好。再一会儿，第一幕戏就要开场了。大家各就各位，各说各的台词，各唱各的歌，而我这个反派将占据舞台中央，任由观众席上嘘声一片。

列车员正站在十号下铺旁边。他伸手进去使劲敲了敲。但无人应答。他回头朝我使了个眼色；然后他解开帘子，俯身向前。就在我眼前，他僵住了，我听见他低声惊叫，看见灰青色的血色从他脸上、脖子上迅速蔓延开来。他后退了一步，十号下铺的内部便暴露在晨光中。

里面那个男人仰面躺着，早晨的阳光直射在他那张朝上的脸上。但这光亮并没有打扰他。他睡衣的前襟染了一小片红色，一路洇到了床单上；他那半睁的眼睛呆呆地盯着上方发亮的木板。

我抓住列车员颤抖的肩膀，俯视着，火车的行进让那具尸体产生了一种可怕的动感。“天哪，”我倒吸一口冷气，“这人被谋杀了！”

## IV / 第四章

Numbers Seven and Nine / 七号与九号

===Original===
Afterwards, when I tried to recall our discovery of the body in lower ten, I found that my most vivid impression was not that made by the revelation of the opened curtain. I had an instantaneous picture of a slender blue-gowned girl who seemed to sense my words rather than hear them, of two small hands that clutched desperately at the seat beside them. The girl in the aisle stood, bent toward us, perplexity and alarm fighting in her face.

===Chinese===
事后回想我们发现下铺十号那具尸体的情形，我发觉印象最深的并非窗帘拉开后映入眼帘的那一幕，而是另有一幅画面清晰地浮现在眼前：一个身着蓝色衣裙的纤细女孩，仿佛不是用耳朵听到了我的话，而是凭直觉感知到了什么；她身旁的两只小手死死抓住座椅边缘。过道里的那个女孩站了起来，身子朝我们探过来，困惑与惊恐在她脸上交战。

===Original===
With twitching hands the porter attempted to draw the curtains together. Then in a paralysis of shock, he collapsed on the edge of my berth and sat there swaying. In my excitement I shook him.

===Chinese===
列车员（porter）双手哆嗦着试图把窗帘拉拢，随后便因极度震惊而僵住了，一屁股跌坐在我的铺位边缘，身子摇摇晃晃。我一时激动，抓住他猛摇了几下。

===Original===
"For Heaven's sake, keep your nerve, man," I said brusquely. "You'll have every woman in the car in hysterics. And if you do, you'll wish you could change places with the man in there." He rolled his eyes.

===Chinese===
"看在老天爷的份上，振作点，伙计，"我粗声粗气地说，"你要把全车厢的女人都吓得歇斯底里不可。真要是闹起来，你会恨不得跟里头那位换个位子。"他翻了翻白眼。

===Original===
A man near, who had been reading last night's paper, dropped it quickly and tiptoed toward us. He peered between the partly open curtains, closed them quietly and went back, ostentatiously solemn, to his seat. The very crackle with which he opened his paper added to the bursting curiosity of the car. For the passengers knew that something was amiss: I was conscious of a sudden tension.

===Chinese===
附近一个原本在看昨晚报纸的男人迅速放下报纸，踮着脚尖凑过来。他从半开的窗帘缝隙间窥了一眼，轻手轻脚地合上帘子，然后故作严肃地踱回自己的座位。他翻开报纸时那刻意弄出的沙沙响声，反而更加撩拨了全车厢快要爆炸的好奇心。乘客们已经觉出出了事——我能感觉到车厢里骤然绷紧的气氛。

===Original===
With the curtains closed the porter was more himself; he wiped his lips with a handkerchief and stood erect.

===Chinese===
窗帘重新合拢后，列车员才缓过神来；他用手帕擦了擦嘴唇，直起身子。

===Original===
"It's my last trip in *this* car," he remarked heavily. "There's something wrong with that berth. Last trip the woman in it took an overdose of some sleeping stuff, and we found her, jes' like that, dead! And it ain't more'n three months now since there was twins born in that very spot. No, sir, it ain't natural."

===Chinese===
"这可是我最后一次跑*这节*车厢了，"他沉甸甸地说道，"那个铺位邪门。上一趟有个女乘客在那铺上安眠药吃多了，我们发现她的时候，就这么——死了！还有不到三个月前，就在那个铺位上，一对双胞胎呱呱坠地。不，先生，这地方不干净。"

===Original===
At that moment a thin man with prominent eyes and a spare grayish goatee creaked up the aisle and paused beside me.

===Chinese===
就在这时，一个瘦削的男人——眼睛突出，下巴上蓄着一撮稀疏的灰白山羊胡——踩着吱嘎作响的地板走过过道，在我身旁停了下来。

===Original===
"Porter sick?" he inquired, taking in with a professional eye the porter's horror-struck face, my own excitement and the slightly gaping curtains of lower ten. He reached for the darky's pulse and pulled out an old-fashioned gold watch.

===Chinese===
"列车员病了？"他问道，一双职业的眼睛扫过列车员惊恐万状的脸、我自己的激动神色，以及下铺十号微微裂开的帘子。他伸手去搭那黑人车夫的手腕，同时掏出一块老式金表。

===Original===
"Hm! Only fifty! What's the matter? Had a shock?" he asked shrewdly.

===Chinese===
"嗯！才五十下！怎么了？受了惊吓？"他精明地问道。

===Original===
"Yes," I answered for the porter. "We've both had one. If you are a doctor, I wish you would look at the man in the berth across, lower ten. I'm afraid it's too late, but I'm not experienced in such matters."

===Chinese===
"是的，"我替列车员回答，"我们俩都受了惊。如果您是医生的话，劳驾您去看看对面下铺十号那位先生。恐怕已经来不及了，不过这种事我不在行。"

===Original===
Together we opened the curtains, and the doctor, bending down, gave a comprehensive glance that took in the rolling head, the relaxed jaw, the ugly stain on the sheet. The examination needed only a moment. Death was written in the clear white of the nostrils, nostrils, the colorless lips, the smoothing away of the sinister lines of the night before. With its new dignity the face was not unhandsome: the gray hair was still plentiful, the features strong and well cut.

===Chinese===
我们一同拉开窗帘。医生弯下腰，飞快地扫了一整眼——歪到一边的头颅，松弛的下巴，床单上那块刺目的污渍。检查只需片刻。死神的印记写得一清二楚：鼻孔惨白，嘴唇毫无血色，昨夜那些阴鸷的纹路已然舒展开去。赋予这张脸一种新的庄严之后，倒也不算难看：灰白的头发依然浓密，五官轮廓分明，棱角有力。

===Original===
The doctor straightened himself and turned to me. "Dead for some time," he said, running a professional finger over the stains. "These are dry and darkened, you see, and rigor mortis is well established. A friend of yours?"

===Chinese===
医生直起身来，转向我。"已经死了一阵子了，"他一边说，一边用职业性的手指拂过那些污渍，"您看，这些血迹已经干涸发暗，尸僵也已经完全出现了。是您的朋友？"

===Original===
"I don't know him at all," I replied. "Never saw him but once before."

===Chinese===
"我根本不认识他，"我回答，"以前只见过一面。"

===Original===
"Then you don't know if he is traveling alone?"

===Chinese===
"那您不知道他是不是一个人出行？"

===Original===
"No, he was not— that is, I don't know anything about him," I corrected myself. It was my first blunder: the doctor glanced up at me quickly and then turned his attention again to the body. Like a flash there had come to me the vision of the woman with the bronze hair and the tragic face, whom I had surprised in the vestibule between the cars, somewhere in the small hours of the morning. I had acted on my first impulse— the masculine one of shielding a woman.

===Chinese===
"不，他不是——也就是说，我对他一无所知，"我赶紧改口。这是我犯的第一个低级错误：医生飞快地瞥了我一眼，然后重新将注意力转回到尸体上。一瞬间，那个铜色头发、面容悲戚的女人又浮现在我脑海中——凌晨时分，我在两节车厢之间的过道里撞见了她。我当时完全是凭第一反应行事——出于男人保护女人的本能。

===Original===
The doctor had unfastened the coat of the striped pajamas and exposed the dead man's chest. On the left side was a small punctured wound of insignificant size.

===Chinese===
医生已经解开了条纹睡衣的扣子，露出了死者的胸膛。左侧有一个很小的刺伤，创口不大。

===Original===
"Very neatly done," the doctor said with appreciation. "Couldn't have done it better myself. Right through the intercostal space: no time even to grunt."

===Chinese===
"手法非常干净利落，"医生赞许地说，"我自己都做不到更好。正中肋间隙——连哼一声的时间都没给他。"

===Original===
"Isn't the heart around there somewhere?" I asked. The medical man turned toward me and smiled austerely.

===Chinese===
"心脏不就在那附近吗？"我问。医生转向我，露出一个严肃的微笑。

===Original===
"That's where it belongs, just under that puncture, when it isn't gadding around in a man's throat or his boots."

===Chinese===
"心脏本来就在那儿，就在那一刺的正下方——除非它跑到人的嗓子眼儿或者靴子里去了。"

===Original===
I had a new respect for the doctor, for anyone indeed who could crack even a feeble joke under such circumstances, or who could run an impersonal finger over that wound and those stains. Odd how a healthy, normal man holds the medical profession in half contemptuous regard until he gets sick, or an emergency like this arises, and then turns meekly to the man who knows the ins and outs of his mortal tenement, takes his pills or his patronage, ties to him like a rudderless ship in a gale.

===Chinese===
我对这位医生油然生出一种新的敬意——事实上，对任何能在这种场合还能开个冷笑话的人，或能用超然的手指拂过伤口和污渍的人，我都心怀敬意。说来也怪，一个健康正常的人总是对医学界抱有半是轻蔑的态度，直到自己生了病，或者碰上这样的紧急状况，才会乖乖地转向那个洞悉他这具凡俗肉身一切奥秘的人，乖乖吃药，乖乖听话，就像暴风雨中失去舵的船紧紧依附着灯塔一样。

===Original===
"Suicide, is it, doctor?" I asked.

===Chinese===
"是自杀吗，医生？"我问。

===Original===
He stood erect, after drawing the bed-clothing over the face, and, taking off his glasses, he wiped them slowly.

===Chinese===
他把被单拉上来盖住死者的脸，然后直起身子，摘下眼镜，慢慢地擦拭着。

===Original===
"No, it is not suicide," he announced decisively. "It is murder."

===Chinese===
"不，不是自杀，"他断然宣布，"这是谋杀。"

===Original===
Of course, I had expected that, but the word itself brought a shiver. I was just a bit dizzy. Curious faces through the car were turned toward us, and I could hear the porter behind me breathing audibly. A stout woman in negligee came down the aisle and querulously confronted the porter. She wore a pink dressing-jacket and carried portions of her clothing.

===Chinese===
当然，我心里早有预感，但那个词本身还是让我打了个寒颤。我有点头晕。好奇的面孔从车厢各处朝我们转过来，我能听到身后列车员沉重的呼吸声。一个穿着家居便服的胖女人沿过道走来，尖声尖气地找列车员理论。她穿着一件粉色梳妆外套，怀里还抱着几件衣物。

===Original===
"Porter," she began, in the voice of the lady who had "dangled," "is there a rule of this company that will allow a woman to occupy the dressing-room for one hour and curl her hair with an alcohol lamp while respectable people haven't a place where they can hook their—"

===Chinese===
"列车员，"她用那个曾经"晃来晃去"的女乘客特有的腔调开了口，"贵公司是不是有条规定，准许一个女人占着化妆室一个小时用酒精灯烫头发，而体面的人连个挂钩的地方都没有——"

===Original===
She stopped suddenly and stared into lower ten. Her shining pink cheeks grew pasty, her jaw fell. I remember trying to think of something to say, and of saying nothing at all. Then— she had buried her eyes in the nondescript garments that hung from her arm and tottered back the way she had come. Slowly a little knot of men gathered around us, silent for the most part. The doctor was making a search of the berth when the conductor elbowed his way through, followed by the inquisitive man, who had evidently summoned him. I had lost sight, for a time, of the girl in blue.

===Chinese===
她突然住了嘴，朝下铺十号里面张望。原本红润发亮的双颊霎时变得煞白，下巴也耷拉下来。我记得自己想说点什么，结果什么也没说出来。接着——她把脸埋进臂弯里那堆不成样子的衣物中，踉踉跄跄地原路退回去了。慢慢地，一小群男人围拢过来，多数人默不作声。医生正在搜查铺位时，列车长（conductor）用胳膊肘挤开人群走了进来，后面跟着那个好奇心旺盛的家伙——显然是他去叫来的列车长。有一阵子，我没再看到那个蓝衣女孩。

===Original===
"Do it himself?" the conductor queried, after a businesslike glance at the body.

===Chinese===
"自己干的？"列车长公事公办地扫了一眼尸体后问道。

===Original===
"No, he didn't," the doctor asserted. "There's no weapon here, and the window is closed. He couldn't have thrown it out, and he didn't swallow it. What on earth are you looking for, man?"

===Chinese===
"不是，"医生断然否定，"凶器不在这里，窗户也是关着的。他不可能把凶器扔出去，也不可能吞下去。你到底在找什么，伙计？"

===Original===
Someone was on the floor at our feet, face down, head peering under the berth. Now he got up without apology, revealing the man who had summoned the conductor. He was dusty, alert, cheerful, and he dragged up with him the dead man's suitcase. The sight of it brought back to me at once my own predicament.

===Chinese===
有人趴在我们脚边的地板上，脸朝下，脑袋探到铺位底下张望。此刻他毫不歉疚地站起身来——原来就是那个叫来列车长的家伙。他满身灰尘，目光机警，表情爽朗，手里拽着死者的那只皮箱。那只皮箱一出现，立刻让我想起了自己的窘境。

===Original===
"I don't know whether there's any connection or not, conductor," I said, "but I am a victim, too, in less degree; I've been robbed of everything I possess, except a red and yellow bathrobe. I happened to be wearing the bathrobe, which was probably the reason the thief overlooked it."

===Chinese===
"我不知道这两件事之间有没有关联，列车长，"我说，"但我也算受害者——程度轻一点罢了；我所有的东西都被偷了，只剩一件红黄相间的浴袍。碰巧我当时正穿着它，这大概是窃贼没有拿走它的原因。"

===Original===
There was a fresh murmur in the crowd. Some body laughed nervously. The conductor was irritated.

===Chinese===
人群中又响起一阵低语。有人紧张地笑了一声。列车长不耐烦了。

===Original===
"I can't bother with that now," he snarled. "The railroad company is responsible for transportation, not for clothes, jewelry and morals. If people want to be stabbed and robbed in the company's cars, it's their affair. Why didn't you sleep in your clothes? I do."

===Chinese===
"我现在没工夫管那个，"他咆哮道，"铁路公司只管运送旅客，不管衣服、首饰和道德。要是有人愿意在公司的车厢里挨刀被抢，那是他们自己的事。你为什么不穿着衣服睡觉？我就穿。"

===Original===
I took an angry step forward. Then somebody touched my arm, and I unclenched my fist. I could understand the conductor's position, and beside, in the law, I had been guilty myself of contributory negligence.

===Chinese===
我怒气冲冲地向前踏了一步。这时有人碰了碰我的胳膊，我松开了拳头。我能理解列车长的立场，况且从法律上讲，我自己也犯了"共同过失"（contributory negligence）的毛病。

===Original===
"I'm not trying to make you responsible," I protested as amiably as I could, "and I believe the clothes the thief left are as good as my own. They are certainly newer. But my valise contained valuable papers and it is to your interest as well as mine to find the man who stole it."

===Chinese===
"我不是想让您承担责任，"我尽量和颜悦色地辩解道，"而且我相信窃贼留下来的衣服跟我的一样好——甚至更新。但我那只旅行袋里装着重要文件，找到偷它的人对您和我都有好处。"

===Original===
"Why, of course," the conductor said shrewdly. "Find the man who skipped out with this gentleman's clothes, and you've probably got the murderer."

===Chinese===
"那当然，"精明的列车长说，"找到那个卷走这位先生衣服的家伙，你八成就抓到了凶手。"

===Original===
"I went to bed in lower nine," I said, my mind full again of my lost papers, "and I wakened in number seven. I was up in the night prowling around, as I was unable to sleep, and I must have gone back to the wrong berth. Anyhow, until the porter wakened me this morning I knew nothing of my mistake. In the interval the thief— murderer, too, perhaps— must have come back, discovered my error, and taken advantage of it to further his escape."

===Chinese===
"我是在下铺九号睡下的，"我说，脑子里满是丢失文件的事，"醒来却发现自己在七号铺。夜里我睡不着，到处溜达，肯定是摸回了错误的铺位。总之，直到今早列车员叫醒我之前，我对自己的错误一无所知。在这段时间里，那个窃贼——也许同时也是凶手——一定回来过，发现了我的错误，并加以利用，以便更好地逃之夭夭。"

===Original===
The inquisitive man looked at me from between narrowed eyelids, ferret-like.

===Chinese===
那个好奇的家伙眯起眼睛打量着我，活像一只鼬鼠。

===Original===
"Did anyone on the train suspect you of having valuable papers?" he inquired. The crowd was listening intently.

===Chinese===
"火车上有人怀疑你带着贵重文件吗？"他追问。人群正竖着耳朵听。

===Original===
"No one," I answered promptly and positively. The doctor was investigating the murdered man's effects. The pockets of his trousers contained the usual miscellany of keys and small change, while in his hip pocket was found a small pearl-handled revolver of the type women usually keep around. A gold watch with a Masonic charm had slid down between the mattress and the window, while a showy diamond stud was still fastened in the bosom of his shirt. Taken as a whole, the personal belongings were those of a man of some means, but without any particular degree of breeding. The doctor heaped them together.

===Chinese===
"没有，"我迅速而肯定地回答。医生正在检查死者的随身物品。裤兜里装着钥匙、零钱之类的杂物，后裤袋里找到一把小型珍珠柄左轮手枪——那种女人通常会放在身边的款式。一块带有共济会徽章的金表滑落到了床垫和车窗之间，衬衫胸口上还别着一枚花哨的钻石饰钉。总体来看，这些私人物品的主人属于有些家底、但谈不上多有教养的人。医生把东西堆成了一堆。

===Original===
"Either robbery was not the motive," he reflected, "or the thief overlooked these things in his hurry."

===Chinese===
"要么作案动机不是抢劫，"他沉思道，"要么窃贼在匆忙之中忽略了这些东西。"

===Original===
The latter hypothesis seemed the more tenable, when, after a thorough search, we found no pocketbook and less than a dollar in small change.

===Chinese===
后一种假设似乎更站得住脚，因为经过彻底搜查，我们没有找到钱包，零钱也不到一美元。

===Original===
The suitcase gave no clue. It contained one empty leather-covered flask and a pint bottle, also empty, a change of linen and some collars with the laundry mark, S. H. In the leather tag on the handle was a card with the name Simon Harrington, Pittsburg. The conductor sat down on my unmade berth, across, and made an entry of the name and address. Then, on an old envelope, he wrote a few words and gave it to the porter, who disappeared.

===Chinese===
皮箱里没有找到任何线索。里面有一个空的皮套酒壶和一个同样空了的半品脱瓶子、一套换洗的亚麻内衣，以及几枚领扣，上面有洗衣店的标记：S. H.（西蒙·哈林顿的姓名缩写）。皮箱提手上的皮质标签里插着一张名片：西蒙·哈林顿（Simon Harrington），匹兹堡（Pittsburg）。列车长在我对面那张没有整理过的铺位上坐下来，记下了姓名和地址。然后他在一个旧信封上写了几个字，交给列车员，列车员便转身不见了。

===Original===
"I guess that's all I can do," he said. "I've had enough trouble this trip to last for a year. They don't need a conductor on these trains any more; what they ought to have is a sheriff and a posse."

===Chinese===
"我想我能做的就这么多了，"他说，"这一趟的麻烦事够我受用一年的了。这些列车不再需要列车长了；该配的是治安官和一队巡警。"

===Original===
The porter from the next car came in and whispered to him. The conductor rose unhappily.

===Chinese===
隔壁车厢的列车员走进来，在他耳边低语了几句。列车长不快地站起身来。

===Original===
"Next car's caught the disease," he grumbled. "Doctor, a woman back there has got mumps or bubonic plague, or something. Will you come back?"

===Chinese===
"隔壁车厢也染上这毛病了，"他嘟囔道，"医生，后面有个女人得了腮腺炎还是黑死病什么的。您能过去看看吗？"

===Original===
The strange porter stood aside.

===Chinese===
那位陌生的列车员闪到一旁。

===Original===
"Lady about the middle of the car," he said, "in black, sir, with queer-looking hair— sort of copper color, I think, sir."

===Chinese===
"车厢中段的一位女士，"他说，"穿黑衣，先生，头发颜色很怪——好像是铜色的，我想，先生。"

## V / 下铺十号隔壁车厢的女人

===Original===
With the departure of the conductor and the doctor, the group around lower ten broke up, to re-form in smaller knots through the car. The porter remained on guard. With something of relief I sank into a seat. I wanted to think, to try to remember the details of the previous night. But my inquisitive acquaintance had other intentions. He came up and sat down beside me. Like the conductor, he had taken notes of the dead man’s belongings, his name, address, clothing and the general circumstances of the crime. Now with his little notebook open before him, he prepared to enjoy the minor sensation of the robbery.

“And now for the second victim,” he began cheerfully. “What is your name and address, please?” I eyed him with suspicion.

“I have lost everything but my name and address,” I parried. “What do you want them for? Publication?”

===Chinese===
随着乘务员和医生的离去，围在下铺十号车厢的人群散开了，三三两两重新散布在车厢各处。搬运工仍然守在车厢门口。我松了口气，一屁股坐到座位上。我想好好想想，努力回忆起昨晚那些零散的细节。可我那位好奇心旺盛的熟人却另有打算。他走过来，在我身边坐下。和乘务员一样，他已经把死者的随身物品、姓名地址、衣着打扮以及这起案子的大致情况都记录下来了。此刻，他摊开小笔记本，准备好好品味一番这场盗窃案带来的小小轰动。

"那么，现在来问问第二位受害者，"他兴冲冲地开口道，"请问您的姓名和地址？"我戒备地看着他。

"我除了自己的姓名和地址，什么都没丢，"我搪塞道，"你要这些干嘛？登报吗？"

===Original===
"Oh, no; dear, no!" he said, shocked at my misapprehension. "Merely for my own enlightenment. I like to gather data of this kind and draw my own conclusions. Most interesting and engrossing. Once or twice I have forestalled the results of police investigation﻿—but entirely for my own amusement."

I nodded tolerantly. Most of us have hobbies; I knew a man once who carried his handkerchief up his sleeve and had a mania for old colored prints cut out of *Godey’s Lady’s Book*.

“I use that inductive method originated by Poe and followed since with such success by Conan Doyle. Have you ever read Gaboriau? Ah, you have missed a treat, indeed. And now, to get down to business, what is the name of our escaped thief and probable murderer?”

===Chinese===
"哦，不，当然不是！"他说，对我的误解感到惊讶，"只是我自己想弄明白罢了。我喜欢搜集这类资料，然后推断出自己的结论。有意思极了，叫人着迷。有那么一两次，我甚至抢在警方调查之前就得出结果了——不过纯粹是为了消遣。"

我宽容地点了点头。我们大多数人多少都有些癖好；我认识一个人，他把手帕塞在袖子里，还痴迷于从《戈迪女士杂志》上剪下老式的彩色版画。

"我用的是爱伦·坡开创、后来柯南道尔成功发扬光大的那种归纳推理法。您读过加博里欧吗？啊，您可真是错过了好东西。好了，言归正传，咱们这位逃跑的小偷、八成也是凶手，他叫什么名字？"

===Original===
"How on earth do I know?" I demanded impatiently. "He didn't write it in blood anywhere, did he?"

The little man looked hurt and disappointed.

"Do you mean to say," he asked, "that the pockets of those clothes are entirely empty?" The pockets! In the excitement I had forgotten entirely the sealskin grip which the porter now sat at my feet, and I had not investigated the pockets at all. With the inquisitive man’s pencil taking note of everything that I found, I emptied them on the opposite seat.

Upper left-hand waistcoat, two lead pencils and a fountain pen; lower right waistcoat, matchbox and a small stamp book; right-hand pocket coat, pair of gray suede gloves, new, size seven and a half; left-hand pocket, gunmetal cigarette case studded with pearls, half-full of Egyptian cigarettes. The trousers pockets contained a gold penknife, a small amount of money in bills and change, and a handkerchief with the initial *S* on it.

===Chinese===
"我怎么会知道？"我不耐烦地反问，"他总不会用血写在哪儿吧？"

小个子男人一脸受伤和失望的样子。

"您的意思是说，"他问道，"那些衣服的口袋全都空了？"口袋！兴奋之下，我完全忘了那个海豹皮手提箱——搬运工此刻正坐在我脚边的那只——我根本没检查过那些口袋。好奇的家伙拿着铅笔，把发现的每样东西都记下来，我把口袋里的东西一股脑倒在对面的座位上。

上装左胸口袋：两支铅笔和一支钢笔；右胸口袋：火柴盒和一本小邮票册；上装右边口袋：一副灰麂皮手套，崭新的，七码半；左边口袋：一个枪金属色香烟盒，镶嵌着珍珠，里面装了半盒埃及香烟。裤子口袋里有一把金质小折刀，一些钞票和零钱，还有一块绣着字母"S"的手帕。

===Original===
Further search through the coat discovered a card-case with cards bearing the name Henry Pinckney Sullivan, and a leather flask with gold mountings, filled with what seemed to be very fair whisky, and monogrammed H. P. S.

"His name evidently is Henry Pinckney Sullivan," said the cheerful follower of Poe, as he wrote it down. "Address as yet unknown. Blond, probably. Have you noticed that it is almost always the blond men who affect a very light gray, with a touch of red in the scarf? Fact, I assure you. I kept a record once of the summer attire of men, and ninety percent, followed my rule. Dark men like you affect navy blue, or brown."

In spite of myself I was amused at the man’s shrewdness.

===Chinese===
再仔细搜了搜上装，发现了一个名片盒，里面的名片上印着"亨利·平克尼·沙利文（Henry Pinckney Sullivan）"的名字，还有一个镶金边的皮质酒壶，里面装着看起来品质相当不错的威士忌，壶身上印着"H. P. S."的字母组合。

"他显然是叫亨利·平克尼·沙利文，"爱伦·坡的忠实追随者一边写一边说道，"地址还不知道。大概是金发。您有没有注意到，穿浅灰色衣服的几乎都是金发男人，而且领带里总带点红色？这是事实，我敢担保。我曾经记录过男士的夏季着装，百分之九十都符合我这个规律。像您这样肤色深的人，喜欢海军蓝或者棕色。"

我忍不住被这个男人的敏锐逗乐了。

===Original===
"Yes; the suit he took was dark﻿—a blue," I said. He rubbed his hands and smiled at me delightedly. "Then you wore black shoes, not tan," he said, with a glance at the aggressive yellow ones I wore.

"Right again," I acknowledged. "Black low shoes and black embroidered hose. If you keep on you’ll have a motive for the crime, and the murderer’s present place of hiding. And if you come back to the smoker with me, I’ll give you an opportunity to judge if he knew good whisky from bad."

I put the articles from the pockets back again and got up. "I wonder if there is a diner on?" I said. "I need something sustaining after all this."

===Chinese===
"是的；他拿走的那套衣服是深色的——蓝色，"我说。他搓了搓手，高兴地对我笑了笑。"那您穿的肯定是黑色皮鞋，不是棕色的，"他一边说，一边瞥了一眼我脚上那双扎眼的黄色皮鞋。

"又说对了，"我承认道，"黑色低帮皮鞋，黑色绣花袜子。照你这么推理下去，恐怕连犯罪动机和凶手的藏身之处都能挖出来。要是你跟我去吸烟车厢，我可以给你个机会判断一下，他懂不懂品威士忌。"

我把口袋里的东西放回去，站起身来。"不知道餐车开了没有？"我说，"折腾了这么一遭，我得吃点东西撑一撑。"

===Original===
I was conscious then of someone at my elbow. I turned to see the young woman whose face was so vaguely familiar. In the very act of speaking she drew back suddenly and colored.

"Oh﻿—I beg your pardon," she said hurriedly, "I﻿—thought you were﻿—someone else." She was looking in a puzzled fashion at my coat. I felt all the cringing guilt of a man who has accidentally picked up the wrong umbrella: my borrowed collar sat tight on my neck.

"I’m sorry," I said idiotically. "I’m sorry, but﻿—I’m not." I have learned since that she has bright brown hair, with a loose wave in it that drops over her ears, and dark blue eyes with black lashes and﻿—but what does it matter? One enjoys a picture as a whole: not as the sum of its parts.

===Chinese===
这时我才意识到身旁有人。我转过身，看见那位面容依稀熟悉的女人。她正要开口说话，却突然往后一缩，脸红了。

"哦——抱歉，"她急忙说，"我——以为您是——别人。"她困惑地看着我的上衣。我顿时像个拿错别人雨伞的人一样，浑身不自在：那条借来的领带紧紧勒着我的脖子。

"对不起，"我傻乎乎地说，"我很抱歉，但是——我很高兴。"后来我才知道，她有一头明亮的棕色头发，松松地打着波浪，垂过耳边，还有一双深蓝色的眼睛，长着乌黑的睫毛——不过这有什么关系呢？一幅画要整体地欣赏，不能把每个部件拆开来看。

===Original===
She saw the flask then, and her errand came back to her. "One of the ladies at the end of car has fainted," she explained. "I thought perhaps a stimulant﻿—"

I picked up the flask at once and followed my guide down the aisle. Two or three women were working over the woman who had fainted. They had opened her collar and taken out her hairpins, whatever good that might do. The stout woman was vigorously rubbing her wrists, with the idea, no doubt, of working up her pulse! The unconscious woman was the one for whom I had secured lower eleven at the station.

I poured a little liquor in a bungling masculine fashion between her lips as she leaned back, with closed eyes. She choked, coughed, and rallied somewhat.

===Chinese===
她看见了那只酒壶，这才想起自己来干什么。"车厢那头有位女士晕倒了，"她解释道，"我想也许来点烈酒——"

我立刻拎起酒壶，跟着我的向导沿过道走去。两三个女人正在围着那位晕倒的女人忙活。她们解开她的衣领，拔掉了她的发夹——不管有什么用。那个胖女人正使劲揉着她的手腕，无疑以为这样能唤醒她的脉搏！晕倒的女人正是我在车站为她买到下铺十一号的那位。

她向后靠着，闭着眼睛，我笨手笨脚地往她嘴里灌了点酒。她呛了一下，咳嗽了几声，慢慢缓了过来。

===Original===
"Poor thing," said the stout lady. "As she lies back that way I could almost think it was my mother; she used to faint so much."

"It would make anybody faint," chimed in another. "Murder and robbery in one night and on one car. I’m thankful I always wear my rings in a bag around my neck﻿—even if they do get under me and keep me awake."

The girl in blue was looking at us with wide, startled eyes. I saw her pale a little, saw the quick, apprehensive glance which she threw at her traveling companion, the small woman I had noticed before. There was an exchange﻿—almost a clash﻿—of glances. The small woman frowned. That was all. I turned my attention again to my patient.

===Chinese===
"可怜的人，"胖太太说，"她这样仰面躺着，简直让我想起我母亲；她以前也老是晕倒。"

"换谁都得晕，"另一个女人插嘴道，"一夜之间，同一节车厢里又是凶杀又是抢劫。幸好我一直把戒指装在挂在脖子上的袋子里——虽然它们硌得我睡不好觉。"

穿蓝衣服的姑娘睁大了受惊的眼睛望着我们。我看到她脸色苍白了些，看到她飞快地、忧心忡忡地瞥了一眼她的旅伴——就是我之前注意到的那个小个子女人。两人对视了一眼——几乎是对峙。小个子女人皱了皱眉。仅此而已。我把注意力重新转向我的病人。

===Original===
She had revived somewhat, and now she asked to have the window opened. The train had stopped again and the car was oppressively hot. People around were looking at their watches and grumbling over the delay. The doctor bustled in with a remark about its being his busy day. The amateur detective and the porter together mounted guard over lower ten. Outside the heat rose in shimmering waves from the tracks: the very wood of the car was hot to touch. A Camberwell Beauty darted through the open door and made its way, in erratic plunges, great wings waving, down the sunny aisle. All around lay the peace of harvested fields, the quiet of the country.

===Chinese===
她缓过来一些，要求把窗户打开。火车又停了，车厢里闷热难当。周围的人纷纷看手表，抱怨着晚点。医生匆匆赶进来，说了一句今天忙得很之类的话。业余侦探和搬运工一起守在下铺十号车厢门口。窗外，热浪从铁轨上蒸腾而起，波光粼粼；车厢的木板摸上去都烫手。一只康贝尔美人蝶从敞开的车门冲了进来，挥舞着宽大的翅膀，歪歪扭扭地在洒满阳光的过道里飞舞。四周是一派丰收田野的安宁，乡村的静谧。

## VI / 蓝衣女子

===Original===
The Girl in Blue

I was growing more and more irritable. The thought of what the loss of the notes meant was fast crowding the murder to the back of my mind. The forced inaction was intolerable.

The porter had reported no bag answering the description of mine on the train, but I was disposed to make my own investigation. I made a tour of the cars, scrutinizing every variety of hand luggage, ranging from luxurious English bags with gold mountings to the wicker nondescripts of the day coach at the rear. I was not alone in my quest, for the girl in blue was just ahead of me. Car by car she preceded me through the train, unconscious that I was behind her, looking at each passenger as she passed. I fancied the proceeding was distasteful, but that she had determined on a course and was carrying it through. We reached the end of the train almost together—empty-handed, both of us.

===Chinese===
蓝衣女子

我越来越烦躁。那些文件丢失意味着什么——这个念头正飞快地把谋杀案挤到脑后。被迫无所作为，简直让人无法忍受。

搬运工说车上没有发现与我那只提包相符的物品，但我打算亲自调查一番。我把每节车厢都巡视了一遍，仔细查看各种各样的手提行李——从镶金扣的英国高级皮箱，到尾部硬座车厢里那些不伦不类的柳条包。我并非独自在搜寻，因为那个蓝衣女子就走在我前面。她一节车厢一节车厢地穿过整列火车，完全没意识到我在身后，每经过一个乘客都要端详一番。我觉得她其实很不情愿这么做，但显然已经下定决心，便一路坚持到底。我们几乎同时走到火车尽头——两个人都两手空空。

===Original===
The girl went out to the platform. When she saw me she moved aside, and I stepped out beside her. Behind us the track curved sharply; the early sunshine threw the train, in long black shadow, over the hot earth. Forward somewhere they were hammering. The girl said nothing, but her profile was strained and anxious.

"I—if you have lost anything," I began, "I wish you would let me try to help. Not that my own success is anything to boast of."

She hardly glanced at me. It was not flattering. "I have not been robbed, if that is what you mean," she replied quietly. "I am—perplexed. That is all."

===Chinese===
