蓝衣女子走上了站台。看到我，她往旁边让了让，我便站到了她身旁。身后的铁轨急剧弯折，初升的阳光把长长的黑色车影投在滚烫的地面上。前方某处传来叮叮当当的锤击声。那姑娘一言不发，但她的侧影紧绷而焦虑。

"我——如果您丢了什么东西，"我开口道，"希望您能让我帮个忙。当然，我自己都毫无建树，实在没什么可吹嘘的。"

她几乎没瞥我一眼，这可不太给面子。"如果您指的是被抢了，那倒没有，"她平静地答道。"我只是——有些困惑。仅此而已。"

===Original===
There was nothing to say to that. I lifted my hat—the other fellow's hat—and turned to go back to my car. Two or three members of the train crew, including the conductor, were standing in the shadow talking. And at that moment, from a farmhouse near came the swift clang of the breakfast bell, calling in the hands from barn and pasture. I turned back to the girl.

"We may be here for an hour," I said, "and there is no buffet car on. If I remember my youth, that bell means ham and eggs and country butter and coffee. If you care to run the risk—"

===Chinese===
这话我接不上来。我举了举帽子——别人的帽子——转身准备回自己的车厢。列车员、搬运工和另外两三名车组人员正站在阴影里交谈。就在这时，附近农舍传来急促的早餐铃声，召唤着谷仓和牧场里的人们回来吃饭。我转过身看着那姑娘。

"我们可能要在这儿待上一个小时，"我说，"而且车上没有餐车。要是我没记错年轻时候的事，那铃声意味着火腿煎蛋、乡下黄油和咖啡。如果您愿意冒这个险——"

===Original===
"I am not hungry," she said, "but perhaps a cup of coffee—dear me, I believe I *am* hungry," she finished. "Only—" She glanced back of her.

"I can bring your companion," I suggested, without enthusiasm. But the young woman shook her head.

"She is not hungry," she objected, "and she is very—well, I know she wouldn't come. Do you suppose we could make it if we run?"

"I haven't any idea," I said cheerfully. "Any old train would be better than this one, if it does leave us behind."

===Chinese===
"我不饿，"她说，"不过来杯咖啡倒也——天哪，我想我*确实*饿了，"她改口道。"只是——"她回头望了一眼。

"我去把您那位同伴也叫上？"我提议，虽然热情不高。但那年轻女子摇了摇头。

"她不饿，"她推辞道，"而且她非常——嗯，我知道她不会来的。您觉得我们跑过去来得及吗？"

"我完全不知道，"我乐观地说，"就算真被落下，随便哪趟车都比眼前这趟强。"

===Original===
"Yes. Any train would be better than this one," she repeated gravely. I found myself watching her changing expression. I had spoken two dozen words to her and already I felt that I knew the lights and shades in her voice—I, who had always known how a woman rode to hounds, and who never could have told the color of her hair.

I stepped down on the ties and turned to assist her, and together we walked back to where the conductor and the porter from our car were in close conversation. Instinctively my hand went to my cigarette pocket and came out empty. She saw the gesture.

"If you want to smoke, you may," she said. "I have a big cousin who smokes all the time. He says I am 'kippered.'"

===Chinese===
"是啊，随便哪趟车都比这趟好。"她认真地重复了一遍。我不由自主地观察着她表情的变化。我跟她说的话不过二十来个字，却已经觉得对她声音里的抑扬顿挫了如指掌——我这个人向来只晓得一位女士骑马猎狐是什么样子，却连她的头发是什么颜色都说不上来。

我踩上枕木，转身伸手扶她。我们一起走回去，列车员和我们车厢的搬运工正聊得起劲。我不由自主地把手伸向烟袋，却摸了个空。她看见了我的动作。

"想抽就抽吧，"她说，"我有个大块头表哥，整天烟不离手。他说我已经被烟熏透了。"

===Original===
I drew out the gunmetal cigarette case and opened it. But this most commonplace action had an extraordinary result: the girl beside me stopped dead still and stood staring at it with fascinated eyes.

"Is—where did you get that?" she demanded, with a catch in her voice; her gaze still fixed on the cigarette case.

"Then you haven't heard the rest of the tragedy?" I asked, holding out the case. "It's frightfully bad luck for me, but it makes a good story. You see—"

===Chinese===
我掏出那只炮铜烟盒，打开来。然而这个再寻常不过的举动却带来了意想不到的结果：身旁的姑娘猛然停住脚步，目不转睛地盯着烟盒，眼神里满是惊异。

"那是——您从哪里得到的？"她追问，声音微微发颤，目光依然死死锁在烟盒上。

"这么说您还不知道后来的那场悲剧？"我问道，把烟盒递过去，"这对我可是倒了大霉，不过倒是个好故事。您看——"

===Original===
At that moment the conductor and porter ceased their colloquy. The conductor came directly toward me, tugging as he came at his bristling gray mustache.

"I would like to talk to you in the car," he said to me, with a curious glance at the young lady.

"Can't it wait?" I objected. "We are on our way to a cup of coffee and a slice of bacon. Be merciful, as you are powerful."

"I'm afraid the breakfast will have to wait," he replied. "I won't keep you long." There was a note of authority in his voice which I resented; but, after all, the circumstances were unusual.

"We'll have to defer that cup of coffee for a while," I said to the girl; "but don't despair; there's breakfast somewhere."

===Chinese===
这时，列车员和搬运工停止了交谈。列车员径直朝我走来，一边走一边拽着他那灰白的粗硬胡须。

"我想跟您在车厢里谈谈。"他对我说，目光好奇地瞥了一位年轻女子一眼。

"不能等等吗？"我抗议道，"我们正要去喝杯咖啡、吃片培根呢。既然位高权重，就请大发慈悲吧。"

"恐怕早餐得等等了，"他答道，"不会耽误您太长时间的。"他语气里带着一种不容置疑的权威感，我心生不满；不过话说回来，眼前的情况确实非同寻常。

"那杯咖啡只好先放放了，"我对姑娘说，"不过别绝望——总有个地方能吃上早餐的。"

===Original===
As we entered the car, she stood aside, but I felt rather than saw that she followed us. I was surprised to see a half dozen men gathered around the berth in which I had wakened, number seven. It had not yet been made up.

As we passed along the aisle, I was conscious of a new expression on the faces of the passengers. The tall woman who had fainted was searching my face with narrowed eyes, while the stout woman of the kindly heart avoided my gaze, and pretended to look out the window.

===Chinese===
我们走进车厢时，她往旁边一站，但我感觉——与其说看见——她跟了上来。我惊讶地发现有六七个人围在我醒来的那张铺位旁——七号铺，还没有收拾过。

走过过道时，我察觉到乘客们脸上有了新的表情。那个晕倒过的高个子女人正眯着眼打量我的脸，而那个心地善良的胖女人却避开了我的目光，装作在看窗外。

===Original===
As we pushed our way through the group, I fancied that it closed around me ominously. The conductor said nothing, but led the way without ceremony to the side of the berth.

"What's the matter?" I inquired. I was puzzled, but not apprehensive. "Have you some of my things? I'd be thankful even for my shoes; these are confoundedly tight."

Nobody spoke, and I fell silent, too. For one of the pillows had been turned over, and the under side of the white case was streaked with brownish stains. I think it was a perceptible time before I realized that the stains were blood, and that the faces around were filled with suspicion and distrust.

===Chinese===
我们挤过人群时，我感觉他们不怀好意地围拢过来。列车员一言不发，径直把我带到铺位旁。

"怎么回事？"我问。心里犯嘀咕，但还不算紧张。"找到我的东西了？就算只找回我的鞋我也谢天谢地——这双实在挤得难受。"

没人说话，我也沉默了。因为其中一只枕头被翻了过来，白色枕套的背面斑斑点点，尽是褐色污渍。我记得过了好一会儿才反应过来那是血迹，而周围的人全都一脸猜忌和不信任。

===Original===
"Why, it—that looks like blood," I said vacuously. There was an incessant pounding in my ears, and the conductor's voice came from far off.

"It is blood," he asserted grimly.

I looked around with a dizzy attempt at nonchalance. "Even if it is," I remonstrated, "surely you don't suppose for a moment that I know anything about it!"

The amateur detective elbowed his way in. He had a scrap of transparent paper in his hand, and a pencil.

"I would like permission to trace the stains," he began eagerly. "Also"—to me—"if you will kindly jab your finger with a pin—needle—anything—"

===Chinese===
"哎呀，这——这看起来像血迹。"我茫然地说。耳边嗡嗡作响，列车员的声音仿佛从很远的地方传来。

"就是血，"他冷冷地说。

我晕晕乎乎地环顾四周，强装镇定。"就算真是血，"我抗辩道，"你们总不至于认为我跟这事有什么关系吧！"

那个业余侦探挤了进来，手里拿着一小张透明纸和一支铅笔。

"请允许我描下这些血迹，"他急切地说道。又转向我——"还有，请您行行好，用别针——针——随便什么扎一下手指——"

===Original===
"If you don't keep out of this," the conductor said savagely, "I will do some jabbing myself. As for you, sir—" he turned to me. I was absolutely innocent, but I knew that I presented a typical picture of guilt; I was covered with cold sweat, and the pounding in my ears kept up dizzily. "As for you, sir—"

The irrepressible amateur detective made a quick pounce at the pillow and pushed back the cover. Before our incredulous eyes he drew out a narrow steel dirk which had been buried to the small cross that served as a head.

There was a chorus of voices around, a quick surging forward of the crowd. So that was what had scratched my hand! I buried the wound in my coat pocket.

===Chinese===
"你再不滚远点，"列车员恶狠狠地说，"我自己来扎你。至于您，先生——"他转向我。我完全是无辜的，但我知道自己这副模样活脱脱就是有罪的样子：浑身冷汗，耳边嗡嗡声持续不断。"至于您，先生——"

那个按捺不住的业余侦探猛地扑向枕头，一把掀开枕套。在我们难以置信的目光中，他抽出了一把窄长的钢制匕首——匕首已经没入枕头，只露出当柄用的小十字架。

周围一片哗然，人群猛地向前涌动。原来划伤我手的就是这东西！我把受伤的手缩进了大衣口袋。

===Original===
"Well," I said, trying to speak naturally, "doesn't that prove what I have been telling you? The man who committed the murder belonged to this berth, and made an exchange in some way after the crime. How do you know he didn't change the tags so I would come back to this berth?" This was an inspiration; I was pleased with it. "That's what he did, he changed the tags," I reiterated.

There was a murmur of assent around. The doctor, who was standing beside me, put his hand on my arm. "If this gentleman committed this crime, and I for one feel sure he did not, then who is the fellow who got away? And why did he go?"

"We have only one man's word for that," the conductor snarled. "I've traveled some in these cars myself, and no one ever changed berths with *me*."

Somebody on the edge of the group asserted that hereafter he would travel by daylight. I glanced up and caught the eye of the girl in blue.

===Chinese===
"好吧，"我尽量自然地说，"这不正好证明我一直在说的话吗？杀人凶手就是这个铺位的乘客，作案之后想办法调换了铺位。你们怎么知道他没有换过铺牌，好让我回到这张铺位上来？"这想法来得灵感一现；我颇为得意。"他就是这么干的，他换了铺牌，"我又强调了一遍。

周围响起一片附和的低语。站在我身旁的那位医生把手搭在我的胳膊上。"如果说这位先生犯了罪——至少我个人确信他没有——那么，那个逃走的家伙是谁？他又为什么逃走？"

"这可是他一面之词，"列车员嗤之以鼻，"我自己也坐过这种车厢的铺位，可从来没人跟我*换*过。"

人群外围有人宣称，从今以后他只坐白天的火车。我抬起头，正好与蓝衣女子四目相对。

===Original===
"They are all mad," she said. Her tone was low, but I heard her distinctly. "Don't take them seriously enough to defend yourself."

"I am glad you think I didn't do it," I observed meekly, over the crowd. "Nothing else is of any importance."

The conductor had pulled out his notebook again. "Your name, please," he said gruffly.

"Lawrence Blakeley, Washington."

"Your occupation?"

"Attorney. A member of the firm of Blakeley and McKnight."

"Mr. Blakeley, you say you have occupied the wrong berth and have been robbed. Do you know anything of the man who did it?"

"Only from what he left behind," I answered. "These clothes—"

===Chinese===
"他们全是疯子，"她说。声音不大，但我听得很清楚。"别太当回事，犯不着为自己辩解。"

"您相信我是清白的，这比什么都重要。"我隔着人群，温顺地说道。"别的都不打紧。"

列车员又掏出了笔记本。"请报上您的名字，"他粗声粗气地说。

"劳伦斯·布莱克利（Lawrence Blakeley），华盛顿。"

"职业？"

"律师。布莱克利与麦克奈特律师事务所（Blakeley and McKnight）的合伙人。"

"布莱克利先生，您说自己占了错误的铺位，还被抢了。您对干这事的人了解多少？"

"只知道他留下的这些东西，"我答道，"这身衣服——"

===Original===
"They fit you," he said with quick suspicion. "Isn't that rather a coincidence? You are a large man."

"Good Heavens," I retorted, stung into fury, "do I look like a man who would wear this kind of a necktie? Do you suppose I carry purple and green barred silk handkerchiefs? Would any man in his senses wear a pair of shoes a full size too small?"

The conductor was inclined to hedge. "You will have to grant that I am in a peculiar position," he said. "I have only your word as to the exchange of berths, and you understand I am merely doing my duty. Are there any clues in the pockets?"

For the second time I emptied them of their contents, which he noted. "Is that all?" he finished. "There was nothing else?"

"Nothing."

"That's not all, sir," broke in the porter, stepping forward. "There was a small black satchel."

"That's so," I exclaimed. "I forgot the bag. I don't even know where it is."

===Chinese===
"这衣服您穿着正合适，"他立刻起了疑心，"这岂不是太巧了？您可是个大块头。"

"天哪，"我被激怒了，反唇相讥，"我看着像是会系这种领带的人吗？您觉得我会随身带着紫绿条纹的丝手帕吗？哪个脑子正常的人会穿小了整整一号的鞋？"

列车员有点想打太极。"您得理解我的处境，"他说，"关于调换铺位的说法，我只有您的一面之词，而我只是在尽职尽责。口袋里还有没有线索？"

我把口袋里的东西又倒了一遍，他一一记录在案。"就这些？"他最后问，"没有别的了？"

"没有。"

"可不只这些，先生，"搬运工插嘴道，走上前来，"还有一个黑色小提箱。"

"说得对，"我叫道，"我忘了那只提包，甚至不知道它在哪儿。"

===Original===
The easily swayed crowd looked suspicious again. I've grown so accustomed to reading the faces of a jury, seeing them swing from doubt to belief, and back again to doubt, that I instinctively watch expressions. I saw that my forgetfulness had done me harm—that suspicion was roused again.

The bag was found a couple of seats away, under somebody's raincoat—another dubious circumstance. Was I hiding it? It was brought to the berth and placed beside the conductor, who opened it at once.

===Chinese===
立场不坚定的人群又露出了怀疑的神色。我太习惯于观察陪审团的表情了——看他们从犹疑到确信，再从确信回到犹疑——因此本能地关注别人的神情。我看得出来，我的健忘又给自己添了麻烦——怀疑再度被点燃。

那只提包在几排座位之外找到了，就在某人的雨衣底下——又一个可疑的细节。我是有意藏的吗？它被拿到铺位旁，放在列车员手边，他立刻打开了。

===Original===
It contained the usual traveling impedimenta—change of linen, collars, handkerchiefs, a bronze-green scarf, and a safety razor. But the attention of the crowd riveted itself on a flat, Russia leather wallet, around which a heavy gum band was wrapped, and which bore in gilt letters the name "Simon Harrington."

===Chinese===
里面装的都是些寻常的旅行用品——换洗的衬衣、领子、手帕、一条铜绿色的围巾，还有一把安全剃须刀。但所有人的目光都锁在了一只扁平的俄国皮革钱包上，钱包外头缠着一圈厚实的橡皮筋，烫金印着一个名字——"西蒙·哈林顿（Simon Harrington）"。

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
A Fine Gold Chain

The conductor held it out to me, his face sternly accusing.

“Is this another coincidence?” he asked. “Did the man who left you his clothes and the barred silk handkerchief and the tight shoes leave you the spoil of the murder?”

The men standing around had drawn off a little, and I saw the absolute futility of any remonstrance. Have you ever seen a fly, who, in these hygienic days, finding no cobwebs to entangle him, is caught in a sheet of fly paper, finds himself more and more mired, and is finally quiet with the sticky stillness of despair?

===Chinese===
一根细金链

列车员把那东西递到我面前，脸上带着严厉的指控神情。

"这又是一个巧合吗？"他问道。"把衣服、有条纹的丝绸手帕和紧脚的鞋留给你的那个人，是不是也把谋杀的赃物留给了你？"

围在旁边的人稍微退开了一点，我看出来了，任何辩解都是徒劳。你见过苍蝇吗？在这个讲究卫生的时代，它找不到蛛网来缠住自己，却落在了一张粘蝇纸上，越陷越深，最后带着绝望的黏腻沉寂安静下来。

===Original===
Well, I was the fly. I had seen too much of circumstantial evidence to have any belief that the establishing of my identity would weigh much against the other incriminating details. It meant imprisonment and trial, probably, with all the notoriety and loss of practice they would entail. A man thinks quickly at a time like that. All the probable consequences of the finding of that pocketbook flashed through my mind as I extended my hand to take it. Then I drew my arm back.

“I don’t want it,” I said. “Look inside. Maybe the other man took the money and left the wallet.”

The conductor opened it, and again there was a curious surging forward of the crowd. To my intense disappointment the money was still there.

===Chinese===
好吧，我就是那只苍蝇。我见过太多间接证据的把戏了，绝不相信证明了我的身份就能抵消其他那些对我不利的细节。那意味着坐牢和受审，八成还会惹来满城风雨、丢掉律师饭碗。人在那种时候脑子转得飞快。发现那个钱包可能带来的一切后果在我脑海里一闪而过，我伸出手去接，随即又缩了回来。

"我不要它，"我说。"打开看看。也许另一个人把钱拿走了，只留下了钱包。"

列车员打开钱包，人群又奇怪地向前涌来。让我大失所望的是，钱还在里面。

===Original===
I stood blankly miserable while it was counted out—five one-hundred-dollar bills, six twenties, and some fives and ones that brought the total to six hundred and fifty dollars.

The little man with the notebook insisted on taking the numbers of the notes, to the conductor’s annoyance. It was immaterial to me: small things had lost their power to irritate. I was seeing myself in the prisoner’s box, going through all the nerve-racking routine of a trial for murder—the challenging of the jury, the endless cross-examinances, the alternate hope and fear. I believe I said before that I had no nerves, but for a few minutes that morning I was as near as a man ever comes to hysteria.

===Chinese===
我呆若木鸡地站在那里，看着钱被点出来——五张一百美元钞票、六张二十美元钞票，加上一些五美元和一美元的零钞，总共六百五十美元。

那个拿笔记本的小个子非要抄下钞票号码，列车员被他惹恼了。我倒无所谓了：小事已经失去了激怒我的力量。我看见自己站在被告席上，经受谋杀案审判那套令人神经崩溃的例行程序——陪审团的甄选、没完没了的交叉询问、希望与恐惧交替折磨。我之前大概说过我神经很坚强，但那天早上有那么几分钟，我离一个人所能达到的歇斯底里只有一步之遥了。

===Original===
I folded my arms and gave myself a mental shake. I seemed to be the center of a hundred eyes, expressing every shade of doubt and distrust, but I tried not to flinch. Then someone created a diversion.

The amateur detective was busy again with the sealskin bag, investigating the make of the safety razor and the manufacturer’s name on the bronze-green tie. Now, however, he paused and frowned, as though some pet theory had been upset.

Then from a corner of the bag he drew out and held up for our inspection some three inches of fine gold chain, one end of which was blackened and stained with blood!

The conductor held out his hand for it, but the little man was not ready to give it up. He turned to me.

“You say no watch was left you? Was there a piece of chain like that?”

“No chain at all,” I said sulkily. “No jewelry of any kind, except plain gold buttons in the shirt I am wearing.”

===Chinese===
我抱起双臂，在心里给自己打了一针强心剂。我似乎成了上百双眼睛的靶心，每双眼睛都表达着不同程度的怀疑和不信任，但我尽量不退缩。这时有人打了个岔。

那位业余侦探又忙活起那只海豹皮包来了，检查安全剃须刀的牌子和那条青铜绿色领带上的制造商名字。然而他停了下来，皱起眉头，好像自己珍视的某个理论被推翻了。

接着他从包角里掏出一根大约三英寸长的细金链，举起来给我们看——一端发黑，还沾着血迹！

列车员伸手要接，但小个子还不打算交出来。他转向我。

"你说没有人给你留表？有没有这样一根链子？"

"根本没链子，"我没好气地说。"什么首饰也没有，除了我身上这件衬衫上的素金纽扣。"

===Original===
“Where are your glasses?” he threw at me suddenly: instinctively my hand went to my eyes. My glasses had been gone all morning, and I had not even noticed their absence. The little man smiled cynically and held out the chain.

“I must ask you to examine this,” he insisted. “Isn’t it a part of the fine gold chain you wear over your ear?”

I didn’t want to touch the thing: the stain at the end made me shudder. But with a baker’s dozen of suspicious eyes—well, we’ll say fourteen: there were no one-eyed men—I took the fragment in the tips of my fingers and looked at it helplessly.

“Very fine chains are much alike,” I managed to say. “For all I know, this may be mine, but I don’t know how it got into that sealskin bag. I never saw the bag until this morning after daylight.”

===Chinese===
"你的眼镜呢？"他突然向我发难：我不由自主地伸手摸了摸眼睛。我的眼镜整个上午都不见了，我甚至没注意到它们丢了。小个子冷笑了一下，递过那根链子。

"请你检查一下这个，"他坚持道。"这不就是你戴在耳朵上那根细金链的一部分吗？"

我不想去碰那东西：末端的血迹让我浑身发抖。可是在十几双充满怀疑的眼睛盯着之下——好吧，说是十四双吧：没有独眼龙——我用指尖捏起那截链子，无可奈何地端详着。

"很细的链子长得都很像，"我总算说道。"说不定这根就是我的，可我不知道它是怎么跑到那只海豹皮包里去的。我今天天亮之前从没见过那只包。"

===Original===
“He admits that he had the bag,” somebody said behind me. “How did you guess that he wore glasses, anyhow?” to the amateur sleuth.

That gentleman cleared his throat. “There were two reasons,” he said, “for suspecting it. When you see a man with the lines of his face drooping, a healthy individual with a pensive eye—suspect astigmatism. Besides, this gentleman has a pronounced line across the bridge of his nose and a mark on his ear from the chain.”

After this remarkable exhibition of the theoretical as combined with the practical, he sank into a seat nearby, and still holding the chain, sat with closed eyes and pursed lips. It was evident to all the car that the solution of the mystery was a question of moments. Once he bent forward eagerly and putting the chain on the windowsill, proceeded to go over it with a pocket magnifying glass, only to shake his head in disappointment. All the people around shook their heads too, although they had not the slightest idea what it was about.

===Chinese===
"他承认包是他的了，"我身后有人说。"对了，你怎么猜到他戴眼镜的？"问的是那位业余侦探。

那位先生清了清嗓子。"有两个理由，"他说，"怀疑他戴眼镜。当你看到一个人面部线条下垂、明明是个健康人却眼神忧郁——就该怀疑散光。此外，这位先生鼻梁上有道明显的压痕，耳朵上还有链子留下的印记。"

这番理论与实践相结合的精彩表演结束后，他一屁股坐到旁边的座位上，手里仍攥着那根链子，闭着眼睛，嘴唇紧抿。整节车厢的人都看出来了，谜底马上就要揭晓。有一次他急切地俯身向前，把链子放在窗台上，掏出一个袖珍放大镜仔细检查，结果失望地摇了摇头。周围的人也跟着摇了摇头，尽管他们压根不知道在看什么。

===Original===
The pounding in my ears began again. The group around me seemed to be suddenly motionless in the very act of moving, as if a hypnotist had called “Rigid!” The girl in blue was looking at me, and above the din I thought she said she must speak to me—something vital. The pounding grew louder and merged into a scream. With a grinding and splintering the car rose under my feet. Then it fell away into darkness.

===Chinese===
我耳朵里的轰鸣声又开始了。周围那群人似乎在移动的动作中突然定格了，好像催眠师喊了一声"定！"穿蓝衣服的姑娘正看着我，在一片喧嚣之上，我恍惚听见她说一定要跟我说话——什么要紧的事。轰鸣声越来越大，汇成了一声尖叫。伴随着刺耳的摩擦和碎裂声，车厢在我脚下猛然升起。然后它坠入了黑暗。

## VIII / 第二节

===Original===
Have you ever been picked up out of your three-meals-a-day life, whirled around in a tornado of events, and landed in a situation so grotesque and yet so horrible that you laugh even while you are groaning, and straining at its hopelessness? McKnight says that is hysteria, and that no man worthy of the name ever admits to it.

Also, as McKnight says, it sounds like a tank drama. Just as the revolving saw is about to cut the hero into stove lengths, the second villain blows up the sawmill. The hero goes up through the roof and alights on the bank of a stream at the feet of his lady love, who is making daisy chains.

Nevertheless, when I was safely home again, with Mrs. Klopton brewing strange drinks that came in paper packets from the pharmacy, and that smelled to heaven, I remember staggering to the door and closing it, and then going back to bed and howling out the absurdity and the madness of the whole thing. And while I laughed my very soul was sick, for the girl was gone by that time, and I knew by all the loyalty that answers between men for honor that I would have to put her out of my mind.

And yet, all the night that followed, filled as it was with the shrieking demons of pain, I saw her as I had seen her last, in the queer hat with green ribbons. I told the doctor this, guardedly, the next morning, and he said it was the morphia, and that I was lucky not to have seen a row of devils with green tails.

===Chinese===
你有没有过这样的经历——从日复一日三餐温饱的生活里被猛然拽出，卷入一场事件的龙卷风，最后落在一个既荒诞又可怕的处境里，让你一边呻吟、一边为它的绝望而发笑？麦克奈特（McKnight）说那叫歇斯底里，任何配得上男子汉这称号的人都不会承认。

他还说，这听起来就像一出蹩脚的冒险剧。就在旋转锯要把英雄锯成柴火段的当口，第二个反派炸毁了锯木厂。英雄从屋顶冲天而起，落在小溪岸边，正好倒在正在编雏菊链的意中人脚边。

然而，当我终于平安回到家中，克洛普顿太太（Mrs. Klopton）在煮从药房纸包里买来的、气味冲天的怪味药水时，我记得自己踉跄着走过去关上门，然后回到床上嚎啕大哭，为整件事的荒谬与疯狂而嚎叫。我一边笑，灵魂却在受苦，因为那时那女孩已经走了，而我深知男人之间讲义气的规矩，我必须把她从脑海里驱逐出去。

可接下来那一整夜，尽管疼痛的恶魔尖声嘶叫，我眼前却始终浮现着她最后的样子——戴着那顶系绿丝带的古怪帽子。第二天早上我谨慎地告诉了医生，他说那是吗啡的作用，我还算走运，没看到一排长着绿尾巴的魔鬼。

===Original===
I don’t know anything about the wreck of September ninth last. You who swallowed the details with your coffee and digested the horrors with your chop, probably know a great deal more than I do. I remember very distinctly that the jumping and throbbing in my arm brought me back to a world that at first was nothing but sky, a heap of clouds that I thought hazily were the meringue on a blue charlotte russe. As the sense of hearing was slowly added to vision, I heard a woman near me sobbing that she had lost her hat pin, and she couldn’t keep her hat on.

===Chinese===
我对去年九月九日的那次事故一无所知。你们那些就着咖啡吞下细节、嚼着牛排消化恐怖场面的人，可能知道的比我多得多。我只清楚地记得，胳膊里的跳动和悸痛把我拽回了一个起初只有天空的世界——一堆云朵，我恍惚觉得那是一道蓝色夏洛特蛋糕上的蛋白霜。随着听觉慢慢加入视觉，我听到旁边有个女人在抽泣，说她丢了帽针，帽子戴不住了。

===Original===
I think I dropped back into unconsciousness again, for the next thing I remember was of my blue patch of sky clouded with smoke, of a strange roaring and crackling, of a rain of fiery sparks on my face and of somebody beating at me with feeble hands. I opened my eyes and closed them again: the girl in blue was bending over me. With that imperviousness to big things and keenness to small that is the first effect of shock, I tried to be facetious, when a spark stung my cheek.

"You will have to rouse yourself!" the girl was repeating desperately. "You’ve been on fire twice already." A piece of striped ticking floated slowly over my head. As the wind caught it its charring edges leaped into flame.

"Looks like a kite, doesn’t it?" I remarked cheerfully. And then, as my arm gave an excruciating throb—"Jove, how my arm hurts!"

The girl bent over and spoke slowly, distinctly, as one might speak to a deaf person or a child.

"Listen, Mr. Blakeley," she said earnestly. "You *must* rouse yourself. There has been a terrible accident. The second section ran into us. The wreck is burning now, and if we don’t move, we will catch fire. Do you hear?"

===Chinese===
我想我又失去了意识，因为我再次有知觉时，只见那片蓝天已被浓烟遮蔽，四周是一片陌生的轰鸣和噼啪作响，火星像雨点一样落在我脸上，有人用软弱无力的手拍打我。我睁开眼又闭上——那个穿蓝衣服的女孩正俯身看着我。人在受了冲击之后，往往对大事麻木、对小事敏感，我就是这样，竟然想说句俏皮话，这时一粒火星灼痛了我的脸颊。

"你得振作起来！"女孩拼命地重复着。"你已经着了两次火了。"一块条纹帆布慢慢飘过我头顶，风一吹，它烧焦的边缘立刻窜起火苗。

"看起来像风筝，是不是？"我乐呵呵地说。接着，胳膊一阵撕心裂肺的剧痛——"天哪，我的胳膊疼死了！"

女孩俯下身，慢慢地说，一个字一个字说得清清楚楚，就像跟聋子或小孩子说话一样。

"听我说，布莱克利先生（Blakeley）"她认真地说。"你*必须*振作起来。出了可怕的事故。第二节车厢撞上了我们。残骸现在正在燃烧，我们不走的话，会被烧着的。你听见了吗？"

===Original===
Her voice and my arm were bringing me to my senses. "I hear," I said. "I—I’ll sit up in a second. Are you hurt?"

"No, only bruised. Do you think you can walk?"

I drew up one foot after another, gingerly.

"They seem to move all right," I remarked dubiously. "Would you mind telling me where the back of my head has gone? I can’t help thinking it isn’t there."

She made a quick examination. "It’s pretty badly bumped," she said. "You must have fallen on it."

===Chinese===
她的声音和我的胳膊让我清醒了过来。"我听见了，"我说。"我——我一会儿就坐起来。你受伤了吗？"

"没有，只是擦伤了。你觉得你能走吗？"

我小心翼翼地先后抬起两只脚。

"看起来还能动，"我迟疑地说。"你介不介意告诉我，我后脑勺去哪儿了？我总觉得它不在了。"

她迅速检查了一下。"撞得挺厉害的，"她说。"你肯定是摔在上面了。

===Original===
I had got up on my uninjured elbow by that time, but the pain threw me back. "Don’t look at the wreck," I entreated her. "It’s no sight for a woman. If—if there is any way to tie up this arm, I might be able to do something. There may be people under those cars!"

"Then it is too late to help," she replied solemnly. A little shower of feathers, each carrying its fiery lamp, blew over us from some burning pillow. A part of the wreck collapsed with a crash. In a resolute endeavor to play a man’s part in the tragedy going on around, I got to my knees. Then I realized what I had not noticed before: the hand and wrist of the broken left arm were jammed through the handle of the sealskin grip. I gasped and sat down suddenly.

===Chinese===
那时我已用没受伤的那只胳膊撑起身子，但一阵剧痛又把我摔了回去。"别看那残骸，"我恳求她。"对女人来说那不是该看的场面。如果——如果有什么办法把这条胳膊绑住，我也许还能做点什么。那些车厢底下可能还有人！"

"那已经来不及救了，"她严肃地回答。一蓬羽毛，每根都顶着一盏小火苗，从某个燃烧的枕头上飘过来，落在我们身上。一部分残骸轰然坍塌。为了在周围这场悲剧中扮演一个男子汉该扮演的角色，我跪了起来。这时我才注意到之前没发现的事：断掉的左臂，手和手腕卡在海豹皮旅行包的提手里了。我倒吸一口凉气，猛地坐了回去。

===Original===
"You must not do that," the girl insisted. I noticed now that she kept her back to the wreck, her eyes averted. "The weight of the traveling-bag must be agony. Let me support the valise until we get back a few yards. Then you must lie down until we can get it cut off."

"Will it have to be cut off?" I asked as calmly as possible. There were red-hot stabs of agony clear to my neck, but we were moving slowly away from the track.

"Yes," she replied, with dumbfounding coolness. "If I had a knife I could do it myself. You might sit here and lean against this fence."

===Chinese===
"你不能那样，"女孩坚持道。我注意到她一直背对着残骸，目光避开。"那个旅行包的重量一定让你痛得要命。让我帮你托着包，等我们退开几码再说。然后你得躺下，等我们找人来把它割掉。"

"非得割掉不可吗？"我尽量平静地问。火烧火燎的剧痛一路窜到脖子，但我们正慢慢离开铁轨。

"是的，"她回答，冷静得令人吃惊。"要是我有把刀，我自己就能割。你可以坐在这儿，靠着这道栅栏。"

===Original===
By that time my returning faculties had realized that she was going to cut off the satchel, not the arm. The dizziness was leaving and I was gradually becoming myself.

"If you pull, it might come," I suggested. "And with that weight gone, I think I will cease to be five feet eleven inches of baby."

She tried gently to loosen the handle, but it would not move, and last, with great drops of cold perspiration over me, I had to give up.

"I’m afraid I can’t stand it," I said. "But there’s a knife somewhere around these clothes, and if I can find it, perhaps you can cut the leather."

As I gave her the knife she turned it over, examining it with a peculiar expression, bewilderment rather than surprise. But she said nothing. She set to work deftly, and in a few minutes the bag dropped free.

"That’s better," I declared, sitting up. "Now, if you can pin my sleeve to my coat, it will support the arm so we can get away from here."

"The pin might give," she objected, "and the jerk would be terrible." She looked around, puzzled; then she got up, coming back in a minute with a draggled, partly scorched sheet. This she tore into a large square, and after she had folded it, she slipped it under the broken arm and tied it securely at the back of my neck.

The relief was immediate, and, picking up the sealskin bag, I walked slowly beside her, away from the track.

The first act was over: the curtain fallen. The scene was "struck."

===Chinese===
那时我逐渐恢复的神志已经明白，她是打算割掉那个皮包，而不是我的胳膊。眩晕正在消退，我渐渐恢复了自己。

"你要是用力拉，也许能拉出来，"我建议道。"那重量一去，我想我就不再是五英尺十一英寸的大宝宝了。"

她轻轻试着松动提手，但它纹丝不动，最后我浑身冷汗直冒，不得不放弃了。

"恐怕我受不了了，"我说。"不过这堆衣服里应该有把刀，如果我能找到，也许你能把皮革割开。"

我把刀递给她时，她翻来覆去地看了看，脸上露出一种奇特的表情——与其说是惊讶，不如说是困惑。但她什么也没说。她灵巧地动手，几分钟后，皮包脱落了。

"这下好多了，"我坐起身说。"现在，如果你能把我的袖子别在外套上，就能固定住胳膊，我们好离开这儿。"

"别针可能会脱开，"她反对道，"那样猛地一拽会疼死人的。"她环顾四周，一脸茫然；然后她站起来，一分钟后拿来一条沾满污迹、部分烧焦的床单。她把床单撕成一大块方布，折叠好后，塞到断臂下面，在我脑后牢牢系住。

束缚解除的轻松感立刻涌了上来，我拎起海豹皮旅行包，慢慢走在他身边，离开了铁轨。

第一幕落幕了：布景撤下。

## IX / 九、宁静的早餐

===Original===
We were still dazed, I think, for we wandered like two troubled children, our one idea at first to get as far away as we could from the horror behind us. We were both bareheaded, grimy, pallid through the grit. Now and then we met little groups of country folk hurrying to the track: they stared at us curiously, and some wished to question us. But we hurried past them; we had put the wreck behind us. That way lay madness.

Only once the girl turned and looked behind her. The wreck was hidden, but the smoke cloud hung heavy and dense. For the first time I remembered that my companion had not been alone on the train.

“It is quiet here,” I suggested. “If you will sit down on the bank I will go back and make some inquiries. I’ve been criminally thoughtless. Your traveling companion—”

She interrupted me, and something of her splendid poise was gone. “Please don’t go back,” she said. “I am afraid it would be of no use. And I don’t want to be left alone.”

===Chinese===
我想我们还处于懵懂之中，因为我们就那样漫无目的地走着，像两个受惊的孩子，一心只想尽可能远离身后的惨祸。我们都没戴帽子，满面尘垢，脸色透过污垢显得苍白。时不时遇到几群匆匆赶往铁轨的乡下人：他们好奇地盯着我们看，有人还想问我们什么。但我们匆匆走过去；我们已经把那场灾难甩在了身后。再想下去，会疯的。

姑娘只回头望过一次。残骸已经看不见了，但浓烟依旧沉甸甸地悬着。我这才第一次想起，我这位同伴在车上并非独自一人。

“这里很安静，”我提议道，“您坐在路堤上，我回去打听一下。我太疏忽了，简直有罪。您那位同车的旅伴——”

她打断了我，那股从容优雅的气质不知怎的已荡然无存。“请别回去，”她说，“我怕回去也没用。而且我不想一个人待着。”

===Original===
Heaven knows I did not want her to be alone. I was more than content to walk along beside her aimlessly, for any length of time. Gradually, as she lost the exaltation of the moment, I was gaining my normal condition of mind. I was beginning to realize that I had lacked the morning grace of a shave, that I looked like some lost hope of yesterday, and that my left shoe pinched outrageously. A man does not rise triumphant above such handicaps. The girl, for all her disordered hair and the crumpled linen of her waist, in spite of her missing hat and the small gold bag that hung forlornly from a broken chain, looked exceedingly lovely.

“Then I won’t leave you alone,” I said manfully, and we stumbled on together. Thus far we had seen nobody from the wreck, but well up the lane we came across the tall dark woman who had occupied lower eleven. She was half crouching beside the road, her black hair about her shoulders, and an ugly bruise over her eye. She did not seem to know us, and refused to accompany us. We left her there at last, babbling incoherently and rolling in her hands a dozen pebbles she had gathered in the road.

The girl shuddered as we went on. Once she turned and glanced at my bandage. “Does it hurt very much?” she asked.

“It’s growing rather numb. But it might be worse,” I answered mendaciously. If anything in this world could be worse, I had never experienced it.

===Chinese===
天知道我也不愿她一个人待着。我很乐意漫无目的地陪她走下去，多久都行。渐渐地，她从那种极度亢奋中平复下来，而我也开始恢复正常心态。我开始意识到，我今早没来得及刮脸，看上去像个被昨日遗弃的希望，而且我的左脚鞋紧得要命。一个男人很难在这种窘况下还趾高气扬。而那姑娘，尽管头发散乱、衬衫褶皱，尽管丢了帽子、那只小金手袋断了链子孤零零地挂着，依然美得惊人。

“那我就陪着您，”我壮着胆子说。于是我们继续跌跌撞撞地往前走。到目前为止，我们还没有遇到一个从灾难现场出来的人，但在巷子深处，我们碰上了那个住在十一下铺的高个子深色头发女人。她半蹲在路边，黑发披散在肩头，眼睛上方有一块难看的淤伤。她似乎不认识我们，不肯跟我们一起走。我们最后只好丢下她，她坐在那儿胡言乱语，手里揉搓着在路上捡的十几颗鹅卵石。

姑娘一路走，一路哆嗦。有一次她回头看了看我的绷带。“很疼吗？”她问。

“已经开始发麻了。不过可能会更糟，”我撒了个谎。如果这世上还有什么比这更糟的，我还没经历过。

===Original===
And so we trudged on bareheaded under the summer sun, growing parched and dusty and weary, doggedly leaving behind us the pillar of smoke. I thought I knew of a trolley line somewhere in the direction we were going, or perhaps we could find a horse and trap to take us into Baltimore. The girl smiled when I suggested it.

“We will create a sensation, won’t we?” she asked. “Isn’t it queer—or perhaps it’s my state of mind—but I keep wishing for a pair of gloves, when I haven’t even a hat!”

When we reached the main road we sat down for a moment, and her hair, which had been coming loose for some time, fell over her shoulders in little waves that were most alluring. It seemed a pity to twist it up again, but when I suggested this, cautiously, she said it was troublesome and got in her eyes when it was loose. So she gathered it up, while I held a row of little shell combs and pins, and when it was done it was vastly becoming, too. Funny about hair: a man never knows he has it until he begins to lose it, but it’s different with a girl. Something of the unconventional situation began to dawn on her as she put in the last hairpin and pinned some stray locks to place.

===Chinese===
就这样，我们在夏日骄阳下无帽行走，越走越渴，越走越脏，越走越累，固执地把身后那根烟柱甩得远远的。我想我应该知道我们这个方向某处有有轨电车线，或许还能找到马车把我们拉进巴尔的摩。当我提出这个建议时，姑娘笑了。

“我们会引起轰动的，是不是？”她问，“说来也怪——也许是我心绪不宁——但我一直想要一副手套，可我连帽子都没了！”

等我们走上大路，我们坐下来歇了一会儿。她的头发已经松了好一阵子，此刻散落在肩头，形成一道道迷人的小波浪。再把它盘上去似乎有些可惜，不过当我小心翼翼地提出这个建议时，她说散着碍事、会遮眼睛。于是她开始拢头发，我拿着一排贝壳小发梳和发卡递给她。头发盘好之后，样子居然也十分好看。头发真是奇妙：男人在开始掉头发之前从来不知道自己有头发，而女人就不一样了。当她别上最后一枚发卡、把几缕碎发归拢好时，某种不合时宜的尴尬开始在她脸上浮现。

===Original===
“I have not told you my name,” she said abruptly. “I forgot that because I know who you are, you know nothing about me. I am Alison West, and my home is in Richmond.”

So that was it! This was the girl of the photograph on John Gilmore’s bedside table. The girl McKnight expected to see in Richmond the next day, Sunday! She was on her way back to meet him! Well, what difference did it make, anyhow? We had been thrown together by the merest chance. In an hour or two at the most we would be back in civilization and she would recall me, if she remembered me at all, as an unshaven creature in a red cravat and tan shoes, with a soiled Pullman sheet tied around my neck. I drew a deep breath.

“Just a twinge,” I said, when she glanced up quickly. “It’s very good of you to let me know, Miss West. I have been hearing delightful things about you for three months.”

“From Richey McKnight?” She was frankly curious.

“Yes. From Richey McKnight,” I assented. Was it any wonder McKnight was crazy about her? I dug my heels into the dust.

===Chinese===
“我还没告诉你我的名字，”她突然说道，“我忘了，因为我知道你是谁，你却对我一无所知。我是艾莉森·韦斯特（Alison West），家在里士满。”

原来如此！这就是约翰·吉尔摩床头柜上那张照片里的姑娘。就是麦克奈特打算周日、也就是明天去里士满见面的那个姑娘！她正赶回去与他相会！好吧，不管怎样，那又有什么关系？我们不过是最偶然的机会被抛在了一起。最多再过一两个小时，我们就会回到文明世界，而她——假如还记得我的话——会记得我是一个胡子拉碴、系着红领带、穿着棕黄鞋、脖子上拴着一条脏兮兮的普尔曼床单的家伙。我深吸了一口气。

“只是一点刺痛，”在她迅速抬头看我时我说，“韦斯特小姐，您能告诉我您的名字，真是太好了。这三个月来，我可没少听人说您的好话。”

“是里奇·麦克奈特（Richey McKnight）告诉您的？”她坦率地好奇道。

“是的。里奇·麦克奈特，”我应道。难怪麦克奈特为她神魂颠倒。我把脚后跟使劲往灰尘里踩了踩。

===Original===
“I have been visiting near Cresson, in the mountains,” Miss West was saying. “The person you mentioned, Mrs. Curtis, was my hostess. We—we were on our way to Washington together.” She spoke slowly, as if she wished to give the minimum of explanation. Across her face had come again the baffling expression of perplexity and trouble I had seen before.

“You were on your way home, I suppose? Richey spoke about seeing you,” I floundered, finding it necessary to say something. She looked at me with level, direct eyes.

“No,” she returned quietly. “I did not intend to go home. I—well, it doesn’t matter; I am going home now.”

A woman in a calico dress, with two children, each an exact duplicate of the other, had come quickly down the road. She took in the situation at a glance, and was explosively hospitable.

“You poor things,” she said. “If you’ll take the first road to the left over there, and turn in at the second pigsty, you will find breakfast on the table and a coffeepot on the stove. And there’s plenty of soap and water, too. Don’t say one word. There isn’t a soul there to see you.”

===Chinese===
“我去山里克雷森（Cresson）附近小住了，”韦斯特小姐接着说，“您提到的那位柯蒂斯太太（Mrs. Curtis）是我们的女主人。我们——我们要一起去华盛顿。”她说话很慢，仿佛想给出尽可能少的解释。她脸上又浮现出那种令人困惑的、既迷惘又忧虑的神情，我之前就见过一次。

“您是回家去，我猜？里奇提到过要见您，”我语无伦次地说，觉得必须说点什么。她目光平静而直率地看着我。

“不，”她平静地答道，“我原本没打算回家。我——唉，无所谓了；反正我现在是要回家了。”

一个穿着印花布裙的女人，带着两个孩子——两个孩子简直就是一模一样——沿着路快步走来。她一眼就看清了情况，热情得简直要炸开来。

“可怜的人啊，”她说，“你们顺着那边第一条左拐的路走，在第二个猪圈那儿拐进去，早饭已经摆在桌上，炉子上煮着咖啡。还有充足的肥皂和水。什么也别说。那儿没人看见你们。”

===Original===
We accepted the invitation and she hurried on toward the excitement and the railroad. I got up carefully and helped Miss West to her feet.

“At the second pigsty to the left,” I repeated, “we will find the breakfast I promised you seven eternities ago. Forward to the pigsty!”

===Chinese===
我们接受邀请，她就匆匆赶去看热闹、看铁路去了。我小心翼翼地站起来，扶韦斯特小姐起身。

“左拐第二个猪圈，”我重复道，“我们就能找到我七辈子前就许诺您的早饭了。向猪圈进发！”

===Original===
We said very little for the remainder of that walk. I had almost reached the limit of endurance: with every step the broken ends of the bone grated together. We found the farmhouse without difficulty, and I remember wondering if I could hold out to the end of the old stone walk that between hedges led to the door.

“Allah be praised,” I said with all the voice I could muster. “Behold the coffeepot!” And then I put down the grip and folded up like a jackknife on the porch floor.

When I came around something hot was trickling down my neck, and a despairing voice was saying, “Oh, I don’t seem to be able to pour it into your mouth. Please open your eyes.”

“But I don’t want it in my eyes,” I replied dreamily. “I haven’t any idea what came over me. It was the shoes, I think: the left one is a red-hot torture.” I was sitting by that time and looking across into her face.

Never before or since have I fainted, but I would do it joyfully, a dozen times a day, if I could waken again to the blissful touch of soft fingers on my face, the hot ecstasy of coffee spilled by those fingers down my neck. There was a thrill in every tone of her voice that morning. Before long my loyalty to McKnight would step between me and the girl he loved: life would develop new complexities. In those early hours after the wreck, full of pain as they were, there was nothing of the suspicion and distrust that came later. Shorn of our gauds and baubles, we were primitive man and woman, together: our world for the hour was the deserted farmhouse, the slope of wheat-field that led to the road, the woodland lot, the pasture.

===Chinese===
在余下的路上我们几乎没怎么说话。我的忍耐已经快到极限了：每走一步，断裂的骨头就相互磨擦。我们毫不费力地找到了那栋农舍，我记得当时还在想，我还能不能撑到那条夹在树篱间通往门口的古老石子路的尽头。

“真主保佑，”我竭尽全力喊道，“咖啡壶啊！”然后我放下旅行袋，像把折刀一样在门廊地板上瘫成一团。

等我醒过来，有什么热乎乎的东西顺着我的脖子往下淌，一个绝望的声音正在说：“哎呀，我好像倒不进你嘴里。请睁开眼睛。”

“可我不想让它流进眼睛里，”我迷迷糊糊地答道，“我完全不知道自己怎么了。我猜是那双鞋：左脚那只简直像烧红了的烙铁。”那时候我已经坐起来了，抬头看着她的脸。

我以前从没晕倒过，以后也再没晕过。不过，如果能再次醒来时感受到柔软的手指轻触我脸庞的美妙感觉，感受到那手指将热咖啡淋在我脖子上的迷醉快感，我愿意一天晕上十几次。那天早晨她的声音里有一种颤栗。要不了多久，我对麦克奈特的忠诚就会挡在我和他心爱的姑娘之间：生活将展现新的复杂。但在事故后最初这几个小时里，尽管痛苦，却全然没有后来那种猜疑与不信任。剥去我们的虚饰与浮华，我们不过是一男一女，最原始的两个人：在那一个小时里，我们的世界就是这栋空寂的农舍、通往大路的小麦田坡地、林地、牧场。

===Original===
We breakfasted together across the homely table. Our cheerfulness, at first sheer reaction, became less forced as we ate great slices of bread from the granny oven back of the house, and drank hot fluid that smelled like coffee and tasted like nothing that I have ever swallowed. We found cream in stone jars, sunk deep in the chill water of the spring house. And there were eggs, great yellow-brown ones—a basket of them.

So, like two children awakened from a nightmare, we chattered over our food: we hunted mutual friends, we laughed together at my feeble witticisms, but we put the horror behind us resolutely. After all, it was the hat with the green ribbons that brought back the strangeness of the situation.

All along I had had the impression that Alison West was deliberately putting out of her mind something that obtruded now and then. It brought with it a return of the puzzled expression that I had surprised early in the day, before the wreck. I caught it once, when, breakfast over, she was tightening the sling that held the broken arm. I had prolonged the morning meal as much as I could, but when the wooden clock with the pink roses on the dial pointed to half after ten, and the mother with the duplicate youngsters had not come back, Miss West made the move I had dreaded.

“If we are to get into Baltimore at all we must start,” she said, rising. “You ought to see a doctor as soon as possible.”

“Hush,” I said warningly. “Don’t mention the arm, please; it is asleep now. You may rouse it.”

“If I only had a hat,” she reflected. “It wouldn’t need to be much of a one, but—” She gave a little cry and darted to the corner. “Look,” she said triumphantly, “the very thing. With the green streamers tied up in a bow, like this—do you suppose the child would mind? I can put five dollars or so here—that would buy a dozen of them.”

===Chinese===
我们隔着一张朴素的桌子一起吃早餐。我们的欢快起初纯粹是劫后余生的反应，但随着我们大口吃着从屋后老式烤炉里拿出的大片面包，喝着那种闻着像咖啡、喝起来却不像我这辈子喝过的任何东西的热饮，就渐渐不那么勉强了。我们在石罐里找到了奶油，那些石罐沉在冷藏室冰冷的水中。还有鸡蛋，大个的黄褐色鸡蛋——整整一篮子。

就这样，像两个刚从噩梦中醒来的孩子，我们边吃边叽叽喳喳聊开了：我们搜寻共同的朋友，我讲的蹩脚笑话她也跟着笑，但我们都坚决把那场惨祸抛在了身后。说到底，正是那顶系着绿丝带的帽子，才重新勾起那场景的诡异气氛。

我一直隐约觉得，艾莉森·韦斯特在刻意把某种不时冒出来的东西压下去。那东西一出现，就会带回来我早些时候、事故发生前在她脸上捕捉到的那种困惑神情。我捕捉到过一次，那是在早餐后她收紧固定断臂的吊带时。我已经尽可能拖长这顿早餐时间了，但当那只表面画着粉红玫瑰的木钟指针指向十点半，而那位带着双胞胎的妇人仍未返回时，韦斯特小姐做出了我一直害怕的举动。

“我们要是还想进巴尔的摩，就必须出发了，”她站起身说，“您该尽快去看医生。”

“嘘，”我警告道，“请别提胳膊；现在它麻木了，别把它弄醒。”

“要是我有一顶帽子就好了，”她思忖道，“不需要太好的，可是——”她轻叫一声，冲到屋角。“瞧，”她得意地说，“正合适。把绿色饰带打成一个蝴蝶结，像这样——您说那孩子会介意吗？我可以放五块钱左右在这儿——够买一打帽子了。”

===Original===
It was a queer affair of straw, that hat, with a round crown and a rim that flopped dismally. With a single movement she had turned it up at one side and fitted it to her head. Grotesque by itself, when she wore it it was a thing of joy.

Evidently the lack of head covering had troubled her, for she was elated at her find. She left me, scrawling a note of thanks and pinning it with a bill to the tablecloth, and ran upstairs to the mirror and the promised soap and water.

I did not see her when she came down. I had discovered a bench with a tin basin outside the kitchen door, and was washing, in a helpless, one-sided way. I felt rather than saw that she was standing in the doorway, and I made a final plunge into the basin.

“How is it possible for a man with only a right hand to wash his left ear?” I asked from the roller towel. I was distinctly uncomfortable: men are more rigidly creatures of convention than women, whether they admit it or not. “There is so much soap on me still that if I laugh I will blow bubbles. Washing with rainwater and homemade soap is like motoring on a slippery road. I only struck the high places.”

Then, having achieved a brilliant polish with the towel, I looked at the girl.

She was leaning against the frame of the door, her face perfectly colorless, her breath coming in slow, difficult respirations. The erratic hat was pinned to place, but it had slid rakishly to one side. When I realized that she was staring, not at me, but past me to the road along which we had come, I turned and followed her gaze. There was no one in sight: the lane stretched dust white in the sun—no moving figure on it, no sign of life.

===Chinese===
那是一顶古怪的草帽，圆顶帽檐耷拉着，一副愁眉苦脸的样子。她一个动作就把它一边翻折上去，戴在头上。帽子本身看着滑稽，戴在她头上却成了个可爱的小玩意儿。

显然缺少头饰让她很困扰，所以她为这个发现欣喜若狂。她把我丢在那儿，草草写了一张感谢便条，用一张钞票别在桌布上，就跑上楼去找那面镜子和答应过的肥皂和水了。

她下来时我没看见。我在厨房门外发现了一条长凳，上面放着一个锡盆，正在笨拙地、单手笨拙地洗脸。我与其说是看见、不如说是感觉到她站在门口，于是猛扎进盆里。

“一个只有右手的人怎么洗左耳呢？”我隔着卷筒毛巾问道。我浑身不自在：男人比女人更拘泥于体面，承不承认都一样。“我身上肥皂太多，一咧嘴就要吹泡泡。用雨水和自制肥皂洗脸，就像在光滑的路上开车——我只蹭到了高处。”

然后，我用毛巾把自己擦得锃亮，抬头看了看那姑娘。

她斜靠在门框上，脸色完全没了血色，呼吸缓慢而费力。那顶歪歪扭扭的帽子已经别好了，却俏皮地歪向一边。当我意识到她不是在看我，而是越过我凝望我们来时的那条路时，我转过身，顺着她的目光望去。路上空无一人：巷子在阳光下白得刺眼——没有移动的人影，没有任何生命的迹象。

## X / 韦斯特小姐的请求

===Original===
The surprising change in her held me speechless. All the animation of the breakfast table was gone: there was no hint of the response with which, before, she had met my nonsensical sallies. She stood there, white-lipped, unsmiling, staring down the dusty road. One hand was clenched tight over some small object. Her eyes dropped to it from the distant road, and then closed, with a quick, indrawn breath. Her color came back slowly. Whatever had caused the change, she said nothing. She was anxious to leave at once, almost impatient over my deliberate masculine way of getting my things together. Afterward I recalled that I had wanted to explore the barn for a horse and some sort of a vehicle to take us to the trolley, and that she had refused to allow me to look. I remembered many things later that might have helped me, and did not. At the time, I was only completely bewildered. Save the wreck, the responsibility for which lay between Providence and the engineer of the second section, all the events of that strange morning were logically connected; they came from one cause, and tended unerringly to one end. But the cause was buried, the end not yet in view.

===Chinese===
她身上那突如其来的变化让我哑口无言。早餐桌上的所有活泼劲儿全没了：再也看不到她先前回应我那些胡言乱语时的神采。她站在那里，嘴唇发白，面无表情，目光顺着尘土飞扬的马路望向远方。一只手紧紧攥着某个小物件。她的视线从远处的路面移到那东西上，随即闭上眼，急促地吸了一口气。脸色才慢慢恢复。不管是什么引起了这番变化，她只字未提。她急着立刻就走，几乎对我慢条斯理收拾东西的男子汉做派感到不耐烦。事后我才想起，我原本想去谷仓找匹马和某种车辆送我们去电车道，而她不许我去查看。后来我想起许多本该当时就注意的事，但当时没有。那时我只有茫然。除了那场事故——其责任介于天意和第二节车厢的司机之间——那个奇特早晨的所有事件在逻辑上都是相互关联的；它们出自同一个原因，又毫无偏差地指向同一个结局。只是那原因已被掩埋，结局尚未显现。

===Original===
Not until we had left the house well behind did the girl’s face relax its tense lines. I was watching her more closely than I had realized, for when we had gone a little way along the road she turned to me almost petulantly. "Please don’t stare so at me," she said, to my sudden confusion. "I know the hat is dreadful. Green always makes me look ghastly."

"Perhaps it was the green." I was unaccountably relieved. "Do you know, a few minutes ago, you looked almost pallid to me!"

She glanced at me quickly, but I was gazing ahead. We were out of sight of the house, now, and with every step away from it the girl was obviously relieved. Whatever she held in her hand, she never glanced at it. But she was conscious of it every second. She seemed to come to a decision about it while we were still in sight of the gate, for she murmured something and turned back alone, going swiftly, her feet stirring up small puffs of dust at every step. She fastened something to the gatepost—I could see the nervous haste with which she worked. When she joined me again it was without explanation. But the clenched fingers were free now, and while she looked tired and worn, the strain had visibly relaxed.

===Chinese===
直到我们离那座房子很远了，女孩紧绷的脸才松弛下来。我注视着她的程度超出了自己的意识，因为沿路走了一小段后，她几乎是任性地转向我。"请别那样盯着我看，"她说，让我突然窘迫起来。"我知道那帽子很可怕。绿色总让我显得死气沉沉。"

"也许就是那绿色。"我莫名地松了口气。"你知道吗，刚才你看起来简直苍白得吓人！"

她飞快地瞥了我一眼，但我正目视前方。现在我们已经看不见那座房子了，每走远一步，女孩就明显轻松一些。无论她手里攥着什么，她从未瞥一眼，却每时每刻都意识到它的存在。我们还能看见大门时，她似乎就拿定了主意，因为她低声说了句什么，独自转身快步走了回去，每一步都扬起一小团尘土。她把什么东西系在了门柱上——我看得出她动作里那种紧张的仓促。她再次回到我身边时，什么也没解释。但攥紧的手指已经松开了，虽然看起来疲惫不堪，那紧张已经明显缓解了。

===Original===
We walked along slowly in the general direction of the suburban trolley line. Once a man with an empty wagon offered us a lift, but after a glance at the springless vehicle I declined.

"The ends of the bone think they are castanets as it is," I explained. "But the lady—"

The young lady, however, declined and we went on together. Once, when the trolley line was in sight, she got a pebble in her low shoe, and we sat down under a tree until she found the cause of the trouble.

"I—I don’t know what I should have done without you," I blundered. "Moral support and—and all that. Do you know, my first conscious thought after the wreck was of relief that you had not been hurt?"

She was sitting beside me, where a big chestnut tree shaded the road, and I surprised a look of misery on her face that certainly my words had not been meant to produce.

"And my first thought," she said slowly, "was regret that I—that I hadn’t been obliterated, blown out like a candle. Please don’t look like that! I am only talking."

But her lips were trembling, and because the little shams of society are forgotten at times like this, I leaned over and patted her hand lightly, where it rested on the grass beside me.

"You must not say those things," I expostulated. "Perhaps, after all, your friends—"

===Chinese===
我们朝着郊区电车道的方向慢慢走着。有一次，一个赶空车的男人要搭我们一程，但我看了一眼那辆没有弹簧的车，谢绝了。

"骨头两端已经觉得自己是响板了，"我解释道。"而且这位女士——"

但那位年轻女士也谢绝了，我们继续一起走。有回电车道已经望得见了，她低帮鞋里进了颗石子，我们便在一棵树下坐下来，直到她找到麻烦的根源。

"我——我不知道没有你我该怎么办，"我脱口而出。"精神上的支持，还有——还有那些。你知道吗，事故后我恢复意识时第一个念头就是松了口气，因为你没有受伤。"

她坐在我旁边，头顶是一棵大栗树，浓荫遮蔽着路面，我意外地捕捉到她脸上一丝痛苦的神情——那显然不是我这番话想要引发的。

"而我的第一个念头，"她缓缓说道，"是遗憾——遗憾我没有被消灭，像蜡烛一样被吹灭。请别那样看着我！我只是说说而已。"

但她的嘴唇在颤抖，而在这种时刻，社会上的那些小小伪装都会被忘掉，我倾过身，轻轻拍了拍她放在身旁草地上的手。

"你不该说那样的话，"我规劝道。"也许，说到底，你的朋友们——"

===Original===
"I had no friends on the train." Her voice was hard again, her tone final. She drew her hand from under mine, not quickly, but decisively. A car was in sight, coming toward us. The steel finger of civilization, of propriety, of visiting cards and formal introductions was beckoning us in. Miss West put on her shoe.

We said little on the car. The few passengers stared at us frankly, and discussed the wreck, emphasizing its horrors. The girl did not seem to hear. Once she turned to me with the quick, unexpected movement that was one of her charms.

"I do not wish my mother to know I was in the accident," she said. "Will you please not tell Richey about having met me?"

I gave my promise, of course. Again, when we were almost into Baltimore, she asked to examine the gunmetal cigarette case, and sat silent with it in her hands, while I told of the early morning’s events on the Ontario.

"So you see," I finished, "this grip, everything I have on, belongs to a fellow named Sullivan. He probably left the train before the wreck—perhaps just after the murder."

"And so—you think he committed the—the crime?" Her eyes were on the cigarette case.

"Naturally," I said. "A man doesn’t jump off a Pullman car in the middle of the night in another man’s clothes, unless he is trying to get away from something. Besides the dirk, there were the stains that you saw. Why, I have the murdered man’s pocketbook in this valise at my feet. What does that look like?"

===Chinese===
"我在火车上没有朋友。"她的声音又硬了起来，语气不容置辩。她把手从我手底下抽走，不快，却很坚决。一辆电车正向我们驶来。文明的手指、礼节的手指、名片和正式介绍的手指在招手示意我们上车。韦斯特小姐（Miss West）穿上了鞋。

我们在车上很少说话。那几个乘客直勾勾地盯着我们，讨论着那场事故，强调它的恐怖。女孩似乎充耳不闻。有一回她转向我，带着那种出其不意的敏捷动作——那是她的魅力之一。

"我不想让我母亲知道我出了事故，"她说。"请你别告诉里奇（Richey）你遇见我的事，好吗？"

我当然答应了。又一次，快到巴尔的摩时，她要求看看那个枪铜色的烟盒，然后默默地捧在手里，而我讲述了清晨在安大略号（Ontario）上发生的事。

"所以你看，"我总结道，"这个皮包，我身上所有的东西，都属于一个叫沙利文（Sullivan）的家伙。他可能在事故前就下了车——也许就在谋杀之后。"

"那么——你觉得他犯了那桩——那桩罪行？"她的目光落在烟盒上。

"当然，"我说。"一个人不会半夜穿着别人的衣服从普尔曼车厢跳下去，除非他想逃离什么。除了那把匕首，还有你看到的那些血迹。而且，被害人的皮夹就在我脚下这个旅行包里。这看起来像什么？"

===Original===
I colored when I saw the ghost of a smile hovering around the corners of her mouth. "That is," I finished, "if you care to believe that I am innocent."

The sustaining chain of her small gold bag gave way just then. She did not notice it. I picked it up and slid the trinket into my pocket for safekeeping, where I promptly forgot it. Afterwards I wished I had let it lie unnoticed on the floor of that dirty little suburban car, and even now, when I see a woman carelessly dangling a similar feminine trinket, I shudder involuntarily: there comes back to me the memory of a girl’s puzzled eyes under the brim of a flopping hat, the haunting suspicion of the sleepless nights that followed.

===Chinese===
我看到一丝若有若无的笑意掠过她的嘴角，脸红了。"当然，"我补充道，"前提是你愿意相信我是无辜的。"

她那条小金包的链子就在这时断了。她没有察觉。我捡起来，把那件小首饰塞进口袋保管，随即就忘了它。事后我真希望当时让它默默躺在那辆脏兮兮的小郊区电车的地板上，直到今天，当我看到一个女人随意晃荡着类似的小首饰，我仍会不由自主地打个冷战：记忆里浮现出一个女孩在歪斜帽檐下的困惑眼神，以及随之而来的那些不眠之夜的挥之不去的疑虑。

===Original===
Just then I was determined that my companion should not stray back to the wreck, and to that end I was determinedly facetious.

"Do you know that it is Sunday?" she asked suddenly, "and that we are actually ragged?"

"Never mind that," I retorted. "All Baltimore is divided on Sunday into three parts, those who rise up and go to church, those who rise up and read the newspapers, and those who don’t rise up. The first are somewhere between the creed and the sermon, and we need not worry about the others."

"You treat me like a child," she said almost pettishly. "Don’t try so hard to be cheerful. It—it is almost ghastly."

After that I subsided like a pricked balloon, and the remainder of the ride was made in silence. The information that she would go to friends in the city was a shock: it meant an earlier separation than I had planned for. But my arm was beginning again. In putting her into a cab I struck it and gritted my teeth with the pain. It was probably for that reason that I forgot the gold bag.

She leaned forward and held out her hand. "I may not have another chance to thank you," she said, "and I think I would better not try, anyhow. I cannot tell you how grateful I am." I muttered something about the gratitude being mine: owing to the knock I was seeing two cabs, and two girls were holding out two hands.

"Remember," they were both saying, "you have never met me, Mr. Blakeley. And—if you ever hear anything about me—that is not—pleasant, I want you to think the best you can of me. Will you?"

The two girls were one now, with little flashes of white light playing all around. "I—I’m afraid that I shall think too well for my own good," I said unsteadily. And the cab drove on.

===Chinese===
那时我下定决心不让同伴再回想起那场事故，为此我执意要耍点俏皮。

"你知道今天是星期天吗？"她突然问道，"而且我们这副样子狼狈不堪？"

"别管那个，"我反驳道。"整个巴尔的摩在星期天分成三部分：起身去教堂的人，起身读报纸的人，以及根本不起身的人。第一种人还在信条和布道之间徘徊，其他人我们不必操心。"

"你把我当小孩，"她几乎任性地说。"别那么拼命装开心。这——这简直可怕极了。"

在那之后，我像被扎破的气球一样瘪了下去，剩下的路程在沉默中度过。她说要去城里的朋友家住，这消息令我震惊：这意味着比计划更早的分别。但我的胳膊又开始疼了。扶她上出租车时我撞到了它，痛得咬牙切齿。大概就是因为这个，我忘了那个金包。

她倾身伸出手。"我可能没有机会再谢你了，"她说，"而且我想无论如何最好别谢了。我无法告诉你我有多感激。"我咕哝着说该感激的是我：因为被撞了一下，我眼前出现了两辆出租车，两个女孩伸出了两只手。

"记住，"她们俩都在说，"你从来没有见过我，布莱克利先生。而且——如果你听到任何关于我的事——不那么——愉快，我希望你能往最好处想我。你会吗？"

两个女孩合成了一个，四周闪烁着细碎的白光。"我——恐怕我会想得太好，好到对我自己不利，"我声音颤抖地说。出租车驶远了。

## XI The Name Was Sullivan / 第十一章 那个名字叫沙利文

===Original===
I had my arm done up temporarily in Baltimore and took the next train home. I was pretty far gone when I stumbled out of a cab almost into the scandalized arms of Mrs. Klopton. In fifteen minutes I was in bed, with that good woman piling on blankets and blistering me in unprotected places with hot-water bottles. And in an hour I had a whiff of chloroform and Doctor Williams had set the broken bone.

I dropped asleep then, waking in the late twilight to a realization that I was at home again, without the papers that meant conviction for Andy Bronson, with a charge of murder hanging over my head, and with something more than an impression of the girl my best friend was in love with, a girl moreover who was almost as great an enigma as the crime itself.

===Chinese===
我在巴尔的摩把手臂临时包扎了一下，赶上了下一班火车回家。我下车时已经晕得厉害，踉踉跄跄地从出租车里钻出来，差点一头栽进克洛普顿太太（Mrs. Klopton）惊骇不已的怀抱里。十五分钟后我就躺在了床上，那位好心的女人往我身上堆满毯子，还把热水袋按在我那些没有被护住的地方，烫得我直叫。又过了一个小时，我闻到一股氯仿的味道，威廉斯医生（Doctor Williams）已经把断骨接好了。

我沉沉睡去，在暮色渐浓时醒来，意识到自己又回到了家中——手里没有那份能让安迪·布朗森（Andy Bronson）定罪的文件，头顶上挂着一项谋杀的指控，而且对我那位挚友所倾心的女孩，我的印象远不止朦胧二字——那个女孩本身，几乎和这桩案件一样令人费解。

===Original===
"And I'm no hand at guessing riddles," I groaned half aloud. Mrs. Klopton came over promptly and put a cold cloth on my forehead.

"Euphemia," she said to someone outside the door, "telephone the doctor that he is still rambling, but that he has switched from green ribbons to riddles."

"There's nothing the matter with me, Mrs. Klopton," I rebelled. "I was only thinking out loud. Confound that cloth: it's trickling all over me!" I gave it a fling, and heard it land with a soggy thud on the floor.

"Thinking out loud is delirium," Mrs. Klopton said imperturbably. "A fresh cloth, Euphemia."

===Chinese===
"我可不是猜谜的高手。"我半大声地呻吟道。克洛普顿太太立刻走过来，把一块凉毛巾敷在我的额头上。

"尤菲米娅（Euphemia），"她朝门外的人喊道，"给医生打个电话，说他还在胡言乱语，不过已经从绿丝带换成了猜谜。"

"我什么事也没有，克洛普顿太太，"我抗议道，"我只是在自言自语。该死的毛巾：水都淌我一身了！"我把毛巾一甩，听见它啪嗒一声湿漉漉地摔在了地板上。

"自言自语就是谵妄，"克洛普顿太太不动声色地说，"换块干净的，尤菲米娅。"

===Original===
This time she held it on with a firm pressure that I was too weak to resist. I expostulated feebly that I was drowning, which she also laid to my mental exaltation, and then I finally dropped into a damp sleep. It was probably midnight when I roused again. I had been dreaming of the wreck, and it was inexpressibly comforting to feel the stability of my bed, and to realize the equal stability of Mrs. Klopton, who sat, fully attired, by the night light, reading Science and Health.

"Does that book say anything about opening the windows on a hot night?" I suggested, when I had got my bearings.

She put it down immediately and came over to me. If there is one time when Mrs. Klopton is chastened—and it is the only time—it is when she reads Science and Health. "I don't like to open the shutters, Mr. Lawrence," she explained. "Not since the night you went away."

===Chinese===
这一次她用力按住毛巾，我虚弱得无力反抗。我有气无力地抗议说我要被淹死了，她同样归结为我精神亢奋，最后我终于在一片潮湿中沉沉睡去。大概是午夜时分，我又醒了过来。我梦见了那场海难，而此刻感受到床铺的稳固，体会到克洛普顿太太同样不动如山的存在——她穿戴整齐地坐在夜灯旁，读着《科学与健康》（Science and Health）——这安慰难以言喻。

"那本书里有没有提到大热天该不该开窗户？"等我缓过神来，试探着问道。

她立刻放下书走过来。如果说克洛普顿太太有什么时候会收敛——而且是唯一会收敛的时候——那就是她读《科学与健康》的时候。"自从你走后那天晚上，我就不想打开百叶窗了，劳伦斯先生（Mr. Lawrence），"她解释道。

===Original===
But, pressed further, she refused to explain. "The doctor said you were not to be excited," she persisted. "Here's your beef tea."

"Not a drop until you tell me," I said firmly. "Besides, you know very well there's nothing the matter with me. This arm of mine is only a false belief." I sat up gingerly. "Now—why don't you open that window?"

Mrs. Klopton succumbed. "Because there are queer goings-on in that house next door," she said. "If you will take the beef tea, Mr. Lawrence, I will tell you."

===Chinese===
但我再追问下去，她就拒绝解释了。"医生说你不能激动，"她坚持道，"来，把牛肉汤喝了吧。"

"你不告诉我，我一滴也不喝，"我态度坚决地说，"再说了，你明明知道我什么事也没有。我这条胳膊不过是某种虚假信念罢了。"我小心翼翼地坐起来。"好了——你为什么不打开那扇窗户？"

克洛普顿太太终于让步了。"因为隔壁那栋房子里有怪事发生，"她说，"你要是肯把牛肉汤喝了，劳伦斯先生，我就告诉你。"

===Original===
The queer goings-on, however, proved to be slightly disappointing. It seemed that after I left on Friday night, a light was seen flitting fitfully through the empty house next door. Euphemia had seen it first and called Mrs. Klopton. Together they had watched it breathlessly until it disappeared on the lower floor.

"You should have been a writer of ghost stories," I said, giving my pillows a thump. "And so it was fitting flitfully!"

"That's what it was doing," she reiterated. "Fitting flitfully—I mean flitting fitfully—how you do throw me out, Mr. Lawrence! And what's more, it came again!"

===Chinese===
然而那些怪事听起来却让人有些失望。据说在我周五晚上离开之后，有人看到隔壁空房子里有一道灯光忽明忽暗地闪动。是尤菲米娅最先发现的，她叫来了克洛普顿太太。两人一起屏息凝神地看着那道光，直到它消失在楼下。

"你真该去写鬼故事，"我一拍枕头说，"'fitting flitfully'——闪闪地飘？"

"它就是在那样做，"她反复强调，"fitting flitfully——我是说 flitting fitfully（忽明忽暗地闪动）——劳伦斯先生，你就是这样把我说糊涂的！而且，那灯光后来又出现了！"

===Original===
"Oh, come now, Mrs. Klopton," I objected, "ghosts are like lightning; they never strike twice in the same night. That is only worth half a cup of beef tea."

"You may ask Euphemia," she retorted with dignity. "Not more than an hour after, there was a light there again. We saw it through the chinks of the shutters. Only—this time it began at the lower floor and climbed!"

"You oughtn't to tell ghost stories at night," came McKnight's voice from the doorway. "Really, Mrs. Klopton, I'm amazed at you. You old duffer! I've got you to thank for the worst day of my life."

Mrs. Klopton gulped. Then realizing that the "old duffer" was meant for me, she took her empty cup and went out muttering.

===Chinese===
"得了吧，克洛普顿太太，"我反驳道，"鬼魂跟闪电一样，同一个晚上不会劈两次。这顶多值半碗牛肉汤。"

"你可以去问尤菲米娅，"她义正辞严地回敬道，"不到一个小时，那灯光又出现了。我们透过百叶窗的缝隙看到了。只不过——这一次灯光从楼下开始，一路往上爬！"

"大晚上的可别讲鬼故事，"麦克奈特的声音从门口传来，"说真的，克洛普顿太太，我真佩服你。你这个老糊涂！我这辈子最糟糕的一天，全拜你所赐。"

克洛普顿太太咽了口唾沫。随即意识到那个"老糊涂"说的是我，她端起空杯子，一边嘀咕一边走了出去。

===Original===
"The Pirate's crazy about me, isn't she?" McKnight said to the closing door. Then he swung around and held out his hand.

"By Jove," he said, "I've been laying you out all day, lilies on the doorbell, black gloves, everything. If you had had the sense of a mosquito in a snowstorm, you would have telephoned me."

"I never even thought of it." I was filled with remorse. "Upon my word, Rich, I hadn't an idea beyond getting away from that place. If you had seen what I saw—"

McKnight stopped me. "Seen it! Why, you lunatic, I've been digging for you all day in the ruins! I've lunched and dined on horrors. Give me something to rinse them down, Lollie."

===Chinese===
"这位海盗迷上我了，对吧？"麦克奈特（McKnight）对着正在关上的门说。然后他转过身，伸出手来。

"天哪，"他说，"我一整天都在给你办后事——门铃上挂百合花，戴上黑手套，全套齐备。你哪怕有一点点求生欲——暴风雪中蚊子那种程度也行——就该给我打个电话。"

"我压根没想到。"我满心愧疚，"说真的，里奇（Rich），我当时一门心思只想逃离那个地方。你要是看到我看到的东西——"

麦克奈特打断了我。"看到！你这疯子，我可是在废墟里挖了你一整天！午饭和晚饭都在恐怖中度过。给我弄点什么冲冲吧，劳利（Lollie）。"

===Original===
He had fished the key of the cellarette from its hiding-place in my shoe bag and was mixing himself what he called a Bernard Shaw—a foundation of brandy and soda, with a little of everything else in sight to give it snap. Now that I saw him clearly, he looked weary and grimy. I hated to tell him what I knew he was waiting to hear, but there was no use wading in by inches. I ducked and got it over.

"The notes are gone, Rich," I said, as quietly as I could. In spite of himself his face fell.

"I—of course I expected it," he said. "But—Mrs. Klopton said over the telephone that you had brought home a grip and I hoped—well, Lord knows we ought not to complain. You're here, damaged, but here." He lifted his glass. "Happy days, old man!"

===Chinese===
他从我鞋袋的藏身处摸出了酒柜钥匙，给自己调了一杯他所谓的"萧伯纳"（Bernard Shaw）——白兰地加苏打打底，再把手边能找到的每样东西都往里兑一点，好提提劲。现在看清了他，他看上去疲惫而邋遢。我不忍心告诉他我知道他等着听的话，但拐弯抹角也没用。我心一横，直接说了。

"那些文件不见了，里奇，"我尽量平静地说。他的脸色不由自主地沉了下来。

"我——当然我料到了，"他说，"不过——克洛普顿太太在电话里说你带回来一个旅行箱，我还以为——唉，老天，我们也没什么好抱怨的了。你人在这儿，受了伤，但人还在。"他举起酒杯，"敬好日子，老伙计！"

===Original===
"If you will give me that black bottle and a teaspoon, I'll drink that in arnica, or whatever the stuff is; Rich—the notes were gone before the wreck!"

He wheeled and stared at me, the bottle in his hand. "Lost, strayed or stolen?" he queried with forced lightness.

"Stolen, although I believe the theft was incidental to something else."

===Chinese===
"你把那个黑瓶子和一把茶匙给我，我一口把它灌下去——山金车酊还是什么的；里奇——那些文件在海难之前就已经不见了！"

他猛地转身盯着我，手里还攥着瓶子。"丢了、走失了还是被偷了？"他故作轻松地问道。

"被偷了，不过我认为那次偷窃只是另一桩事的副产品。"

===Original===
Mrs. Klopton came in at that moment, with an eggnog in her hand. She glanced at the clock, and, without addressing anyone in particular, she intimated that it was time for self-respecting folks to be at home in bed. McKnight, who could never resist a fling at her back, spoke to me in a stage whisper.

"Is she talking still? or again?" he asked, just before the door closed. There was a second's indecision with the knob, then, judging discretion the better part, Mrs. Klopton went away.

"Now, then," McKnight said, settling himself in a chair beside the bed, "spit it out. Not the wreck—I know all I want about that. But the theft. I can tell you beforehand that it was a woman."

===Chinese===
就在这时克洛普顿太太端着一碗蛋奶酒进来了。她瞥了一眼钟，不针对任何特定的人，只是暗示说自重的人这时候都该在家睡觉了。麦克奈特从来忍不住要在她背后刺一句，便压着嗓子对我来了个舞台式耳语。

"她还在说？还是又开始了？"就在门关上之前，他问道。门把手犹豫了一秒钟，然后克洛普顿太太权衡之下觉得多一事不如少一事，走了。

"好了，"麦克奈特说着在床边的椅子上坐定，"说吧。海难的事不用说了——我已经知道了。说盗窃。我可以先告诉你，干这事的是个女人。"

===Original===
I had crawled painfully out of bed, and was in the act of pouring the eggnog down the pipe of the washstand. I paused, with the glass in the air.

"A woman!" I repeated, startled. "What makes you think that?"

"You don't know the first principles of a good detective yarn," he said scornfully. "Of course, it was the woman in the empty house next door. You said it was brass pipes, you will remember. Well—on with the dance: let joy be unconfined."

===Chinese===
我忍着痛爬下床，正要把蛋奶酒倒进洗手池的管道里。我举着杯子停住了。

"一个女人！"我吃了一惊，重复道，"你凭什么这么想？"

"你连侦探小说的第一原则都不知道，"他不屑地说，"当然是隔壁空房子里的那个女人。你记得吧，你说过是铜管。好了——舞继续跳吧：让欢乐尽情释放。"

===Original===
So I told the story; I had told it so many times that day that I did it automatically. And I told about the girl with the bronze hair, and my suspicions. But I did not mention Alison West. McKnight listened to the end without interruption. When I had finished he drew a long breath.

"Well!" he said. "That's something of a mess, isn't it? If you can only prove your mild and childlike disposition, they couldn't hold you for the murder—which is a regular ten-twent-thirt crime, anyhow. But the notes—that's different. They are not burned, anyhow. Your man wasn't on the train—therefore, he wasn't in the wreck. If he didn't know what he was taking, as you seem to think, he probably reads the papers, and unless he is a fathead, he's awake by this time to what he's got. He'll try to sell them to Bronson, probably."

"Or to us," I put in.

===Chinese===
于是我便讲了那天的经历；那天我已经讲了太多遍，几乎是机械地复述。我还讲到了那个铜发女孩和我的疑心。但我没有提到艾莉森·韦斯特（Alison West）。麦克奈特一直听到最后，没有打断。等我说完，他长长地呼了一口气。

"行啊！"他说，"这可真是一团乱麻，对吧？你要是能证明你那温和纯真的天性，他们就没法以谋杀罪扣住你——反正那桩案子本来也就是个三流货色。但那些文件就不同了。无论如何它们没被烧掉。你那个家伙不在火车上——所以，他没遇上海难。如果他确实不知道自己拿的是什么——你似乎是这么想的——那他多半会看报纸，而且只要他不是个蠢货，现在早该知道自己手里攥着什么了。他很可能会去找布朗森（Bronson）兜售。"

"或者来找我们，"我插嘴道。

===Original===
We said nothing for a few minutes. McKnight smoked a cigarette and stared at a photograph of Candida over the mantel. Candida is the best pony for a heavy mount in seven states.

"I didn't go to Richmond," he observed finally. The remark followed my own thoughts so closely that I started. "Miss West is not home yet from Seal Harbor."

Receiving no response, he lapsed again into thoughtful silence. Mrs. Klopton came in just as the clock struck one, and made preparation for the night by putting a large gaudy comfortable into an armchair in the dressing-room, with a smaller, stiff-backed chair for her feet. She was wonderfully attired in a dressing-gown that was reminiscent, in parts, of all the ones she had given me for a half dozen Christmases, and she had a purple veil wrapped around her head, to hide Heaven knows what deficiency. She examined the empty eggnog glass, inquired what the evening paper had said about the weather, and then stalked into the dressing-room, and prepared, with much ostentatious creaking, to sit up all night.

===Chinese===
我们沉默了几分钟。麦克奈特抽着一根烟，盯着壁炉上方坎迪达（Candida）的照片。坎迪达是方圆七个州最好的矮种马，驮重能力一流。

"我没去里士满（Richmond），"他终于开口了。这句话恰好击中了我正在想的事，让我吃了一惊。"韦斯特小姐（Miss West）还没有从海豹港（Seal Harbor）回来。"

没有得到回应，他又陷入了沉思。时钟敲响一点的时候克洛普顿太太进来了，为过夜做准备——她把一条花里胡哨的大毛毯铺在梳妆室的扶手椅上，又找了一把靠背硬邦邦的小椅子来搁脚。她穿着一件睡袍，那睡袍的部分面料让人不禁联想到过去六七个圣诞节她送我的那些睡袍的集合体；头上还裹着一圈紫色面纱，天知道要遮住什么缺陷。她检查了空的蛋奶酒碗，问了晚报对天气的预报，然后大步走进梳妆室，伴随着刻意做作的嘎吱声，准备熬个通宵。

===Original===
We fell silent again, while McKnight traced a rough outline of the berths on the white table-cover, and puzzled it out slowly. It was something like this:

"You think he changed the tags on seven and nine, so that when you went back to bed you thought you were crawling into nine, when it was really seven, eh?"

"Probably—yes."

"Then toward morning, when everybody was asleep, your theory is that he changed the numbers again and left the train."

"I can't think of anything else," I replied wearily.

===Chinese===
我们又沉默了，麦克奈特用手指在白色桌布上画出卧铺的大致轮廓，慢慢地推演着。大致是这样的：

"你的意思是，他把七号和九号的标签对调了，所以你回去躺下的时候以为自己爬进了九号铺，其实是七号，对吧？"

"大概——是的。"

"然后快到早晨、大家都睡熟的时候，按你的理论，他又把号码换了回来，然后下了火车。"

"我想不出别的可能了，"我疲惫地答道。

===Original===
"Jove, what a game of bridge that fellow would play! It was like finessing an eight-spot and winning out. They would scarcely have doubted your story had the tags been reversed in the morning. He certainly left you in a bad way. Not a jury in the country would stand out against the stains, the stiletto, and the murdered man's pocketbook in your possession."

"Then you think Sullivan did it?" I asked.

"Of course," said McKnight confidently. "Unless you did it in your sleep. Look at the stains on his pillow, and the dirk stuck into it. And didn't he have the man Harrington's pocketbook?"

===Chinese===
"天哪，这家伙要是打桥牌该多厉害！这简直就是用一张八点牌偷袭成功。要是早上标签已经换回来，他们根本不会怀疑你的说法。他确实把你逼到了绝境。全美国没有哪个陪审团会无视那些血迹、那把匕首和死者钱包在你手上这件事。"

"那你觉得是沙利文干的？"我问。

"那还用说，"麦克奈特信心十足地说，"除非你是梦游杀了人。看看他枕头上那些血迹，还有插在上面的匕首。而且那个哈林顿（Harrington）的钱包不是在他身上吗？"

===Original===
"But why did he go off without the money?" I persisted. "And where does the bronze-haired girl come in?"

"Search me," McKnight retorted flippantly. "Inflammation of the imagination on your part."

"Then there is the piece of telegram. It said lower ten, car seven. It's extremely likely that she had it. That telegram was about me, Richey."

"I'm getting a headache," he said, putting out his cigarette against the sole of his shoe. "All I'm certain of just now is that if there hadn't been a wreck, by this time you'd be sitting in an eight by ten cell, and feeling like the rhyme for it."

===Chinese===
"但他为什么不带钱就走了？"我追问，"那个铜发女孩又算怎么回事？"

"你问我，我问谁，"麦克奈特轻佻地回了一句，"纯粹是你想象力发炎了。"

"还有那截电报纸。上面写着下铺十号，七号车厢。很有可能那截电报在她手里。那份电报说的就是我，里奇。"

"我头疼了，"他把烟头在鞋底上掐灭，说道，"我现在唯一确定的是，要是没出那场海难，这会儿你正蹲在一间八乘十英尺的牢房里，体会着押韵的那种感觉。"

===Original===
"But listen to this," I contended, as he picked up his hat, "this fellow Sullivan is a fugitive, and he's a lot more likely to make advances to Bronson than to us. We could have the case continued, release Bronson on bail and set a watch on him."

"Not my watch," McKnight protested. "It's a family heirloom."

"You'd better go home," I said firmly. "Go home and go to bed. You're sleepy. You can have Sullivan's red necktie to dream over if you think it will help any."

===Chinese===
"但你听我说，"他拿起帽子时我争辩道，"这个沙利文是个逃犯，他去找布朗森比来找我们的可能性大得多。我们把案子申请延期，让布朗森保释出来，然后盯着他。"

"盯他的事别算我，"麦克奈特抗议道，"那可是我的传家宝。"

"你最好回家去，"我语气坚决地说，"回家睡觉。你困了。你要愿意的话，可以把沙利文那条红领带拿去当催眠道具。"

===Original===
Mrs. Klopton's voice came drowsily from the next room, punctuated by a yawn. "Oh, I forgot to tell you," she called, with the suspicious lisp which characterizes her at night, "somebody called up about noon, Mr. Lawrence. It was long distance, and he said he would call again. The name was"—she yawned—"Sullivan."

===Chinese===
克洛普顿太太的声音从隔壁房间昏昏沉沉地传来，中间夹着一个哈欠。"噢，我忘了告诉你，"她喊道，声音带着她夜间特有的那种可疑的齿音，"中午前后有人打过电话，劳伦斯先生。是长途，他说会再打来的。那个人的名字叫——"她打了个哈欠——"沙利文（Sullivan）。"

## XII / 金包

===Original===
I have always smiled at those cases of spontaneous combustion which, like fusing the component parts of a seidlitz powder, unite two people in a bubbling and ephemeral ecstasy. But surely there is possible, with but a single meeting, an attraction so great, a community of mind and interest so strong, that between that first meeting and the next the bond may grow into something stronger. This is especially true, I fancy, of people with temperament, the modern substitute for imagination. It is a nice question whether lovers begin to love when they are together, or when they are apart.

Not that I followed any such line of reasoning at the time. I would not even admit my folly to myself. But during the restless hours of that first night after the accident, when my back ached with lying on it, and any other position was torture, I found my thoughts constantly going back to Alison West. I dropped into a doze, to dream of touching her fingers again to comfort her, and awoke to find I had patted a teaspoonful of medicine out of Mrs. Klopton’s indignant hand. What was it McKnight had said about making an egregious ass of myself?

And that brought me back to Richey, and I fancy I groaned. There is no use expatiating on the friendship between two men who have gone together through college, have quarreled and made it up, fussed together over politics and debated creeds for years: men don’t need to be told, and women can not understand. Nevertheless, I groaned. If it had been anyone but Rich!

===Chinese===
我一直对那些一见钟情、像把赛尔茨泡腾粉的成分搅在一起般在嘶嘶作响中短暂地合二为一的案例嗤之以鼻。但毫无疑问，仅凭一次见面，就可能产生如此强烈的吸引、如此深厚的心灵与兴趣的契合，以至于在初次相遇到下次相见之间，这份纽带能生长为更坚固的东西。我想，对于那些有性情的人——想象力的现代替代品——尤其如此。恋人们究竟是在一起时才开始相爱，还是在分开后才开始相爱，这是个微妙的问题。

当时我并非遵循着这样的推理路线。我甚至不愿对自己承认自己的愚蠢。但在事故后第一个辗转反侧的夜晚，当我的后背因久躺而疼痛、换个姿势又是折磨时，我发现自己的思绪不断地回到艾莉森·韦斯特（Alison West）身上。我打了个盹，梦见再次触碰她的手指以安慰她，醒来时发现我竟从克洛普顿太太愤怒的手中拍掉了一茶匙的药。麦克奈特说我出尽洋相的那番话是怎么说的来着？

这又让我想起了里奇，我想我呻吟了一声。两个一起上大学、吵过架又和好、年复一年为政治争论、为信条辩论的男人之间的友谊，无需赘言：男人们不需要被告知，女人们则无法理解。然而，我还是呻吟了。如果对象不是里奇就好了！

===Original===
Some things were mine, however, and I would hold them: the halcyon breakfast, the queer hat, the pebble in her small shoe, the gold bag with the broken chain—the bag! Why, it was in my pocket at that moment.

I got up painfully and found my coat. Yes, there was the purse, bulging with an opulent suggestion of wealth inside. I went back to bed again, somewhat dizzy, between effort and the touch of the trinket, so lately hers. I held it up by its broken chain and gloated over it. By careful attention to orders, I ought to be out in a day or so. Then—I could return it to her. I really ought to do that: it was valuable, and I wouldn’t care to trust it to the mail. I could run down to Richmond, and see her once—there was no disloyalty to Rich in that.

I had no intention of opening the little bag. I put it under my pillow—which was my reason for refusing to have the linen slips changed, to Mrs. Klopton’s dismay. And sometimes during the morning, while I lay under a virgin field of white, ornamented with strange flowers, my cigarettes hidden beyond discovery, and Science and Health on a table by my elbow, as if by the merest accident, I slid my hand under my pillow and touched it reverently.

===Chinese===
然而，有些东西是我的，我要留住它们：那顿平静的早餐、那顶怪帽子、她小鞋里的石子、那条断了链子的金包——那个包！此刻它就在我口袋里。

我痛苦地爬起来，找到外套。是的，钱包就在那儿，鼓鼓囊囊的，暗示着内里的丰盈。我又躺了回去，有些头晕，一半是因为用力，一半是因为触碰那件不久前还属于她的饰品。我提着断链把包举起来，贪婪地注视着。只要遵照医嘱，我大概一两天就能出院。然后——我就能把它还给她。我真该这么做：这东西很贵重，我不放心托付给邮局。我可以去一趟里士满（Richmond），再见她一次——这算不上对里奇的背叛。

我本没打算打开那个小包。我把它塞在枕头底下——这就是我拒绝换亚麻床单的原因，让克洛普顿太太很不高兴。有时候，上午我躺在洁白的床铺上，被子上点缀着奇怪的花纹，香烟被藏得无影无踪，《科学与健康》被随意地放在肘边的桌上，仿佛纯属偶然，我会把手伸到枕头下，虔诚地触摸它。

===Original===
McKnight came in about eleven. I heard his car at the curb, followed almost immediately by his slam at the front door, and his usual clamor on the stairs. He had a bottle under his arm, rightly surmising that I had been forbidden stimulant, and a large box of cigarettes in his pocket, suspecting my deprivation.

"Well," he said cheerfully. "How did you sleep after keeping me up half the night?"

I slid my hand around: the purse was well covered. "Have it now, or wait till I get the cork out?" he rattled on.

"I don’t want anything," I protested. "I wish you wouldn’t be so darned cheerful, Richey." He stopped whistling to stare at me.

===Chinese===
麦克奈特大约十一点进来的。我听到他的车停在路边，紧接着是他砰地关上正门的声音，以及他惯常的楼梯喧闹。他胳膊下夹着一瓶酒，猜对了我被禁止喝酒，口袋里还有一大盒香烟，料到我被剥夺了这些。

"好了，"他愉快地说。"让我熬了半宿，你睡得怎么样？"

我伸手摸了摸：钱包藏得很好。"现在就喝，还是等我拔了塞子？"他喋喋不休地说。

"我什么都不想喝，"我抗议道。"里奇，你别那么该死的开心好不好。"他停下口哨，瞪着我。

===Original===
"‘I am saddest when I sing!’" he quoted unctuously. "It’s pure reaction, Lollie. Yesterday the sky was low: I was digging for my best friend. Today—he lies before me, his peevish self. Yesterday I thought the notes were burned: today—I look forward to a good cross-country chase, and with luck we will draw." His voice changed suddenly. "Yesterday—she was in Seal Harbor. Today—she is here."

"Here in Washington?" I asked, as naturally as I could.

"Yes. Going to stay a week or two."

"Oh, I had a little hen and she had a wooden leg

And nearly every morning she used to lay an egg—"

"Will you stop that racket, Rich! It’s the real thing this time, I suppose?"

===Chinese===
"‘歌唱时我最悲伤！’"他油腔滑调地引用道。"纯粹是情绪反弹，洛利。昨天天空低沉：我正挖掘着我最好的朋友。今天——他躺在我面前，还是那副暴躁样子。昨天我以为那音符被烧毁了：今天——我期待着一场痛快的越野追逐，运气好的话，我们能打个平手。"他的声音突然变了。"昨天——她在锡尔港（Seal Harbor）。今天——她来了。"

"在华盛顿这儿？"我尽量自然地问。

"是的。要待上一两周。"

"哦，我有只小母鸡，她有条木腿

几乎每天早上她都要下一个蛋——"

"里奇，你能不能别唱了！这次是真的了，我猜？"

===Original===
"She’s the best little chicken that we have on the farm

And another little drink won’t do us any harm—"

he finished, twisting out the corkscrew. Then he came over and sat down on the bed.

"Well," he said judicially, "since you drag it from me, I think perhaps it is. You—you’re such a confirmed woman-hater that I hardly knew how you would take it."

"Nothing of the sort," I denied testily. "Because a man reaches the age of thirty without making maudlin love to every—"

"I’ve taken to long country rides," he went on reflectively, without listening to me, "and yesterday I ran over a sheep; nearly went into the ditch. But there’s a Providence that watches over fools and lovers, and just now I know darned well that I’m one, and I have a sneaking idea I’m both."

"You are both," I said with disgust. "If you can be rational for one moment, I wish you would tell me why that man Sullivan called me over the telephone yesterday morning."

"Probably hadn’t yet discovered the Bronson notes—providing you hold to your theory that the theft was incidental to the murder. May have wanted his own clothes again, or to thank you for yours. Search me: I can’t think of anything else." The doctor came in just then.

===Chinese===
"她是农场上最棒的小母鸡

再来一小杯也不会有啥坏处——"

他唱完，拧开了开瓶器。然后他走过来坐在床边。

"好吧，"他用法官的口吻说，"既然你逼我说，我想也许是真的。你——你一向是个铁杆厌女症患者，我都不知道你会怎么想。"

"根本不是那么回事，"我烦躁地否认。"一个男人到了三十岁还没有对每个女人多愁善感地求爱——"

"我最近迷上了长途乡间兜风，"他若有所思地继续说，没听我说话，"昨天我轧到一只羊；差点冲进沟里。但冥冥之中有上帝保佑傻瓜和恋人，而我现在很确定我就是个傻瓜，而且我隐约觉得我两样都是。"

"你两样都是，"我厌恶地说。"如果你能理性一秒钟，我希望你告诉我，那个沙利文昨天早上为什么打电话给我。"

"大概还没发现布朗森的借据——前提是坚持你的理论，说盗窃是谋杀的附带行为。也许是想要回他自己的衣服，或者感谢你给了他你的。别问我：我想不出别的理由。"就在这时医生进来了。

===Original===
As I said before, I think a lot of my doctor—when I am ill. He is a young man, with an air of breezy self-confidence and good humor. He looked directly past the bottle, which is a very valuable accomplishment, and shook hands with McKnight until I could put the cigarettes under the bedclothes. He had interdicted tobacco. Then he sat down beside the bed and felt around the bandages with hands as gentle as a baby’s.

"Pretty good shape," he said. "How did you sleep?"

"Oh, occasionally," I replied. "I would like to sit up, doctor."

"Nonsense. Take a rest while you have an excuse for it. I wish to thunder I could stay in bed for a day or so. I was up all night."

"Have a drink," McKnight said, pushing over the bottle.

"Twins!" The doctor grinned.

"Have two drinks."

But the medical man refused.

"I wouldn’t even wear a champagne-colored necktie during business hours," he explained. "By the way, I had another case from your accident, Mr. Blakeley, late yesterday afternoon. Under the tongue, please." He stuck a thermometer in my mouth.

===Chinese===
正如我之前所说，我相当敬重我的医生——在我生病的时候。他是个年轻人，带着一种爽朗的自信和幽默感。他目光直接越过那瓶酒——这是一种非常宝贵的本事——跟麦克奈特握手，直到我把香烟藏到被单下面。他曾禁止我抽烟。然后他在床边坐下，用像婴儿一样轻柔的手触摸绷带。

"情况不错，"他说。"睡得怎么样？"

"偶尔睡着，"我回答。"医生，我想坐起来。"

"胡说。趁有借口就多休息。我真希望能卧床一两天。我熬了一整夜。"

"喝一杯，"麦克奈特说着，把酒推过去。

"双胞胎！"医生咧嘴笑了。

"喝两杯。"

但医生拒绝了。

"工作时间我连香槟色领带都不会戴，"他解释道。"对了，昨天傍晚我又接了一个你们那场事故的伤员，布莱克利先生。请张嘴，伸舌头。"他把体温计塞进我嘴里。

===Original===
I had a sudden terrible vision of the amateur detective coming to light, notebook, cheerful impertinence and incriminating data. "A small man?" I demanded, "gray hair—"

"Keep your mouth closed," the doctor said peremptorily. "No. A woman, with a fractured skull. Beautiful case. Van Kirk was up to his eyes and sent for me. Hemorrhage, right-sided paralysis, irregular pupils—all the trimmings. Worked for two hours."

"Did she recover?" McKnight put in. He was examining the doctor with a new awe.

"She lifted her right arm before I left," the doctor finished cheerily, "so the operation was a success, even if she should die."

"Good Heavens," McKnight broke in, "and I thought you were just an ordinary mortal, like the rest of us! Let me touch you for luck. Was she pretty?"

"Yes, and young. Had a wealth of bronze-colored hair. Upon my soul, I hated to cut it."

McKnight and I exchanged glances.

"Do you know her name, doctor?" I asked.

"No. The nurses said her clothes came from a Pittsburg tailor."

"She is not conscious, I suppose?"

"No; she may be, tomorrow—or in a week."

===Chinese===
我突然恐怖地预见到了业余侦探即将曝光——笔记本、乐天的厚颜无耻以及确凿的罪证。"是个小个子男人？"我追问，"灰白头发——"

"闭嘴，"医生命令道。"不是。是个女人，颅骨骨折。漂亮的病例。范柯克（Van Kirk）忙不过来，把我叫去了。出血、右侧瘫痪、瞳孔不等——全套症状。干了两个小时。"

"她醒过来了吗？"麦克奈特插嘴道。他用一种新的敬畏目光打量着医生。

"我走之前她抬起了右臂，"医生愉快地总结道，"所以手术是成功的，即使她最终死了。"

"天哪，"麦克奈特打断道，"我还以为你跟我们一样只是个凡人呢！让我沾沾你的福气。她漂亮吗？"

"是的，而且年轻。有一头浓密的古铜色头发。说实话，我都不忍心剪掉它。"

麦克奈特和我交换了一个眼神。

"医生，你知道她叫什么名字吗？"我问。

"不知道。护士说她的衣服是匹兹堡（Pittsburg）一家裁缝店的。"

"她还没醒，我猜？"

"没有；也许明天——或者一周后。"

===Original===
He looked at the thermometer, murmured something about liquid diet, avoiding my eye—Mrs. Klopton was broiling a chop at the time—and took his departure, humming cheerfully as he went downstairs. McKnight looked after him wistfully.

"Jove, I wish I had his constitution," he exclaimed. "Neither nerves nor heart! What a chauffeur he would make!"

But I was serious.

"I have an idea," I said grimly, "that this small matter of the murder is going to come up again, and that your uncle will be in the deuce of a fix if it does. If that woman is going to die, somebody ought to be around to take her deposition. She knows a lot, if she didn’t do it herself. I wish you would go down to the telephone and get the hospital. Find out her name, and if she is conscious."

McKnight went under protest. "I haven’t much time," he said, looking at his watch. "I’m to meet Mrs. West and Alison at one. I want you to know them, Lollie. You would like the mother."

"Why not the daughter?" I inquired. I touched the little gold bag under the pillow.
