"Well," he said judicially, "you’ve always declared against the immaturity and romantic nonsense of very young women—"

"I never said anything of the sort," I retorted furiously.

===Chinese===
他看了看体温计，嘟囔着什么流质饮食，避开我的目光——当时克洛普顿太太正在烤牛排——然后告辞了，下楼时愉快地哼着歌。麦克奈特恋恋不舍地看着他的背影。

"天哪，我真希望我有他那样的体格，"他感叹道。"既不神经衰弱，也没有心脏病！他会是个多棒的司机啊！"

但我很严肃。

"我有个想法，"我阴沉地说，"这场小小的谋杀案还会浮出水面，如果你叔叔真陷进去了，处境会很糟糕。如果那女人要死了，应该有人去录她的口供。她知道很多事，即使不是她自己干的。我希望你去楼下打电话给医院。查查她的名字，还有她是否清醒。"

麦克奈特不情愿地去了。"我没多少时间，"他看着表说。"我一点钟要和韦斯特太太（Mrs. West）以及艾莉森（Alison）见面。我想让你认识她们，洛利。你会喜欢那位母亲的。"

"为什么不喜欢女儿？"我问道。我碰了碰枕头下的小金包。

"嗯，"他用法官的口吻说，"你一向声称反对年轻女子的不成熟和浪漫胡闹——"

"我从没说过那种话，"我愤怒地反驳。

===Original===
"‘There is more satisfaction to be had out of a good saddle horse!’" he quoted me. "‘More excitement out of a polo pony, and as for the eternal matrimonial chase, give me instead a good stubble, a fox, some decent hounds and a hunter, and I’ll show you the real joys of the chase!’"

"For Heaven’s sake, go down to the telephone, you make my head ache," I said savagely.

I hardly know what prompted me to take out the gold purse and look at it. It was an imbecile thing to do—call it impulse, sentimentality, what you wish. I brought it out, one eye on the door, for Mrs. Klopton has a ready eye and a noiseless shoe. But the house was quiet. Downstairs McKnight was flirting with the telephone central and there was an odor of boneset tea in the air. I think Mrs. Klopton was fascinated out of her theories by the "boneset" in connection with the fractured arm.

Anyhow, I held up the bag and looked at it. It must have been unfastened, for the next instant there was an avalanche on the snowfield of the counterpane—some money, a wisp of a handkerchief, a tiny booklet with thin pages, covered with a powdery substance—and a necklace. I drew myself up slowly and stared at the necklace.

===Chinese===
"‘好鞍马带来的满足感更多！’"他引用我的话说。"‘马球矮种马带来的刺激更多，至于那永恒的婚姻追逐，不如给我一片好荒地、一只狐狸、几条像样的猎犬和一匹好猎马，我让你见识追猎的真正乐趣！’"

"看在老天份上，去楼下打电话吧，你让我头疼，"我恶狠狠地说。

我也不知道是什么促使我掏出金包来看。这是件蠢事——随你怎么叫它，冲动也好，多愁善感也好。我把它拿出来，眼睛瞟着门口，因为克洛普顿太太眼尖脚轻。但屋里很安静。楼下麦克奈特正在跟电话总机调情，空气中弥漫着佩兰茶的气味。我想克洛普顿太太是被"佩兰"跟骨折联系在一起的说辞迷住了，不再坚持她的理论了。

不管怎样，我举起包端详着。它一定是没扣好，因为下一秒钟，包里的东西就像雪崩一样洒落在床单的雪原上——一些钱、一方手帕、一本薄薄的小册子，上面覆盖着粉末状的东西——还有一条项链。我慢慢地坐直身子，盯着那条项链。

===Original===
It was one of the semi-barbaric affairs that women are wearing now, a heavy pendant of gold chains and carved cameos, swung from a thin neck chain of the same metal. The necklace was broken: in three places the links were pulled apart and the cameos swung loose and partly detached. But it was the supporting chain that held my eye and fascinated with its sinister suggestion. Three inches of it had been snapped off, and as well as I knew anything on earth, I knew that the bit of chain that the amateur detective had found, bloodstain and all, belonged just there.

And there was no one I could talk to about it, no one to tell me how hideously absurd it was, no one to give me a slap and tell me there are tons of fine gold chains made every year, or to point out the long arm of coincidence!

With my one useful hand I fumbled the things back into the bag and thrust it deep out of sight among the pillows. Then I lay back in a cold perspiration. What connection had Alison West with this crime? Why had she stared so at the gunmetal cigarette case that morning on the train? What had alarmed her so at the farmhouse? What had she taken back to the gate? Why did she wish she had not escaped from the wreck? And last, in Heaven’s name, how did a part of her necklace become torn off and covered with blood?

===Chinese===
这是一条如今女人们常戴的半野蛮风格的项链，沉重的吊坠由金链和雕刻的浮雕组成，挂在一条纤细的同材质颈链上。项链断了：有三处链节被拉开，浮雕松动并部分脱落。但吸引我目光并以其不祥暗示令人着迷的是那条支撑链。三英寸长的链子被扯断了，我敢像了解世上任何事一样确定，业余侦探发现的那段带血的链条，就属于那里。

而我找不到任何人可以倾诉，没人能告诉我这有多荒谬绝伦，没人能扇我一巴掌告诉我每年生产成吨的细金链，没人能指出巧合之手何其漫长！

我用那只尚能活动的手，摸索着把东西塞回包里，再把它深深塞到枕头堆里看不见的地方。然后我躺了回去，出了一身冷汗。艾莉森·韦斯特跟这桩罪行有什么关系？那天早上在火车上，她为什么那么死盯着那个枪铜色的烟盒？在农舍里是什么把她吓成那样？她回到门口去系了什么？为什么她希望自己没能逃出事故现场？最后，老天作证，她项链的一部分怎么会断裂并且沾上了血？

===Original===
Downstairs McKnight was still at the telephone, and amusing himself with Mrs. Klopton in the interval of waiting.

"Why did he come home in a gray suit, when he went away in a blue?" he repeated. "Well, wrecks are queer things, Mrs. Klopton. The suit may have turned gray with fright. Or perhaps wrecks do as queer stunts as lightning. Friend of mine once was struck by lightning; he and the caddy had taken refuge under a tree. After the flash, when they recovered consciousness, there was my friend in the caddy’s clothes, and the caddy in his. And as my friend was a large man and the caddy a very small boy—"

McKnight’s story was interrupted by the indignant slam of the dining-room door. He was obliged to wait some time, and even his eternal cheerfulness was ebbing when he finally got the hospital.

"Is Doctor Van Kirk there?" he asked. "Not there? Well, can you tell me how the patient is whom Doctor Williams, from Washington, operated on last night? Well, I’m glad of that. Is she conscious? Do you happen to know her name? Yes, I’ll hold the line." There was a long pause, then McKnight’s voice:

"Hello—yes. Thank you very much. Goodbye."

He came upstairs, two steps at a time.

"Look here," he said, bursting into the room, "there may be something in your theory, after all. The woman’s name—it may be a coincidence, but it’s curious—her name is Sullivan."

"What did I tell you?" I said, sitting up suddenly in bed. "She’s probably a sister of that scoundrel in lower seven, and she was afraid of what he might do."

===Chinese===
楼下麦克奈特还在打电话，在等待的间隙里跟克洛普顿太太逗趣。

"他穿蓝西装出门，怎么穿灰西装回来了？"他重复道。"嗯，事故这种事真是古怪，克洛普顿太太。那套衣服可能是吓灰的。也许事故能像闪电一样变出古怪的把戏。我一个朋友被雷劈过；他和球童躲到一棵树下。闪电过后他们恢复知觉时，我朋友穿着球童的衣服，球童穿着他的衣服。而我朋友是个大块头，球童只是个小男孩——"

麦克奈特的故事被愤怒的餐厅门的砰然关上声打断了。他不得不等了一段时间，当他终于联系上医院时，连他那永恒的乐观也在消退。

"范柯克医生在吗？"他问。"不在？好吧，你能告诉我昨晚华盛顿来的威廉姆斯医生（Doctor Williams）动手术的那个病人情况如何？嗯，我很高兴听到这个。她清醒了吗？你碰巧知道她名字吗？好的，我在线等着。"停顿了很长时间，然后是麦克奈特的声音：

"喂——是的。非常感谢。再见。"

他两步并作一步地上了楼。

"听着，"他冲进房间说，"你的理论也许真有点道理。那个女人的名字——也许是巧合，但很奇怪——她姓沙利文（Sullivan）。"

"我跟你说什么来着？"我在床上猛地坐起来。"她大概就是下铺七号那个恶棍的妹妹，她害怕他可能干出什么事来。"

===Original===
"Well, I’ll go there some day soon. She’s not conscious yet. In the meantime, the only thing I can do is to keep an eye, through a detective, on the people who try to approach Bronson. We’ll have the case continued, anyhow, in the hope that the stolen notes will sooner or later turn up."

"Confound this arm," I said, paying for my energy with some excruciating throbs. "There’s so much to be looked after, and here I am, bandaged, splinted, and generally useless. It’s a beastly shame."

"Don’t forget that I am here," said McKnight pompously. "And another thing, when you feel this way just remember there are two less desirable places where you might be. One is jail, and the other is—" He strummed on an imaginary harp, with devotional eyes.

But McKnight’s lightheartedness jarred on me that morning. I lay and frowned under my helplessness. When by chance I touched the little gold bag, it seemed to scorch my fingers. Richey, finding me unresponsive, left to keep his luncheon engagement with Alison West. As he clattered down the stairs, I turned my back to the morning sunshine and abandoned myself to misery. By what strain on her frayed nerves was Alison West keeping up, I wondered? Under the circumstances, would I dare to return the bag? Knowing that I had it, would she hate me for my knowledge? Or had I exaggerated the importance of the necklace, and in that case had she forgotten me already?

But McKnight had not gone, after all. I heard him coming back, his voice preceding him, and I groaned with irritation.

"Wake up!" he called. "Somebody’s sent you a lot of flowers. Please hold the box, Mrs. Klopton; I’m going out to be run down by an automobile."

===Chinese===
"好吧，我过几天会去看她。她还没醒。眼下我能做的，就是通过侦探盯着那些试图接近布朗森的人。无论如何，我们会申请延期审理，希望那些被盗的借据迟早能出现。"

"这胳膊真该死，"我说，为自己的逞强付出了几阵剧痛的代价。"有那么多事要处理，而我却缠着绷带、打着夹板，基本是个废人。真他妈的窝囊。"

"别忘了还有我呢，"麦克奈特浮夸地说。"还有一件事，当你有这种感觉时，记住你可能身处两个更糟的地方。一个是监狱，另一个是——"他用眼睛做出虔诚的表情，手指在想象中的竖琴上拨弄。

但麦克奈特的轻松愉快那天早上让我心烦。我躺着，为自己的无能而皱眉。偶然碰到那个小金包时，它似乎灼伤了我的手指。里奇见我毫无反应，便离开去赴与艾莉森·韦斯特的午餐约了。他下楼的咔嗒声远去时，我背对着晨光，沉入了痛苦之中。艾莉森·韦斯特在怎样的神经紧绷下硬撑着，我暗自思忖？在这种情形下，我敢把包还给她吗？她知道我拿着它，会因为我的知情而恨我吗？还是我夸大了那条项链的重要性，果真如此的话，她已经把我忘了？

但麦克奈特终究还没走。我听到他回来了，声音先于人到，我恼怒地呻吟了一声。

"醒醒！"他喊道。"有人送了你一大盒花。克洛普顿太太，请帮我捧着盒子；我要出去让汽车撞一下。"

===Original===
I roused to feeble interest. My brother’s wife is punctilious about such things; all the new babies in the family have silver rattles, and all the sick people flowers.

McKnight pulled up an armful of roses, and held them out to me.

"Wonder who they’re from?" he said, fumbling in the box for a card. "There’s no name—yes, here’s one."

He held it up and read it with exasperating slowness.

"‘Best wishes for an early recovery.

A Companion in Misfortune.’

"Well, what do you know about that!" he exclaimed. "That’s something you didn’t tell me, Lollie."

"It was hardly worth mentioning," I said mendaciously, with my heart beating until I could hear it. She had not forgotten, after all.

McKnight took a bud and fastened it in his buttonhole. I’m afraid I was not especially pleasant about it. They were her roses, and anyhow, they were meant for me. Richey left very soon, with an irritating final grin at the box.

"Goodbye, sir woman-hater," he jeered at me from the door.

So he wore one of the roses she had sent me, to luncheon with her, and I lay back among my pillows and tried to remember that it was his game, anyhow, and that I wasn’t even drawing cards. To remember that, and to forget the broken necklace under my head!

===Chinese===
我勉强提起一点兴趣。我嫂子对这类事一丝不苟；家族里所有新生婴儿都有银拨浪鼓，所有病人都有鲜花。

麦克奈特抱起一大捧玫瑰，递给我。

"猜猜是谁送的？"他说着，在盒子里翻找名片。"没有名字——不，这里有一张。"

他举起来，以令人恼火的慢速念道：

"‘祝早日康复。

一位不幸的同行。’

"好家伙，你有什么要说的！"他惊叫道。"洛利，这事你可没告诉我。"

"不值一提，"我撒谎道，心跳得自己都能听见。她毕竟没有忘记。

麦克奈特摘下一朵花蕾别在纽扣孔里。我恐怕对此并不怎么友善。那是她的玫瑰，而且无论如何，是送给我的。里奇很快就走了，临走时还对着盒子令人恼火地咧嘴一笑。

"再见，厌女症先生，"他在门口嘲笑道。

于是他戴着一朵她送给我的玫瑰，去跟她共进午餐，而我仰卧在枕头堆里，试图记住那终究是他的游戏，而我甚至连抽牌的资格都没有。要记住这个，并且忘掉我脑袋下面那条断裂的项链！

## XIII / 枯萎的玫瑰

===Original===
I was in the house for a week. Much of that time I spent in composing and destroying letters of thanks to Miss West, and in growling at the doctor. McKnight dropped in daily, but he was less cheerful than usual. Now and then I caught him eying me as if he had something to say, but whatever it was he kept it to himself. Once during the week he went to Baltimore and saw the woman in the hospital there. From the description I had little difficulty in recognizing the young woman who had been with the murdered man in Pittsburg. But she was still unconscious. An elderly aunt had appeared, a gaunt person in black, who sat around like a buzzard on a fence, according to McKnight, and wept, in a mixed figure, into a damp handkerchief.

===Chinese===
我在家里待了整整一个星期。其中大部分时间，我都花在给韦斯特小姐（Miss West）写感谢信然后又揉掉，以及对医生发脾气上。麦克奈特（McKnight）天天来，但没平时那么开朗。我偶尔撞见他用一种若有所思的眼神打量我，像是有话要说，可到底什么话他始终憋着。这周里他去了一趟巴尔的摩（Baltimore），探望了住院的那个女人。听他一描述，我毫不费力就认出了——那就是在匹兹堡（Pittsburg）跟死者在一起的年轻女人。不过她仍然昏迷不醒。她一位上了年纪的姑妈赶来照看，据麦克奈特说，是个穿黑衣的瘦削女人，像秃鹫蹲在篱笆上一样在病房里干坐着，哭的时候——用一个四不像的比喻——哭成一团湿手帕。

===Original===
On the last day of my imprisonment he stopped in to thrash out a case that was coming up in court the next day, and to play a game of double solitaire with me.

"Who won the ball game?" I asked.

"We were licked. Ask me something pleasant. Oh, by the way, Bronson's out today."

"I'm glad I'm not on his bond," I said pessimistically. "He'll clear out."

"Not he." McKnight pounced on my ace. "He's no fool. Don't you suppose he knows you took those notes to Pittsburg? The papers were full of it. And he knows you escaped with your life and a broken arm from the wreck. What do we do next? The Commonwealth continues the case. A deaf man on a dark night would know those notes are missing."

===Chinese===
禁足的最后一天，他来坐了坐，先跟我掰扯了一桩第二天就要开庭的案子，然后跟我打了一局双人纸牌接龙。

"棒球赛谁赢了？"我问。

"我们被收拾了。问点让人高兴的事。哦对了，布朗森（Bronson）今天出来了。"

"我庆幸自己没给他做担保，"我悲观地说，"他肯定会跑路。"

"才不会。"麦克奈特一把压住了我的尖子。"他可不傻。你以为他不知道你把那几张票据带去了匹兹堡？报纸上铺天盖地。而且他知道你从那场车祸里捡回了一条命，外加一条断掉的胳膊。接下来怎么办？案子还在追查。大晚上哪怕是个聋子都能听出那几张票据不见了。"

===Original===
"Don't play so fast," I remonstrated. "I have only one arm to your two. Who is trailing Bronson? Did you try to get Johnson?"

"I asked for him, but he had some work on hand."

"The murder's evidently a dead issue," I reflected. "No, I'm not joking. The wreck destroyed all the evidence. But I'm firmly convinced those notes will be offered, either to us or to Bronson very soon. Johnson's a blackguard, but he's a good detective. He could make his fortune as a game dog. What's he doing?"

McKnight put down his cards, and rising, went to the window. As he held the curtain back his customary grin looked a little forced.

"To tell you the truth, Lollie," he said, "for the last two days he has been watching a well-known Washington attorney named Lawrence Blakeley. He's across the street now."

===Chinese===
"别出那么快，"我抗议道，"我只有一条胳膊，你有两条。谁在盯着布朗森？你试过找约翰逊（Johnson）吗？"

"我请他了，他手头有事脱不开。"

"谋杀案显然是死案了，"我自言自语。"不，我没开玩笑。车祸把所有证据都毁了。但我敢断定那几张票据很快就会露面——要么送到我们手上，要么落到布朗森手里。约翰逊是个无赖，可他是个好侦探。他要是去当猎犬，能发大财。他在干什么？"

麦克奈特放下牌，起身走到窗边。他掀开窗帘时，惯常的那副嬉皮笑脸看着有点僵硬。

"跟你说实话吧，洛利（Lollie），"他说，"这两天他一直在盯梢一位华盛顿名律师，叫劳伦斯·布莱克利（Lawrence Blakeley）。他就住在街对面。"

===Original===
It took a moment for me to grasp what he meant.

"Why, it's ridiculous," I asserted. "What would they trail me for? Go over and tell Johnson to get out of there, or I'll pot at him with my revolver."

"You can tell him that yourself." McKnight paused and bent forward. "Hello, here's a visitor; little man with string halt."

"I won't see him," I said firmly. "I've been bothered enough with reporters."

We listened together to Mrs. Klopton's expostulating tones in the lower hall and the creak of the boards as she came heavily up the stairs. She had a piece of paper in her hand torn from a pocket account-book, and on it was the name, "Mr. Wilson Budd Hotchkiss. Important business."

"Oh, well, show him up," I said resignedly. "You'd better put those cards away, Richey. I fancy it's the rector of the church around the corner."

===Chinese===
我愣了一下才明白他什么意思。

"胡扯，"我断然否定，"他们跟踪我干什么？你过去告诉约翰逊走人，不然我拿左轮手枪打他。"

"你自己跟他说吧。"麦克奈特顿了顿，俯身向前，"哟，来客人了；一个走路一瘸一拐的小个子。"

"我不见，"我语气坚定，"记者已经够烦我了。"

我们俩一起听着克洛普顿太太（Mrs. Klopton）在楼下门厅里的抗议声，还有她沉甸甸地上楼时木地板吱嘎作响。她手里捏着一张从便携账簿上撕下来的纸条，上面写着"威尔逊·巴德·霍奇基斯先生（Mr. Wilson Budd Hotchkiss）。重要事务。"

"行吧，请他上来，"我认命了。"你最好把牌收了，里奇（Richey）。我猜是街角教堂的牧师。"

===Original===
But when the door opened to admit a curiously alert little man, adjusting his glasses with nervous fingers, my face must have shown my dismay.

It was the amateur detective of the Ontario!

I shook hands without enthusiasm. Here was the one survivor of the wrecked car who could do me any amount of harm. There was no hope that he had forgotten any of the incriminating details. In fact, he held in his hand the very notebook which contained them.

His manner was restrained, but it was evident he was highly excited. I introduced him to McKnight, who has the imagination I lack, and who placed him at once, mentally.

"I only learned yesterday that you had been—er—saved," he said rapidly. "Terrible accident—unspeakable. Dream about it all night and think about it all day. Broken arm?"

===Chinese===
但当门打开，放进来一个眼神异常锐利的小个子男人，用紧张的手指正了正眼镜时，我脸上一定露出了惊慌。

他就是"安大略号"（Ontario）上的那位业余侦探！

我跟他握了握手，毫无热情。这是那节失事车厢里唯一一个能把我害惨的幸存者。指望他已经忘了那些足以给我定罪的细节，那是白日做梦。事实上，他手里正拿着那个记满这些细节的笔记本。

他举止收敛，但显然异常兴奋。我把他介绍给麦克奈特——他拥有我欠缺的想象力——麦克奈特立刻就在脑子里给他对上了号。

"我昨天才知道你——呃——被救了，"他语速飞快，"太可怕了——惨不忍睹。整晚做噩梦，白天也一直在想。胳膊断了？"

===Original===
"No. He just wears the splint to be different from other people," McKnight drawled lazily. I glared at him: there was nothing to be gained by antagonizing the little man.

"Yes, a fractured humerus, which isn't as funny as it sounds."

"Humerus—humorous! Pretty good," he cackled. "I must say you keep up your spirits pretty well, considering everything."

"You seem to have escaped injury," I parried. He was fumbling for something in his pockets.

"Yes, I escaped," he replied abstractedly. "Remarkable thing, too. I haven't a doubt I would have broken my neck, but I landed on—you'll never guess what! I landed head first on the very pillow which was under inspection at the time of the wreck. You remember, don't you? Where did I put that package?"

He found it finally and opened it on a table, displaying with some theatricalism a rectangular piece of muslin and a similar patch of striped ticking.

===Chinese===
"没有。他只是夹着夹板好跟别人区分开，"麦克奈特懒洋洋地拖着腔调说。我瞪了他一眼——得罪这个小个子可没半点好处。

"是肱骨骨折，听着没那么搞笑。"

"肱骨（humerus）——幽默（humorous）！妙极了，"他咯咯笑起来。"我得说你心态真不错，经历了这些还能保持乐观。"

"你倒是毫发无伤，"我绕开话题。他在口袋里翻找什么东西。

"是的，我没事，"他心不在焉地回答。"说来也是奇事。我毫不怀疑当时我脖子都会摔断，结果我落下来——你绝对猜不到！我脑袋朝下，正好落在了车祸发生时正在被检查的那只枕头上。你记得吧？我那个包裹放哪儿了？"

他终于找到了，搁在桌上打开来，颇有些戏剧性地展示出一块长方形的白棉布和一块同样形状的条纹布垫。

===Original===
"You recognize it?" he said. "The stains, you see, and the hole made by the dirk. I tried to bring away the entire pillow, but they thought I was stealing it, and made me give it up."

Richey touched the pieces gingerly. "By George," he said, "and you carry that around in your pocket! What if you should mistake it for your handkerchief?"

But Mr. Hotchkiss was not listening. He stood bent somewhat forward, leaning over the table, and fixed me with his ferret-like eyes.

"Have you seen the evening papers, Mr. Blakeley?" he inquired.

I glanced to where they lay unopened, and shook my head.

"Then I have a disagreeable task," he said with evident relish. "Of course, you had considered the matter of the man Harrington's death closed, after the wreck. I did myself. As far as I was concerned, I meant to let it remain so. There were no other survivors, at least none that I knew of, and in spite of circumstances, there were a number of points in your favor."

===Chinese===
"你认出来了吗？"他说，"你看，血迹，还有匕首扎出来的洞。我本来想把整个枕头带走，可他们以为我是偷东西，硬让我交了出来。"

里奇小心翼翼地碰了碰那两块布片。"老天，"他说，"你就这么揣在兜里到处走？万一你把它当成手帕怎么办？"

但霍奇基斯先生根本没在听。他微微弓着身子，俯在桌上，用那双鼬鼠般的眼睛直盯着我。

"您看过今晚的报纸了吗，布莱克利先生？"他问道。

我瞥了一眼桌上没拆封的报纸，摇了摇头。

"那我有件不太愉快的事要告知您，"他明显带着幸灾乐祸的口吻说，"当然，车祸之后您大概已经认定哈林顿（Harrington）先生的死是个了结了。我自己也这么想。就我个人而言，我本打算让事情就这么过去。没有其他幸存者了——至少据我所知没有——而且尽管情势不利，还是有不少对您有利的地方。"

===Original===
"Thank you," I put in with a sarcasm that was lost on him.

"I verified your identity, for instance, as soon as I recovered from the shock. Also—I found on inquiring of your tailor that you invariably wore dark clothing."

McKnight came forward threateningly. "Who are you, anyhow?" he demanded. "And how is this any business of yours?" Mr. Hotchkiss was entirely unruffled.

"I have a minor position here," he said, reaching for a visiting card. "I am a very small patch on the seat of government, sir."

McKnight muttered something about certain offensive designs against the said patch and retired grumbling to the window. Our visitor was opening the paper with a tremendous expenditure of energy.

"Here it is. Listen." He read rapidly aloud:

"The Pittsburg police have sent to Baltimore two detectives who are looking up the survivors of the ill-fated Washington Flier. It has transpired that Simon Harrington, the Wood Street merchant of that city, was not killed in the wreck, but was murdered in his berth the night preceding the accident. Shortly before the collision, John Flanders, the conductor of the Flier, sent this telegram to the chief of police:

===Chinese===
"多谢，"我带刺地插了一句，可惜他完全没听出来。

"比如说，我一从惊吓中缓过神来，就核实了您的身份。另外——我向您的裁缝打听了一下，得知您向来只穿深色衣服。"

麦克奈特气势汹汹地走上前。"你到底是什么人？"他质问道，"这跟你有什么关系？"霍奇基斯先生完全不为所动。

"鄙人在此地有个小差事，"他说着伸手去掏名片，"鄙人不过是政府座椅上一块很小的补丁，先生。"

麦克奈特嘟囔了几句要对那块补丁施以何种冒犯性手段的话，骂骂咧咧地退回了窗边。我们的来客正以惊人的力气拆报纸。

"找到了。听好了。"他飞快地朗声念道：

"匹兹堡警方已派遣两名侦探前往巴尔的摩，调查不幸的'华盛顿号特快'（Washington Flier）失事车厢的幸存者。据查实，该市木材街商人西蒙·哈林顿（Simon Harrington）并非死于车祸，而是在事故发生前一晚被人刺杀于铺位。列车相撞前不久，'华盛顿号特快'列车长约翰·弗兰德斯（John Flanders）给警察局长发了这样一封电报：

===Original===
"'Body of Simon Harrington found stabbed in his berth, lower ten, Ontario, at six-thirty this morning. John Flanders, Conductor.'

"It is hoped that the survivors of the wrecked car Ontario will be found, to tell what they know of the discovery of the crime.

"Mr. John Gilmore, head of the steel company for which Mr. Harrington was purchasing agent, has signified his intention of sifting the matter to the bottom."

"So you see," Hotchkiss concluded, "there's trouble brewing. You and I are the only survivors of that unfortunate car."

I did not contradict him, but I knew of two others, at least: Alison West, and the woman we had left beside the road that morning, babbling incoherently, her black hair tumbling over her white face.

"Unless we can find the man who occupied lower seven," I suggested.

===Chinese===
"'今晨六时三十分，发现西蒙·哈林顿遗体于安大略号下铺十号，系遭刺杀身亡。列车长约翰·弗兰德斯。'

"望寻获安大略号失事车厢之幸存者，以查明发现案情之经过。

"哈林顿先生生前效力之钢铁公司负责人约翰·吉尔摩先生（John Gilmore）已表示将彻查此案。"

"所以您看，"霍奇基斯总结道，"麻烦来了。您和我是那节不幸车厢里仅有的幸存者。"

我没有反驳他，但我知道至少还有另外两个：艾莉森·韦斯特（Alison West），以及那天早晨被我们留在路边、语无伦次地喃喃自语、黑发散落在苍白脸庞上的那个女人。

"除非我们能找到住在下铺七号的那个人，"我提议道。

===Original===
"I have already tried and failed. To find him would not clear you, of course, unless we could establish some connection between him and the murdered man. It is the only thing I see, however. I have learned this much," Hotchkiss concluded: "Lower seven was reserved from Cresson."

Cresson! Where Alison West and Mrs. Curtis had taken the train!

McKnight came forward and suddenly held out his hand. "Mr. Hotchkiss," he said, "I—I'm sorry if I have been offensive. I thought when you came in, that, like the Irishman and the government, you were 'forninst' us. If you will put those cheerful relics out of sight somewhere, I should be glad to have you dine with me at the Incubator." (His name for his bachelor apartment.) "Compared with Johnson, you are the great original protoplasm."

The strength of this was lost on Hotchkiss, but the invitation was clear. They went out together, and from my window I watched them get into McKnight's car. It was raining, and at the corner the Cannonball skidded. Across the street my detective, Johnson, looked after them with his crooked smile. As he turned up his collar he saw me, and lifted his hat.

===Chinese===
"我已经试过了，没找到。找到他也不能替您洗清嫌疑，除非我们能证明他和死者之间有关联。但眼下我只看到这一条路。还有一件事我查到了，"霍奇基斯最后说道，"下铺七号是从克雷森（Cresson）上的车。"

克雷森！艾莉森·韦斯特和柯蒂斯太太（Mrs. Curtis）就是从那里上的车！

麦克奈特走上前，突然伸出了手。"霍奇基斯先生，"他说，"我——如果我刚才有冒犯之处，我道歉。你一进门，我还以为你像爱尔兰人说政府那样，是冲着咱们来的。你要是能把那些乐呵呵的遗物塞到看不见的地方去，我很乐意请你到我的'孵化器'（Incubator）共进晚餐。"——这是他对自己单身公寓的戏称。"跟约翰逊一比，你简直就是原初生命原生质。"

霍奇基斯没听懂这个比喻，但邀请本身是清楚的。两人一起出了门。我从窗口看着他们上了麦克奈特的车。天在下雨，车到了拐弯处打了个滑。街对面，我的侦探约翰逊歪着嘴笑了笑，目送他们离去。他竖起衣领时看到了我，举了举帽子。

===Original===
I left the window and sat down in the growing dusk. So the occupant of lower seven had got on the car at Cresson, probably with Alison West and her companion. There was someone she cared about enough to shield. I went irritably to the door and summoned Mrs. Klopton.

"You may throw out those roses," I said without looking at her. "They are quite dead."

"They have been quite dead for three days," she retorted spitefully. "Euphemia said you threatened to dismiss her if she touched them."

===Chinese===
我离开窗口，在渐浓的暮色中坐了下来。原来下铺七号的那个乘客是在克雷森上的车，很可能跟艾莉森·韦斯特和她的同伴一起。有个人她在乎到不惜为之遮掩。我烦躁地走到门口，叫来了克洛普顿太太。

"那些玫瑰你可以扔掉了，"我头也不抬地说，"它们已经彻底枯了。"

"都枯了三天了，"她没好气地回嘴。"尤菲米娅（Euphemia）说你威胁过她，说要是她碰了那些花就炒她鱿鱼。"

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
The Trapdoor

By Sunday evening, a week after the wreck, my inaction had goaded me to frenzy. The very sight of Johnson across the street or lurking, always within sight of the house, kept me constantly exasperated. It was on that day that things began to come to a focus, a burning-glass of events that seemed to center on me.

I dined alone that evening in no cheerful frame of mind. There had been a polo game the day before and I had lent a pony, which is always a bad thing to do. And she had wrenched her shoulder, besides helping to lose the game. There was no one in town: the temperature was ninety and climbing, and my left hand persistently cramped under its bandage.

===Chinese===
活板门

到星期天晚上，也就是车祸后整整一周，我的无所事事已经把我逼得快发疯了。光是看见约翰逊（Johnson）在街对面晃悠，或者在房子附近鬼鬼祟祟地蹲守——他总是待在看得见房子的地方——就足以让我怒火中烧。正是在这一天，事情开始聚焦了，像一个聚光镜把所有事件汇聚到我身上。

那天晚上我独自用餐，心情好不起来。前一天有场马球赛，我借出了一匹矮种马——借马这事儿从来就没有好下场。那匹马不光害我们输了比赛，还把肩膀给扭伤了。镇上一个人也没有：气温九十度还在往上爬，我的左手在绷带底下不停地抽筋。

===Original===
Mrs. Klopton herself saw me served, my bread buttered and cut in tidbits, my meat ready for my fork. She hovered around me maternally, obviously trying to cheer me.

“The paper says still warmer,” she ventured. “The thermometer is ninety-two now.”

“And this coffee is two hundred and fifty,” I said, putting down my cup. “Where is Euphemia? I haven’t seen her around, or heard a dish smash all day.”

“Euphemia is in bed,” Mrs. Klopton said gravely. “Is your meat cut small enough, Mr. Lawrence?” Mrs. Klopton can throw more mystery into an ordinary sentence than anyone I know. She can say, “Are your sheets damp, sir?” And I can tell from her tone that the house across the street has been robbed, or that my left hand neighbor has appendicitis. So now I looked up and asked the question she was waiting for.

===Chinese===
克洛普顿夫人亲自照看我用餐，面包涂好黄油、切成小块，肉切好了等着我下叉。她像母亲一样在我身边转来转去，显然是想让我开心些。

"报纸说还会更热，"她试探着说。"温度计已经九十二度了。"

"这杯咖啡有两百五十度，"我把杯子放下说。"尤菲米亚（Euphemia）在哪儿？我一整天没看见她，也没听见摔盘子的声响。"

"尤菲米亚在床上躺着呢，"克洛普顿夫人郑重其事地说。"劳伦斯先生，您的肉切得够小了吗？"克洛普顿夫人能用最寻常的句子营造出最浓厚的神秘气氛。她说一句"先生，您的床单潮吗？"我就能从她的语气里听出街对面那家被抢了，或者我左手边的邻居得了阑尾炎。于是现在我抬起头来，问了那个她正等着我问的问题。

===Original===
“What’s the matter with Euphemia?” I inquired idly.

“Frightened into her bed,” Mrs. Klopton said in a stage whisper. “She’s had three hot water bottles and she hasn’t done a thing all day but moan.”

“She oughtn’t to take hot water bottles,” I said in my severest tone. “One would make me moan. You need not wait, I’ll ring if I need anything.”

Mrs. Klopton sailed to the door, where she stopped and wheeled indignantly. “I only hope you won’t laugh on the wrong side of your face some morning, Mr. Lawrence,” she declared, with Christian fortitude. “But I warn you, I am going to have the police watch that house next door.”

===Chinese===
"尤菲米亚怎么了？"我漫不经心地问。

"吓病了，"克洛普顿夫人压低声音说。"她放了三个热水瓶，一整天什么都没干，光在那儿哼哼。"

"她不该用热水瓶，"我用最严厉的口气说。"一个热水瓶就够我哼哼的了。你不用守着，有事我按铃。"

克洛普顿夫人翩然走向门口，到了门口又愤愤地转过身来。"我只希望您哪天早上别笑错了地方，劳伦斯先生，"她带着基督徒般的坚忍说道。"但我警告您，我打算让警察来监视隔壁那栋房子。"

===Original===
I was half inclined to tell her that both it and we were under police surveillance at that moment. But I like Mrs. Klopton, in spite of the fact that I make her life a torment for her, so I refrained.

“Last night, when the paper said it was going to storm, I sent Euphemia to the roof to bring the rugs in. Eliza had slipped out, although it was her evening in. Euphemia went up to the roof—it was eleven o’clock—and soon I heard her running downstairs crying. When she got to my room she just folded up on the floor. She said there was a black figure sitting on the parapet of the house next door—the empty house—and that when she appeared it rose and waved long black arms at her and spit like a cat.”

===Chinese===
我差点告诉她，此刻那栋房子和我们自己都在警方的监视之下。但我喜欢克洛普顿夫人，尽管我让她过的日子苦不堪言，所以我忍住了没说。

"昨天晚上报纸说要暴风雨，我打发尤菲米亚上天台去收地毯。伊莱扎（Eliza）溜出去了，明明轮到她歇晚班。尤菲米亚上了天台——那时已经十一点了——不一会儿我就听见她哭着跑下楼来。到了我房门口，她直接瘫倒在地上了。她说隔壁那栋空房子的护墙上坐着一个黑影，她一出现，那黑影就站起来，朝她挥舞长长的黑手臂，还像猫一样'噗噗'地吐口水。"

===Original===
I had finished my dinner and was lighting a cigarette. “If there was anyone up there, which I doubt, they probably sneezed,” I suggested. “But if you feel uneasy, I’ll take a look around the roof tonight before I turn in. As far as Euphemia goes, I wouldn’t be uneasy about her—doesn’t she always have an attack of some sort when Eliza rings in an extra evening on her?”

So I made a superficial examination of the window locks that night, visiting parts of the house that I had not seen since I bought it. Then I went to the roof. Evidently it had not been intended for any purpose save to cover the house, for unlike the houses around, there was no staircase. A ladder and a trapdoor led to it, and it required some nice balancing on my part to get up with my useless arm. I made it, however, and found this unexplored part of my domain rather attractive. It was cooler than downstairs, and I sat on the brick parapet and smoked my final cigarette. The roof of the empty house adjoined mine along the back wing, but investigation showed that the trapdoor across the low dividing wall was bolted underneath.

===Chinese===
我吃完晚饭，点了一支烟。"就算上面有人——我表示怀疑——大概也只是打了个喷嚏，"我说。"不过你要是不放心，今晚睡前我去天台查看一下。至于尤菲米亚嘛，用不着替她担心——哪次伊莱扎偷跑出去，她不是闹点毛病出来？"

于是那天晚上我草草检查了一下窗户锁，顺带巡视了这栋自从买下来就没再看过的几个角落。然后我上了天台。显然这天台除了给房子遮风挡雨之外没有别的用途，因为跟周围的房子不同，它没有楼梯。一架梯子和一扇活板门通往上面，我只有一只手能使，爬上去费了不少平衡功夫。不过我还是上去了，发现这片从未涉足的领地还挺有吸引力。天台上比楼下凉快，我坐在砖砌的护墙上抽完最后一支烟。隔壁那栋空房子的天台沿着后翼跟我的连在一起，但查看发现，隔墙上那扇活板门从下面上了栓。

===Original===
There was nothing out of the ordinary anywhere, and so I assured Mrs. Klopton. Needless to say, I did not tell her that I had left the trapdoor open, to see if it would improve the temperature of the house. I went to bed at midnight, merely because there was nothing else to do. I turned on the night lamp at the head of my bed, and picked up a volume of Shaw at random (it was Arms and the Man, and I remember thinking grimly that I was a good bit of a chocolate cream soldier myself), and prepared to go to sleep. Shaw always puts me to sleep. I have no apologies to make for what occurred that night, and not even an explanation that I am sure of. I did a foolish thing under impulse, and I have not been sorry.

===Chinese===
一切都很正常，我这样向克洛普顿夫人保证道。不用说，我没告诉她我故意把那扇活板门打开了，想看看能不能改善房子里的温度。我半夜上了床，纯粹是因为没有别的事可做。我拧亮床头的夜灯，随手拿起一本萧伯纳的书（是《武器与人》（Arms and the Man），我记得当时苦涩地想，我自己倒是个不折不扣的巧克力奶油兵）。萧伯纳的书总能让我睡着。对那天晚上发生的事，我不打算辩解，甚至拿不出一个自己确信的解释。我凭冲动做了一件蠢事，但我不后悔。

===Original===
It was something after two when the doorbell rang. It rang quickly, twice. I got up drowsily, for the maids and Mrs. Klopton always lock themselves beyond reach of the bell at night, and put on a dressing-gown. The bell rang again on my way downstairs. I lit the hall light and opened the door. I was wide-awake now, and I saw that it was Johnson. His bald head shone in the light—his crooked mouth was twisted in a smile.

“Good Heavens, man,” I said irritably. “Don’t you ever go home and go to bed?”

He closed the vestibule door behind him and cavalierly turned out the light. Our dialogue was sharp, staccato.

“Have you a key to the empty house next door?” he demanded. “Somebody’s in there, and the latch is caught.”

===Chinese===
门铃响的时候大约过了两点。铃声急促地响了两下。我睡眼惺忪地爬起来，因为女佣们和克洛普顿夫人晚上总把自己锁在够不着门铃的地方，我披上晨袍。下楼的时候铃又响了一次。我打开走廊灯，开了门。这时我已经完全清醒了，认出是约翰逊。他的秃头在灯光下闪闪发亮——歪斜的嘴角扭曲成一丝微笑。

"老天爷，你这家伙，"我恼火地说。"你就不能回家睡一觉吗？"

他随手关上门厅的门，毫不客气地把灯关了。我们的对话简短、生硬。

"你有隔壁那栋空房子的钥匙吗？"他问道。"有人在里面，门闩是卡住的。"

===Original===
“The houses are alike. The key to this door may fit. Did you see them go in?”

“No. There’s a light moving up from room to room. I saw something like it last night, and I have been watching. The patrolman reported queer doings there a week or so ago.”

“A light!” I exclaimed. “Do you mean that you—”

“Very likely,” he said grimly. “Have you a revolver?”

“All kinds in the gun rack,” I replied, and going into the den, I came back with a Smith and Wesson. “I’m not much use,” I explained, “with this arm, but I’ll do what I can. There may be somebody there. The servants here have been uneasy.”

===Chinese===
"这两栋房子是一样的。这把钥匙也许能开。你看见他们进去了吗？"

"没有。有一盏灯在各个房间之间移动。昨晚我看到类似的情况，一直盯着。巡警大约一周前报告说那里面有可疑动静。"

"一盏灯！"我惊叫道。"你是说你——"

"很有可能，"他冷冷地说。"你有手枪吗？"

"枪架上什么都有，"我答道，走进书房，拿了一把史密斯威森（Smith & Wesson）出来。"我这只胳膊不太管用，"我解释道，"但我会尽力。那里面可能真有人。这边的佣人一直心神不宁。"

===Original===
Johnson planned the campaign. He suggested on account of my familiarity with the roof, that I go there and cut off escape in that direction. “I have Robison out there now—the patrolman on the beat,” he said. “He’ll watch below and you above, while I search the house. Be as quiet as possible.”

I was rather amused. I put on some clothes and felt my way carefully up the stairs, the revolver swinging free in my pocket, my hand on the rail. At the foot of the ladder I stopped and looked up. Above me there was a gray rectangle of sky dotted with stars. It occurred to me that with my one serviceable hand holding the ladder, I was hardly in a position to defend myself, that I was about to hoist a body that I am rather careful of into a danger I couldn’t see and wasn’t particularly keen about anyhow. I don’t mind saying that the seconds it took me to scramble up the ladder were among the most unpleasant that I recall.

===Chinese===
约翰逊制定了作战计划。他建议由我上天台去，因为我对天台比较熟悉，可以截断那个方向的退路。"我已经安排了罗比森（Robison）在外面——就是巡逻的片儿警，"他说。"他在下面盯梢，你在上面堵截，我进屋搜查。尽量别出声。"

我觉得挺有意思。我穿上一些衣服，小心翼翼地摸黑上楼，手枪在口袋里晃来晃去，手扶着楼梯扶手。到了梯子底下，我停下来抬头望。头顶是一方缀满星星的灰色天幕。我突然想到，我只有一只手能用，还得抓着梯子，根本没法自卫——也就是说，我要把自己这副自己还挺爱惜的身板吊到一个我看不见、也压根不想靠近的危险上去。我不介意说，爬那架梯子的几秒钟，是我记忆中最难熬的时刻之一。

===Original===
I got to the top, however, without incident. I could see fairly well after the darkness of the house beneath, but there was nothing suspicious in sight. The roofs, separated by two feet of brick wall, stretched around me, unbroken save by an occasional chimney. I went very softly over to the other trap, the one belonging to the suspected house. It was closed, but I imagined I could hear Johnson’s footsteps ascending heavily. Then even that was gone. A nearby clock struck three as I stood waiting. I examined my revolver then, for the first time, and found it was empty!

===Chinese===
不过我平安爬了上去。经过楼下一片漆黑之后，上面的光线还算看得清，但视野里没有可疑的东西。两堵一砖墙隔开的屋顶在我四周铺展开来，除了偶尔凸出的烟囱，一片平坦。我蹑手蹑脚地走向那扇属于可疑房屋的活板门。它关着，但我似乎听到了约翰逊沉重的脚步声正在上楼。然后连那声音也消失了。附近一座钟敲了三下，我站在那儿等着。这时我第一次检查了我的手枪——发现它是空的！

===Original===
I had been rather skeptical until now. I had had the usual tolerant attitude of the man who is summoned from his bed to search for burglars, combined with the artificial courage of firearms. With the discovery of my empty gun, I felt like a man on the top of a volcano in lively eruption. Suddenly I found myself staring incredulously at the trapdoor at my feet. I had examined it early in the evening and found it bolted. Did I imagine it, or had it raised about an inch? Wasn’t it moving slowly as I looked? No, I am not a hero: I was startled almost into a panic. I had one arm, and whoever was raising that trapdoor had two. My knees had a queer inclination to bend the wrong way.

===Chinese===
此前我一直将信将疑。被从床上叫起来抓贼的人，通常都带着那种宽容的无所谓态度，再加上一把枪撑腰，便有了几分底气。发现枪是空的之后，我感觉自己像站在一座正猛烈喷发的火山顶上。突然间，我难以置信地盯着脚下的活板门。傍晚的时候我检查过它，明明上了栓。是我眼花了，还是它已经翘起了一英寸左右？我看着它的时候，它是不是在缓慢地移动？不，我不是英雄：我被吓得几乎要恐慌了。我只有一只手，而掀那扇活板门的人有两只手。我的膝盖不由自主地想往反方向弯。

===Original===
Johnson’s footsteps were distinct enough, but he was evidently far below. The trap, raised perhaps two inches now, remained stationary. There was no sound from beneath it: once I thought I heard two or three gasping respirations: I am not sure they were not my own. I wanted desperately to stand on one leg at a time and hold the other up out of focus of a possible revolver.

I did not see the hand appear. There was nothing there, and then it was there, clutching the frame of the trap. I did the only thing I could think of; I put my foot on it!

===Chinese===
约翰逊的脚步声清晰可闻，但他显然还在楼下很远的地方。活板门大概已经翘起了两英寸，但静止不动了。下面没有声响：有一次我以为听到了两三声急促的喘息——我不确定那是不是我自己的。我拼命想单脚站立，把另一条腿抬高，躲开可能的射击角度。

我没看见那只手出现。那里空无一物，然后它就在那儿了——紧紧抓住了活板门的门框。我做了我唯一能想到的事——一脚踩了上去！

===Original===
There was not a sound from beneath. The next moment I was kneeling and had clutched the wrist just above the hand. After a second’s struggle, the arm was still. With something real to face, I was myself again.

“Don’t move, or I’ll stand on the trap and break your arm,” I panted. What else could I threaten? I couldn’t shoot, I couldn’t even fight. “Johnson!” I called.

And then I realized the thing that stayed with me for a month, the thing I can not think of even now without a shudder. The hand lay ice cold, strangely quiescent. Under my fingers, an artery was beating feebly. The wrist was as slender as—I held the hand to the light. Then I let it drop.

===Chinese===
下面没有一点声响。下一秒我已经跪下来，死死抓住那只手腕——就在手掌上方。挣扎了一秒钟之后，那只胳膊不动了。面对着实实在在的东西，我又恢复了自己。

"别动，不然我踩下去把你的胳膊踩断，"我气喘吁吁地说。我还能威胁什么？我开不了枪，甚至打不了架。"约翰逊！"我喊道。

然后我意识到了一件事——这件事困扰了我整整一个月，直到现在想起来仍会打个冷战。那只手冰凉，出奇地安静。我的手指底下，一根动脉在微弱地搏动。那只手腕纤细得像——我把那只手举到灯光下看了看。然后松开了。

===Original===
“Good Lord,” I muttered, and remained on my knees, staring at the spot where the hand had been. It was gone now: there was a faint rustle in the darkness below, and then silence.

I held up my own hand in the starlight and stared at a long scratch in the palm. “A woman!” I said to myself stupidly. “By all that’s ridiculous, a woman!”

===Chinese===
"老天爷，"我喃喃道，仍然跪在那儿，盯着那只手刚才待过的地方。它已经不见了：下方的黑暗中传来一阵轻微的窸窣声，然后归于沉寂。

我在星光下举起自己的手，盯着掌心的一道长长的抓痕。"一个女人！"我傻愣愣地对自己说。"简直荒唐透顶，竟然是个女人！"

===Original===
Johnson was striking matches below and swearing softly to himself. “How the devil do you get to the roof?” he called. “I think I’ve broken my nose.”

He found the ladder after a short search and stood at the bottom, looking up at me. “Well, I suppose you haven’t seen him?” he inquired. “There are enough darned cubbyholes in this house to hide a patrol wagon load of thieves.” He lighted a fresh match. “Hello, here’s another door!”

By the sound of his diminishing footsteps I supposed it was a rear staircase. He came up again in ten minutes or so, this time with the policeman.

“He’s gone, all right,” he said ruefully. “If you’d been attending to your business, Robison, you’d have watched the back door.”

“I’m not twins.” Robison was surly.

===Chinese===
约翰逊在下面划着火柴，轻声咒骂着。"这鬼天台到底怎么上去？"他喊道。"我觉得我鼻子撞断了。"

找了一会儿之后，他找到了梯子，站在底下仰头看我。"好吧，我想你没看见他吧？"他问道。"这破房子里的暗角旮旯足够藏下满满一车贼了。"他又划了一根火柴。"嘿，这儿还有一扇门！"

从他渐渐远去的脚步声判断，那大概是通向后面的楼梯。大约十分钟后他重新上来了，这次带着那个警察。

"他跑了，没跑了，"他懊恼地说。"你要是稍微尽点职，罗比森，就该守住后门。"

"我又不是双胞胎。"罗比森闷闷不乐。

===Original===
“Well,” I broke in, as cheerfully as I could, “if you are through with this jolly little affair, and can get down my ladder without having my housekeeper ring the burglar alarm, I have some good Monongahela whisky—eh?”

They came without a second invitation across the roof, and with them safely away from the house I breathed more freely. Down in the den I fulfilled my promise, which Johnson drank to the toast, “Coming through the rye.” He examined my gun rack with the eye of a connoisseur, and even when he was about to go he cast a loving eye back at the weapons.

“Ever been in the army?” he inquired.

“No,” I said with a bitterness that he noticed but failed to comprehend. “I’m a chocolate cream soldier—you don’t read Shaw, I suppose, Johnson?”

“Never heard of him,” the detective said indifferently. “Well, good night, Mr. Blakeley. Much obliged.” At the door he hesitated and coughed.

===Chinese===
"好了，"我尽量轻快地插话道，"要是你们这场愉快的小行动结束了，能从我的梯子上下来，还不至于让我管家拉响防盗铃的话，我那儿有上好的莫农加希拉威士忌（Monongahela whisky）——来一杯？"

他们二话没说就穿过天台过来了。眼看他俩安全离开了那栋房子，我松了口气。到了书房里，我兑现了承诺，约翰逊举杯祝酒道："穿过黑麦地"（Coming through the rye）。他用行家的目光审视着我的枪架，临走时还恋恋不舍地回头看了一眼那些武器。

"当过兵吗？"他问道。

"没有，"我带着一种他注意到了却没能理解的苦涩说。"我是个巧克力奶油兵——你不读萧伯纳吧，约翰逊？"

"没听说过，"侦探淡漠地说。"好了，晚安，布莱克利先生。多谢了。"到了门口他犹豫了一下，清了清嗓子。

===Original===
“I suppose you understand, Mr. Blakeley,” he said awkwardly, “that this—er—surveillance is all in the day’s work. I don’t like it, but it’s duty. Every man to his duty, sir.”

“Sometime when you are in an open mood, Johnson,” I returned, “you can explain why I am being watched at all.”

===Chinese===
"我想您明白，布莱克利先生，"他尴尬地说，"这个——呃——监视不过是例行公事。我不喜欢这样，但这是职责。各人尽各人的职，先生。"

"哪天你心情好了，约翰逊，"我回嘴道，"倒可以跟我解释解释，为什么我非得被监视不可。"

## XV / 活动电影

===Original===
On Monday I went out for the first time. I did not go to the office. I wanted to walk. I thought fresh air and exercise would drive away the blue devils that had me by the throat. McKnight insisted on a long day in his car, but I refused.

"I don't know why not," he said sulkily. "I can't walk. I haven't walked two consecutive blocks in three years. Automobiles have made legs mere ornaments—and some not even that. We could have Johnson out there chasing us over the country at five dollars an hour!"

"He can chase us just as well at five miles an hour," I said. "But what gets me, McKnight, is why I am under surveillance at all. How do the police know *I* was accused of that thing?"

===Chinese===
星期一我第一次出门。没去事务所。我想走路。新鲜空气和运动应该能把扼住我喉咙的忧郁鬼赶走。麦克奈特非要用他的车载我兜一整天，我拒绝了。

"搞不懂你为什么不肯，"他没好气地说，"我又走不动路。三年了，我连两个连续的街区都没走全过。汽车让两条腿成了摆设——有些人连摆设都不算。咱们可以把约翰逊（Johnson）叫出来，让他在乡下追我们，五块钱一小时！"

"他以五英里的时速追我们照样追得上，"我说，"不过让我想不通的是，麦克奈特，我为什么会被监视？警察怎么知道是*我*被指控了那件事？"

===Original===
"The young lady who sent the flowers—she isn't likely to talk, is she?"

"No. That is, I didn't say it was a lady." I groaned as I tried to get my splinted arm into a coat. "Anyhow, she didn't tell," I finished with conviction, and McKnight laughed.

It had rained in the early morning, and Mrs. Klopton predicted more showers. In fact, so firm was her belief and so determined her eye that I took the umbrella she proffered me.

"Never mind," I said. "We can leave it next door; I have a story to tell you, Richey, and it requires proper setting."

McKnight was puzzled, but he followed me obediently round to the kitchen entrance of the empty house. It was unlocked, as I had expected. While we climbed to the upper floor I retailed the events of the previous night.

===Chinese===
"送花的那个年轻小姐——她总不至于告密吧？"

"不会。呃，我又没说那是位小姐。"我呻吟着把夹着夹板的胳膊塞进外套。"不管怎样，她没告密，"我语气笃定地说完，麦克奈特笑了。

清晨下过雨，克洛普顿太太（Mrs. Klopton）预言还会有阵雨。事实上，她信念之坚、目光之决，让我不得不接过了她递来的伞。

"无所谓，"我说，"放隔壁就行。里奇（Richey），我有个故事要讲给你听，需要找个合适的场景。"

麦克奈特一脸困惑，但还是乖乖跟着我绕到那栋空房子的厨房入口。门没锁，跟我预料的一样。我们往上楼爬的时候，我把昨晚的事原原本本讲了一遍。

===Original===
"It's the finest thing I ever heard of," McKnight said, staring up at the ladder and the trap. "What a vaudeville skit it would make! Only you ought not to have put your foot on her hand. They don't do it in the best circles."

I wheeled on him impatiently.

"You don't understand the situation at all, Richey!" I exclaimed. "What would you say if I tell you it was the hand of a lady? It was covered with rings."

"A lady!" he repeated. "Why, I'd say it was a darned compromising situation, and that the less you say of it the better. Look here, Lawrence, I think you dreamed it. You've been in the house too much. I take it all back: you do need exercise."

"She escaped through this door, I suppose," I said as patiently as I could. "Evidently down the back staircase. We might as well go down that way."

===Chinese===
"这是我听过的最精彩的事，"麦克奈特仰头望着梯子和活板门说，"拿去演杂耍短剧简直绝了！只不过你不该把脚踩到她手上。上流社会不兴这个。"

我转过身，不耐烦地瞪着他。

"你完全没搞懂状况，里奇！"我叫道，"我告诉你那是一只女士的手你信不信？手上戴满了戒指。"

"女士！"他重复道，"要我说，那可是够呛的暧昧局面，少提为妙。听着，劳伦斯（Lawrence），我觉得你在做梦。你在家待太久了。我把话收回来：你确实需要运动。"

"她是从这扇门逃出去的，"我尽量耐心地说，"显然是走的后楼梯。咱们也从那儿下去吧。"

===Original===
"According to the best precedents in these affairs, we should find a glove about here," he said as we started down. But he was more impressed than he cared to own. He examined the dusty steps carefully, and once, when a bit of loose plaster fell just behind him, he started like a nervous woman.

"What I don't understand is why you let her go," he said, stopping once, puzzled. "You're not usually quixotic."

"When we get out into the country, Richey," I replied gravely, "I am going to tell you another story, and if you don't tell me I'm a fool and a craven, on the strength of it, you are no friend of mine."

We stumbled through the twilight of the staircase into the blackness of the shuttered kitchen. The house had the moldy smell of closed buildings: even on that warm September morning it was damp and chilly. As we stepped into the sunshine McKnight gave a shiver.

===Chinese===
"按这类事件的最佳先例，我们该在这儿找到一只手套，"他一边往下走一边说。但他比我愿意承认的要紧张得多。他仔细查看布满灰尘的台阶，有一次一块松动的灰泥正好落在他身后，他像受惊的女人一样打了个哆嗦。

"我不明白的是你为什么放她走，"他停下脚步，困惑地说，"你平时可没这么侠义。"

"等到了乡下，里奇，"我严肃地说，"我要再给你讲一个故事。要是听完这个你不说我是个傻瓜和懦夫，你就不是我朋友。"

我们跌跌撞撞穿过楼梯间的昏暗，走进了百叶窗紧闭的厨房的漆黑。房子有一种封闭建筑特有的霉味：即便在九月这样一个温暖的早晨，里面又湿又冷。踏进阳光的那一刻，麦克奈特打了个寒战。

===Original===
"Now that we are out," he said, "I don't mind telling you that I have been there before. Do you remember the night you left, and, the face at the window?"

"When you speak of it—yes."

"Well, I was curious about that thing," he went on, as we started up the street, "and I went back. The street door was unlocked, and I examined every room. I was Mrs. Klopton's ghost that carried a light, and clumb."

"Did you find anything?"

"Only a clean place rubbed on the window opposite your dressing-room. Splendid view of an untidy interior. If that house is ever occupied, you'd better put stained glass in that window of yours."

===Chinese===
"既然出来了，"他说，"我也不妨告诉你，我之前去过那儿。你还记得你走的那天晚上窗户上那张脸吗？"

"你提起的话——记得。"

"嗯，我对那事挺好奇，"我们沿街走着，他接着说，"就回去看了一趟。街门没锁，我一间一间检查了每个房间。我就是克洛普顿太太的鬼魂——提着灯，爬来爬去。"

"发现什么了吗？"

"就发现你梳妆室对面的窗户上擦出了一块干净的地方。透过那扇窗能一览不雅的室内全景。那栋房子要是住人了，你最好把那扇窗换成彩色玻璃。"

===Original===
As we turned the corner I glanced back. Half a block behind us Johnson was moving our way slowly. When he saw me he stopped and proceeded with great deliberation to light a cigar. By hurrying, however, he caught the car that we took, and stood unobtrusively on the rear platform. He looked fagged, and absentmindedly paid our fares, to McKnight's delight.

"We will give him a run for his money," he declared, as the car moved countryward. "Conductor, let us off at the muddiest lane you can find."

At one o'clock, after a six-mile ramble, we entered a small country hotel. We had seen nothing of Johnson for a half hour. At that time he was a quarter of a mile behind us, and losing rapidly. Before we had finished our luncheon he staggered into the inn. One of his boots was under his arm, and his whole appearance was deplorable. He was coated with mud, streaked with sweat, and he limped as he walked. He chose a table not far from us and ordered Scotch. Beyond touching his hat he paid no attention to us.

===Chinese===
拐弯时我回头瞥了一眼。我们身后半个街区处，约翰逊正慢悠悠地朝这边走。看到我，他停下来，不紧不慢地点了根雪茄。不过他还是赶上了我们坐的那趟车，悄无声息地站在后车厢台上。他看起来精疲力竭，心不在焉地替我们付了车费，麦克奈特乐坏了。

"咱们得让他跑个痛快，"电车往乡间驶去，他宣布道，"售票员，给我们找个泥巴最多的路口下。"

一点钟，走了六英里路后，我们进了一家乡村小旅馆。我们有半小时没见约翰逊的影子了。那时他落在我们后面四分之一英里，而且越落越远。我们还没吃完午饭，他就踉踉跄跄地走进了旅店。一只胳膊下面夹着一只靴子，整个人惨不忍睹。浑身是泥，汗渍斑斑，走路一瘸一拐。他挑了一张离我们不远的桌子，点了苏格兰威士忌。除了碰了碰帽子，他没搭理我们。

===Original===
"I'm just getting my second wind," McKnight declared. "How do you feel, Mr. Johnson? Six or eight miles more and we'll all enjoy our dinners." Johnson put down the glass he had raised to his lips without replying.

The fact was, however, that I was like Johnson. I was soft from my week's inaction, and I was pretty well done up. McKnight, who was a well spring of vitality and high spirits, ordered a strange concoction, made of nearly everything in the bar, and sent it over to the detective, but Johnson refused it.

"I hate that kind of person," McKnight said pettishly. "Kind of a fellow that thinks you're going to poison his dog if you offer him a bone."

When we got back to the car line, with Johnson a draggled and drooping tail to the kite, I was in better spirits. I had told McKnight the story of the three hours just after the wreck; I had not named the girl, of course; she had my promise of secrecy. But I told him everything else. It was a relief to have a fresh mind on it: I had puzzled so much over the incident at the farmhouse, and the necklace in the gold bag, that I had lost perspective.

===Chinese===
"我刚缓过劲来，"麦克奈特宣布道，"你觉得呢，约翰逊先生？再走个六英里八英里的，咱们晚饭都能吃得香了。"约翰逊把举到嘴边的杯子放下，没有回答。

然而事实是，我也跟约翰逊差不多。一周没动弹让我变得松松垮垮，已经累得够呛。麦克奈特——他简直是活力和兴致的源泉——要了一杯怪里怪气的大杂烩，里面几乎掺了吧台上所有的酒，叫人给那位侦探送过去，但约翰逊拒绝了。

"我就讨厌这种人，"麦克奈特气鼓鼓地说，"就像那种你给他根骨头他还怕你毒死他家狗的人。"

我们回到电车线的时候，约翰逊像风筝后面一条耷拉着的脏尾巴，我倒是心情好多了。我跟麦克奈特讲了车祸后那三个小时的事；当然没提那个女孩的名字——她答应过要保密。但其他一切都说了。有个新的人来听这件事让我松了口气：我为农舍里那桩事和金手提袋里的项链绞尽了脑汁，已经钻进了牛角尖。

===Original===
He had been interested, but inclined to be amused, until I came to the broken chain. Then he had whistled softly.

"But there are tons of fine gold chains made every year," he said. "Why in the world do you think that the—er—smeary piece came from that necklace?"

I had looked around. Johnson was far behind, scraping the mud off his feet with a piece of stick.

"I have the short end of the chain in the sealskin bag," I reminded him. "When I couldn't sleep this morning I thought I would settle it, one way or the other. It was hell to go along the way I had been doing. And—there's no doubt about it, Rich. It's the same chain."

We walked along in silence until we caught the car back to town.

===Chinese===
他听得有趣，但多少有些戏谑，直到我讲到断掉的那段项链。他轻轻地吹了声口哨。

"可每年做出来的细金链子多得是，"他说，"你到底为什么觉得那——呃——沾了污渍的东西是从那条项链上掉下来的？"

我环顾四周。约翰逊远远落在后面，正拿一根树枝刮脚上的泥。

"海豹皮袋子里有项链的另一截，"我提醒他，"今天早上睡不着的时候我想把这事弄个明白。像之前那样悬着简直折磨人。而且——里奇，我确定。是同一条项链。"

我们沉默地走着，直到搭上了回城的电车。

===Original===
"Well," he said finally, "you know the girl, of course, and I don't. But if you like her—and I think myself you're rather hard hit, old man—I wouldn't give a whoop about the chain in the gold purse. It's just one of the little coincidences that hang people now and then. And as for last night—if she's the kind of a girl you say she is, and you think she had anything to do with that, you—you're addled, that's all. You can depend on it, the lady of the empty house last week is the lady of last night. And yet your train acquaintance was in Altoona at that time."

Just before we got off the car, I reverted to the subject again. It was never far back in my mind.

"About the—young lady of the train, Rich," I said, with what I suppose was elaborate carelessness, "I don't want you to get a wrong impression. I am rather unlikely to see her again, but even if I do, I—I believe she is already 'bespoke,' or next thing to it."

He made no reply, but as I opened the door with my latchkey he stood looking up at me from the pavement with his quizzical smile.

"Love is like the measles," he orated. "The older you get it, the worse the attack."

===Chinese===
"好吧，"他终于说道，"你认识那个女孩，我不认识。可如果你喜欢她——我觉得你已经相当神魂颠倒了，老兄——我不会在乎金手提袋里那条项链的。那只不过是时不时会吊死人的那种小巧合。至于昨晚——如果她真是你说的那种女孩，你觉得她跟那事有半点关系，那你就——你就糊涂了，没别的。你大可以相信，上星期空房子里的那位女士就是昨晚的那位女士。可你火车上遇到的那位当时在阿尔图纳（Altoona）。"

下车前我又把这个话题捡了起来。它在我脑子里就没远过。

"关于火车上那位——年轻小姐，里奇，"我故作漫不经心地说，"我不希望你误会。我大概不会再见到她了，即便见到，我——我已经有她在身——或者说差不多了。"

他没有回答，但当我用钥匙开门时，他站在人行道上仰头看着我，带着那种似笑非笑的表情。

"恋爱就像麻疹，"他振振有词地说，"年纪越大，病得越重。"

===Original===
Johnson did not appear again that day. A small man in a raincoat took his place. The next morning I made my initial trip to the office, the raincoat still on hand. I had a short conference with Miller, the district attorney, at eleven. Bronson was under surveillance, he said, and any attempt to sell the notes to him would probably result in their recovery. In the meantime, as I knew, the Commonwealth had continued the case, in hope of such contingency.

At noon I left the office and took a veterinarian to see Candida, the injured pony. By one o'clock my first day's duties were performed, and a long Sahara of hot afternoon stretched ahead. McKnight, always glad to escape from the grind, suggested a vaudeville, and in sheer ennui I consented. I could neither ride, drive nor golf, and my own company bored me to distraction.

"Coolest place in town these days," he declared. "Electric fans, breezy songs, airy costumes. And there's Johnson just behind—the coldest proposition in Washington."

===Chinese===
那天约翰逊没再出现。一个穿雨衣的小个子接了他的班。第二天早上我第一次去了事务所，那个穿雨衣的还在。十一点钟我跟地方检察官米勒（Miller）开了个简短的会。他说布朗森（Bronson）正被监视着，任何企图把票据卖给他的举动都可能被截获。与此同时，如我所知，当局一直在追查这个案子，以防出现意外情况。

中午我离开事务所，带了个兽医去看受伤的小马坎迪达（Candida）。一点钟前，第一天的公务就办完了，漫长而酷热的下午像撒哈拉沙漠一样铺展在面前。麦克奈特——他总是乐得从苦差事里脱身——提议去看杂耍表演，我无聊透顶，也就答应了。我既不能骑马也不能开车也不能打高尔夫，跟自己待着简直烦透了。

"城里这些天最凉快的地方，"他宣称道，"电风扇、轻快的歌曲、清凉的服装。约翰逊就在后面跟着——华盛顿最冷的一块料。"

===Original===
He gravely bought three tickets and presented the detective with one. Then we went in. Having lived a normal, busy life, the theater in the afternoon is to me about on a par with ice-cream for breakfast. Up on the stage a very stout woman in short pink skirts, with a smile that McKnight declared looked like a slash in a roll of butter, was singing nasally, with a laborious kick at the end of each verse. Johnson, two rows ahead, went to sleep. McKnight prodded me with his elbow.

"Look at the first box to the right," he said, in a stage whisper. "I want you to come over at the end of this act."

It was the first time I had seen her since I put her in the cab at Baltimore. Outwardly I presume I was calm, for no one turned to stare at me, but every atom of me cried out at the sight of her. She was leaning, bent forward, lips slightly parted, gazing raptly at the Japanese conjurer who had replaced what McKnight disrespectfully called the Columns of Hercules. Compared with the draggled lady of the farmhouse, she was radiant.

===Chinese===
他一本正经地买了三张票，送了侦探一张。然后我们进了剧场。我一直过着正常忙碌的生活，对我来说下午看戏跟早上吃冰淇淋差不多。舞台上一个极胖的女人穿着粉色短裙，脸上挂着一种麦克奈特说像在黄油卷上切了一刀的笑容，正用鼻音唱歌，每唱完一段还要费力地踢一下腿。约翰逊坐在我们前两排，已经睡着了。麦克奈特用胳膊肘捅了捅我。

"看右边第一个包厢，"他压低声音说，"这幕完了我要你过去打个招呼。"

这是自从我在巴尔的摩把她送上马车以来第一次见到她。表面上我大概很镇定——没有人回头看我——但我身体的每一个细胞在看到她的那一刻都在呐喊。她身子前倾，嘴唇微启，出神地望着那个日本魔术师——麦克奈特不敬地管之前那个节目叫"赫拉克勒斯之柱"（Columns of Hercules）——而那魔术师已经替代了那个节目。跟农舍里那个衣衫不齐整的女人相比，她容光焕发。

===Original===
For that first moment there was nothing but joy at the sight of her. McKnight's touch on my arm brought me back to reality.

"Come over and meet them," he said. "That's the cousin Miss West is visiting, Mrs. Dallas."

But I would not go. After he went I sat there alone, painfully conscious that I was being pointed out and stared at from the box. The abominable Japanese gave way to yet more atrocious performing dogs.

"How many offers of marriage will the young lady in the box have?" The dog stopped sagely at "none," and then pulled out a card that said eight. Wild shouts of glee by the audience. "The fools," I muttered.

After a little I glanced over. Mrs. Dallas was talking to McKnight, but She was looking straight at me. She was flushed, but more calm than I, and she did not bow. I fumbled for my hat, but the next moment I saw that they were going, and I sat still. When McKnight came back he was triumphant.

===Chinese===
最初那一刻，看到她只有纯粹的喜悦。麦克奈特碰了碰我的胳膊，把我拉回了现实。

"过去打个招呼吧，"他说，"那是韦斯特小姐（Miss West）来拜访的表姐，达拉斯太太（Mrs. Dallas）。"

但我没有去。他走后我独自坐在那儿，痛苦地意识到包厢里有人在指指点点地盯着我看。那个讨厌的日本魔术师让位给了更糟糕的表演狗。

"包厢里的年轻小姐会收到多少份求婚？"那只狗明智地在"零份"处停下，然后抽出一张写着"八份"的卡片。观众爆发出热烈的欢呼。"一群傻瓜，"我咕哝了一句。

过了一会儿我瞥了一眼。达拉斯太太在跟麦克奈特说话，但她正直直地看着我。她脸红了，但比我镇定得多，也没有鞠躬。我手忙脚乱地去摸帽子，但下一刻看到他们要走了，便又坐定不动。麦克奈特回来时一副得意洋洋的样子。

===Original===
"I've made an engagement for you," he said. "Mrs. Dallas asked me to bring you to dinner tonight, and I said I knew you would fall all over yourself to go. You are requested to bring along the broken arm, and any other souvenirs of the wreck that you may possess."

"I'll do nothing of the sort," I declared, struggling against my inclination. "I can't even tie my necktie, and I have to have my food cut for me."

"Oh, that's all right," he said easily. "I'll send Stogie over to fix you up, and Mrs. Dal knows all about the arm. I told her."

(Stogie is his Japanese factotum, so called because he is lean, a yellowish brown in color, and because he claims to have been shipped into this country in a box.)

===Chinese===
"我给你约好了，"他说，"达拉斯太太让我今晚带你去赴宴，我说我知道你会迫不及待地答应。请你带上断胳膊，以及任何你拥有的车祸纪念品。"

"我绝不会干那种事，"我宣称，一边跟自己的意愿搏斗，"我连领带都系不了，吃饭还得人切好。"

"哦，那没关系，"他轻松地说，"我让斯托基（Stogie）过来帮你收拾。达拉斯太太知道你胳膊的事。我跟她说了。"

（斯托基是他的日本全能帮手，之所以这么叫，是因为他瘦，肤色偏黄棕，而且他声称自己是被装在箱子里运到这个国家的。）

===Original===
The Cinematograph was finishing the program. The house was dark and the music had stopped, as it does in the circus just before somebody risks his neck at so much a neck in the Dip of Death, or the hundred-foot dive. Then, with a sort of shock, I saw on the white curtain the announcement:

The Next Picture

is the doomed Washington Flier, taken a short distance from the scene of the wreck on the fatal morning of September tenth. Two miles farther on it met with almost complete annihilation.

I confess to a return of some of the sickening sensations of the wreck; people around me were leaning forward with tense faces. Then the letters were gone, and I saw a long level stretch of track, even the broken stone between the ties standing out distinctly. Far off under a cloud of smoke a small object was rushing toward us and growing larger as it came.

===Chinese===
活动电影机（Cinematograph）正在播映节目尾声。剧场暗下来，音乐停了，就像马戏团里有人要冒着脖子断掉的风险去跳"死亡之渊"或百英尺高台跳水之前那样。然后，带着一阵冲击，我在白色幕布上看到了这行字幕：

下一部影片

是不幸的"华盛顿号特快"（Washington Flier），摄于九月十日那个命运般的清晨，距事发地点仅一小段距离。再往前两英里，它几乎被完全摧毁了。

我承认车祸时那种令人作呕的感觉又回来了；周围的人都身子前倾，表情紧绷。接着字幕消失了，我看到一段笔直延伸的铁轨，枕木之间的碎石都历历可辨。远处一团烟雾下，一个小物体正朝我们冲来，越冲越大。

===Original===
Now it was on us, a mammoth in size, with huge drivers and a colossal tender. The engine leaped aside, as if just in time to save us from destruction, with a glimpse of a stooping fireman and a grimy engineer. The long train of sleepers followed. From a forward vestibule a porter in a white coat waved his hand. The rest of the cars seemed still wrapped in slumber. With mixed sensations I saw my own car, Ontario, fly past, and then I rose to my feet and gripped McKnight's shoulder.

On the lowest step at the last car, one foot hanging free, was a man. His black derby hat was pulled well down to keep it from blowing away, and his coat was flying open in the wind. He was swung well out from the car, his free hand gripping a small valise, every muscle tense for a jump.

"Good God, that's my man!" I said hoarsely, as the audience broke into applause. McKnight half rose: in his seat ahead Johnson stifled a yawn and turned to eye me.

===Chinese===
现在它到了我们面前——庞然大物，巨大的驱动轮和庞大的煤水车。机车猛地偏到一边，仿佛千钧一发之际救了我们一命——透过一闪而过的画面，我看到一个弯着腰的司炉和一脸煤灰的司机。长长的卧铺车厢紧随其后。前面一节车厢的门廊里，一个穿白制服的列车员挥了挥手。其余的车厢似乎仍沉睡在梦中。怀着复杂的感情，我看到我自己的那节车厢——安大略号（Ontario）——从眼前飞过，接着我站起身，一把抓住了麦克奈特的肩膀。

最后一节车厢的最底下一级台阶上，有一只脚悬空着，站着一个男人。他的黑色圆顶礼帽压得很低以防被风吹跑，外套在风中敞开着。他整个人大幅度地探出车厢，空着的那只手提着一个小皮箱，每一块肌肉都绷紧了，随时准备跳车。

"天哪，就是那个人！"我嘶哑地喊道，观众爆发出热烈的掌声。麦克奈特欠了欠身；前排座位上的约翰逊忍住一个哈欠，转头看了我一眼。

===Original===
I dropped into my chair limply, and tried to control my excitement. "The man on the last platform of the train," I said. "He was just about to leap; I'll swear that was my bag."

"Could you see his face?" McKnight asked in an undertone. "Would you know him again?"

"No. His hat was pulled down and his head was bent. I'm going back to find out where that picture was taken. They say two miles, but it may have been forty."

The audience, busy with its wraps, had not noticed. Mrs. Dallas and Alison West had gone. In front of us Johnson had dropped his hat and was stooping for it.

"This way," I motioned to McKnight, and we wheeled into the narrow passage beside us, back of the boxes. At the end there was a door leading into the wings, and as we went boldly through I turned the key.

===Chinese===
我瘫坐回椅子，试图控制住激动的情绪。"火车最后那节车厢尾台上的人，"我说，"他正要跳车——我敢打赌那是我的箱子。"

"看见他的脸了吗？"麦克奈特低声问，"还能认出他吗？"

"没有。他的帽子压得很低，头也低着。我要回去查清楚那张照片是在哪儿拍的。他们说两英里，但可能是四十英里。"

观众正忙着穿外套，没人注意。达拉斯太太和艾莉森·韦斯特已经走了。我们前面的约翰逊掉了帽子，正弯腰去捡。

"这边，"我向麦克奈特一招手，我们转身钻进了包厢后面的窄通道。通道尽头有一扇通向侧幕的门，我们大步走了进去，我把门从里面锁上了。

===Original===
The final set was being struck, and no one paid any attention to us. Luckily they were similarly indifferent to a banging at the door I had locked, a banging which, I judged, signified Johnson.

"I guess we've broken up his interference," McKnight chuckled.

Stage hands were hurrying in every direction; pieces of the last drawing-room menaced us; a switchboard behind us was singing like a teakettle. Everywhere we stepped we were in somebody's way. At last we were across, confronting a man in his shirt sleeves, who by dots and dashes of profanity seemed to be directing the chaos.

"Well?" he said, wheeling on us. "What can I do for you?"

"I would like to ask," I replied, "if you have any idea just where the last cinematograph picture was taken."

"Broken board—picnickers—lake?"

"No. The Washington Flier."

He glanced at my bandaged arm.

"The announcement says two miles," McKnight put in, "but we should like to know whether it is railroad miles, automobile miles, or policeman miles."

"I am sorry I can't tell you," he replied, more civilly. "We get those pictures by contract. We don't take them ourselves."

"Where are the company's offices?"

"New York." He stepped forward and grasped a super by the shoulder. "What in blazes are you doing with that gold chair in a kitchen set? Take that piece of pink plush there and throw it over a soap box, if you haven't got a kitchen chair."

I had not realized the extent of the shock, but now I dropped into a chair and wiped my forehead. The unexpected glimpse of Alison West, followed almost immediately by the revelation of the picture, had left me limp and unnerved. McKnight was looking at his watch.

"He says the moving picture people have an office downtown. We can make it if we go now."

So he called a cab, and we started at a gallop. There was no sign of the detective. "Upon my word," Richey said, "I feel lonely without him."

The people at the downtown office of the cinematograph company were very obliging. The picture had been taken, they said, at M——, just two miles beyond the scene of the wreck. It was not much, but it was something to work on. I decided not to go home, but to send McKnight's Jap for my clothes, and to dress at the Incubator. I was determined, if possible, to make my next day's investigations without Johnson. In the meantime, even if it was for the last time, I would see Her that night. I gave Stogie a note for Mrs. Klopton, and with my dinner clothes there came back the gold bag, wrapped in tissue paper.

===Chinese===
最后一堂布景正在拆除，没人注意到我们。幸运的是，他们对我锁上的那扇门的捶打同样无动于衷——那捶打声，我判断，来自约翰逊。

"看来咱们搅黄了他的监视，"麦克奈特窃笑道。

舞台工人们四面奔忙；上一堂客厅布景的部件摇摇欲坠地从我们身边掠过；身后的配电盘像茶壶一样嘶嘶作响。我们每走一步都会挡住别人的路。终于穿过了侧幕，迎面碰到一个穿着衬衫的男人，他嘴里蹦出的咒骂断断续续地——像莫尔斯电码——似乎在指挥这场混乱。

"什么事？"他转过身来，"我能帮什么忙？"

"我想问一下，"我回答，"您是否知道最后那段活动电影是在哪里拍的。"

"断桥板——野餐——湖？"

"不。是'华盛顿号特快'。"

他瞟了一眼我缠着绷带的胳膊。

"字幕上写的是两英里，"麦克奈特插嘴说，"但我们想知道是铁路英里、汽车英里还是警察英里。"

"抱歉，我没法告诉您，"他客气了些，"那些影片是我们按合同采购的，不是自己拍的。"

"公司的办公室在哪儿？"

"纽约。"他走上前一把拽住一个临时演员的肩膀，"你把那把金色椅子塞进厨房布景里干什么？要是没有厨房椅子，就把那边那块粉色绒布搭在肥皂箱上。"

我没有意识到自己受到的冲击有多大，但这时我坐到了椅子上，擦着额头。意外瞥见艾莉森·韦斯特，紧接着又是影片的揭示，让我浑身发软、心神不宁。麦克奈特在看表。

"他说电影公司在市区有个办公室。现在赶去还来得及。"

于是他叫了辆马车，我们一路疾驰而去。侦探不见踪影。"说真的，"里奇说，"没他我倒觉得冷清了。"

市区那家电影公司的职员非常客气。他们说，影片是在M——拍摄的，就在事发地点前方两英里处。线索不算多，但至少有了一个着手点。我决定不回家，让麦克奈特的日本帮手去取我的衣服，就在"孵化器"（Incubator）换装。我打定主意，如果可能的话，第二天的调查要甩开约翰逊。同时，即便这是最后一次，今晚也要见到她。我托斯托基给克洛普顿太太带了张条子，晚礼服送回来时，里面裹着那只金手提袋，外面包着薄纸。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
The Shadow of a Girl

Certain things about the dinner at the Dallas house will always be obscure to me. Dallas was something in the Fish Commission, and I remember his reeling off fish eggs in billions while we ate our caviar. He had some particular stunt he had been urging the government to for years—something about forbidding the establishment of mills and factories on riverbanks—it seems they kill the fish, either the smoke, or the noise, or something they pour into the water.

Mrs. Dallas was there, I think. Of course, I suppose she must have been; and there was a woman in yellow: I took her in to dinner, and I remember she loosened my clams for me so I could get them. But the only real person at the table was a girl across in white, a sublimated young woman who was as brilliant as I was stupid, who never by any chance looked directly at me, and who appeared and disappeared across the candles and orchids in a sort of halo of radiance.

===Chinese===
一个姑娘的阴影

在达拉斯（Dallas）家吃的那顿晚饭，有些事我至今一头雾水。达拉斯在鱼类委员会任什么职，我记得他一边吃鱼子酱一边滔滔不绝地讲着几十亿枚鱼卵。他有个推行多年的特别计划，是关于禁止在河岸上建工厂的——据说那些工厂会害死鱼，不管是烟雾、噪音，还是往水里排放的什么东西。

达拉斯夫人在场，我想。当然，她应该是在场的；还有个穿黄裙子的女人：我挽她入席，记得她帮我把蛤蜊撬开好让我能吃上。但餐桌上唯一一个真实存在的人，是对面那个穿白裙子的姑娘——一个超凡脱俗的年轻女子，她有多光彩照人我就有多愚钝木讷，她从未碰巧正眼看过我一次，她在烛光和兰花的那一头翩然出现又消失，周身仿佛笼罩着一圈光晕。

===Original===
When the dinner had progressed from salmon to roast, and the conversation had done the same thing—from fish to scandal—the yellow gown turned to me. “We have been awfully good, haven’t we, Mr. Blakeley?” she asked. “Although I am crazy to hear, I have not said ‘wreck’ once. I’m sure you must feel like the survivor of Waterloo, or something of the sort.”

“If you want me to tell you about the wreck,” I said, glancing across the table, “I’m sorry to be disappointing, but I don’t remember anything.”

“You are fortunate to be able to forget it.” It was the first word Miss West had spoken directly to me, and it went to my head.

===Chinese===
晚饭从鲑鱼吃到烤肉，聊天内容也亦步亦趋——从鱼聊到了八卦——黄裙子转向我说："我们表现得够好了吧，布莱克利先生？"她问道。"尽管我恨不得听个痛快，可我一个字都没提'车祸'。我敢说你肯定觉得自己像个滑铁卢的幸存者什么的。"

"要是你想听我讲车祸的事，"我瞥了一眼桌子对面说，"抱歉让你失望了，我什么都不记得了。"

"能忘记是你的福气。"这是韦斯特小姐（Miss West）第一次直接跟我说话，这话让我的心跳都漏了一拍。

===Original===
“There are some things I have not forgotten,” I said, over the candles. “I recall coming to myself some time after, and that a girl, a beautiful girl—”

“Ah!” said the lady in yellow, leaning forward breathlessly. Miss West was staring at me coldly, but, once started, I had to stumble on.

“That a girl was trying to rouse me, and that she told me I had been on fire twice already.” A shudder went around the table.

===Chinese===
"有些事我可没忘，"我隔着烛光说。"我记得过了一阵子我醒了过来，有一个姑娘，一个美丽的姑娘——"

"啊！"黄裙子女士屏住呼吸探过身来。韦斯特小姐冷冷地盯着我，但话已出口，我只能磕磕绊绊地往下讲。

"有一个姑娘在试图唤醒我，她告诉我我已经着了两次火。"一阵战栗传遍了整张餐桌。

===Original===
“But surely that isn’t the end of the story,” Mrs. Dallas put in aggrievedly. “Why, that’s the most tantalizing thing I ever heard.”

“I’m afraid that’s all,” I said. “She went her way and I went mine. If she recalls me at all, she probably thinks of me as a weak-kneed individual who faints like a woman when everything is over.”

“What did I tell you?” Mrs. Dallas asserted triumphantly. “He fainted, did you hear? when everything was over! He hasn’t begun to tell it.”

===Chinese===
"可故事总不该到这儿就完了吧，"达拉斯夫人委屈地插嘴道。"天哪，这是我听过最吊人胃口的事了。"

"恐怕就这些了，"我说。"她走她的路，我走我的路。如果她还记得我的话，大概会把我当成一个软骨头——事情一了就晕过去，跟女人似的。"

"我怎么说来着？"达拉斯夫人洋洋得意地宣布。"他晕过去了，你听见没有？事情一了就晕过去了！他才刚开始讲呢。"

===Original===
I would have given a lot by that time if I had not mentioned the girl. But McKnight took it up there and carried it on.

“Blakeley is a regular geyser,” he said. “He never spouts until he reaches the boiling point. And by that same token, although he hasn’t said much about the Lady of the Wreck, I think he is crazy about her. In fact, I am sure of it. He thinks he has locked his secret in the caves of his soul, but I call you to witness that he has it nailed to his face. Look at him!”

I squirmed miserably and tried to avoid the startled eyes of the girl across the table. I wanted to choke McKnight and murder the rest of the party.

===Chinese===
那时候要是我没提那个姑娘就好了，我愿意付出很大的代价。但麦克奈特接过了话头，接着往下说。

"布莱克利是个活脱脱的间歇泉，"他说。"不达到沸点他绝不会喷。同理可证，虽然他关于'车祸女神'说得不多，我觉得他为她神魂颠倒。事实上，我敢打包票。他以为自己把秘密锁在了灵魂深处的洞穴里，可我请各位作证——那秘密就钉在他脸上。瞧瞧他！"

我狼狈不堪地扭动着身子，拼命躲避桌子对面那双惊讶的眼睛。我恨不得掐死麦克奈特，顺便把在座的其他人都干掉。

===Original===
“It isn’t fair,” I said as coolly as I could. “I have my fingers crossed; you are five against one.”

“And to think that there was a murder on that very train,” broke in the lady in yellow. “It was a perfect crescendo of horrors, wasn’t it? And what became of the murdered man, Mr. Blakeley?”

McKnight had the sense to jump into the conversation and save my reply.

“They say good Pittsburgers go to Atlantic City when they die,” he said. “So—we are reasonably certain the gentleman did *not* go to the seashore.”

===Chinese===
"这不公平，"我尽量冷静地说。"我一个人，你们五个对一个。"

"想想看，那趟车上还发生了谋杀案，"黄裙子女士又插嘴了。"简直是恐怖的高潮迭起，不是吗？那个被杀的人后来怎么样了，布莱克利先生？"

麦克奈特识时务地接过话头，替我解了围。

"他们说好匹兹堡人死了以后去大西洋城（Atlantic City），"他说。"所以——我们有充分的理由认为那位先生*没有*去海边。"

===Original===
The meal was over at last, and once in the drawing-room it was clear we hung heavy on the hostess’ hands. “It is so hard to get people for bridge in September,” she wailed, “there is absolutely nobody in town. Six is a dreadful number.”

“It’s a good poker number,” her husband suggested.

The matter settled itself, however. I was hopeless, save as a dummy; Miss West said it was too hot for cards, and went out on a balcony that overlooked the Mall. With obvious relief Mrs. Dallas had the card-table brought, and I was face to face with the minute I had dreaded and hoped for for a week.

===Chinese===
晚饭终于吃完了，一到客厅，我们就显然成了女主人的负担。"九月份要凑人打桥牌可真难，"她哀叹道，"镇上根本没人。六个人实在太尴尬了。"

"打扑克倒是好人数，"她丈夫提议道。

不过事情自有了着落。我除了当个摆设毫无用处；韦斯特小姐说天太热不适合打牌，就去了俯瞰林荫大道的阳台。达拉斯夫人明显松了口气，吩咐摆上牌桌，而我则终于要面对这一周以来又怕又盼的那个时刻了。

===Original===
Now it had come, it was more difficult than I had anticipated. I do not know if there was a moon, but there was the urban substitute for it—the arc light. It threw the shadow of the balcony railing in long black bars against her white gown, and as it swung sometimes her face was in the light. I drew a chair close so that I could watch her.

“Do you know,” I said, when she made no effort at speech, “that you are a much more formidable person tonight, in that gown, than you were the last time I saw you?”

The light swung on her face; she was smiling faintly. “The hat with the green ribbons!” she said. “I must take it back; I had almost forgotten.”

===Chinese===
此刻它来了，却比我预想的更难。我不知道当时有没有月亮，但有城市的替代品——弧光灯。它把阳台栏杆的影子拉成长长的黑条，投射在她白裙子上，灯摆来摆去，她的脸时而隐入暗处，时而映在光中。我搬了把椅子凑近，好仔细看着她。

"你知道吗，"她没有要开口的意思，我便说道，"你今晚穿这条裙子，比上次我见到你的时候要厉害得多？"

灯光晃到她脸上；她浅浅地笑了。"那顶系着绿缎带的帽子！"她说。"我得把话收回来；我差点忘了。"

===Original===
“I have not forgotten—anything.” I pulled myself up short. This was hardly loyalty to Richey. His voice came through the window just then, and perhaps I was wrong, but I thought she raised her head to listen.

“Look at this hand,” he was saying. “Regular pianola: you could play it with your feet.”

“He’s a dear, isn’t he?” Alison said unexpectedly. “No matter how depressed and downhearted I am, I always cheer up when I see Richey.”

===Chinese===
"我什么都没忘——一件也没有。"我猛地刹住话头。这可算不上对里奇的忠诚。他的声音恰在此时从窗口传来，也许是我多心了，但我看见她抬起头来倾听。

"看看这手牌，"他正说道。"简直像自动钢琴：用脚都能弹。"

"他是个好人，对吧？"艾莉森（Alison）出其不意地说。"不管我多沮丧、多消沉，只要看见里奇，我总会高兴起来。"

===Original===
“He’s more than that,” I returned warmly. “He is the most honorable fellow I know. If he wasn’t so much that way, he would have a career before him. He wanted to put on the doors of our offices, Blakeley and McKnight, P.B.H., which is Poor But Honest.”

From my comparative poverty to the wealth of the girl beside me was a single mental leap. From that wealth to the grandfather who was responsible for it was another.

“I wonder if you know that I had been to Pittsburg to see your grandfather when I met you?” I said.

“You?” She was surprised.

“Yes. And you remember the alligator bag that I told you was exchanged for the one you cut off my arm?” She nodded expectantly. “Well, in that valise were the forged Andy Bronson notes, and Mr. Gilmore’s deposition that they were forged.”

===Chinese===
"他不止于此，"我热切地说。"他是我认识的最正直的人。要不是他正直到这份上，他本来能有一番事业。他想在我们事务所的门上写：布莱克利-麦克奈特，P.B.H.，意思是'穷但诚实'（Poor But Honest）。"

从我相对的贫穷到身旁这个姑娘的富有，只需要一次心理跳跃。从那份富有到造就它的祖父，又是另一次。

"不知道你是否知道，我遇见你之前刚去过匹兹堡看你祖父？"我说。

"你？"她很惊讶。

"是的。你记得我跟你说过的那只鳄鱼皮箱吗？就是跟你砍断我胳膊时拿走的那只调换了的那只？"她期待地点了点头。"那只皮箱里装着安迪·布朗森（Andy Bronson）的伪造票据，还有吉尔摩先生证明它们是伪造的证词。"

===Original===
She was on her feet in an instant. “In that bag!” she cried. “Oh, why didn’t you tell me that before? Oh, it’s so ridiculous, so—so hopeless. Why, I could—”

She stopped suddenly and sat down again. “I do not know that I am sorry, after all,” she said after a pause. “Mr. Bronson was a friend of my father’s. I—I suppose it was a bad thing for you, losing the papers?”

===Chinese===
她立刻站了起来。"在那只箱子里！"她喊道。"哦，你为什么之前不告诉我？哦，这太荒谬了，太——太绝望了。我本来可以——"

她突然停住，重新坐了下来。"说到底，我不知道自己是否真的遗憾，"她停顿了一下说道。"布朗森先生是我父亲的朋友。我——我猜那些文件丢了对你是件糟糕的事吧？"

===Original===
“Well, it was not a good thing,” I conceded. “While we are on the subject of losing things, do you remember—do you know that I still have your gold purse?”

She did not reply at once. The shadow of a column was over her face, but I guessed that she was staring at me.

“*You* have it!” She almost whispered.

“I picked it up in the street car,” I said, with a cheerfulness I did not feel. “It looks like a very opulent little purse.”

Why didn’t she speak about the necklace? For just a careless word to make me sane again!

===Chinese===
"嗯，确实不是什么好事，"我承认道。"说到丢东西，你还记不记得——你知道你的金荷包还在我这儿吗？"

她没有立刻回答。一根廊柱的影子落在她脸上，但我猜她正盯着我。

"*你*拿着它！"她几乎是耳语般地说。

"我在电车上捡到的，"我用并不真切的轻快语气说。"看上去是个很阔气的小荷包。"

她为什么不说那条项链的事？只要随口一句话就能让我恢复正常啊！

===Original===
“You!” she repeated, horror-stricken. And then I produced the purse and held it out on my palm. “I should have sent it to you before, I suppose, but, as you know, I have been laid up since the wreck.”

We both saw McKnight at the same moment. He had pulled the curtains aside and was standing looking out at us. The tableau of give and take was unmistakable; the gold purse, her outstretched hand, my own attitude. It was over in a second; then he came out and lounged on the balcony railing.

“They’re mad at me in there,” he said airily, “so I came out. I suppose the reason they call it bridge is because so many people get cross over it.”

===Chinese===
"你！"她惊恐地重复道。然后我掏出那只荷包，摊在手掌上递过去。"我早该寄给你了，可你知道，车祸之后我一直在养伤。"

我们同时看见了麦克奈特。他拉开窗帘，站在那里看着我们。那幅递来接去的画面一览无遗——金荷包、她伸出的手、我的姿势。一切在一瞬间结束；接着他走出来，懒洋洋地靠在阳台栏杆上。

"里面那帮人生我气了，"他轻描淡写地说，"所以我出来了。我想桥牌之所以叫桥牌，就是因为好多人为它翻了脸。"

===Original===
The heat broke up the card group soon after, and they all came out for the night breeze. I had no more words alone with Alison.

I went back to the Incubator for the night. We said almost nothing on the way home; there was a constraint between us for the first time that I could remember. It was too early for bed, and so we smoked in the living-room and tried to talk of trivial things. After a time even those failed, and we sat silent. It was McKnight who finally broached the subject.

“And so she wasn’t at Seal Harbor at all.”

“No.”

===Chinese===
热浪很快就打散了牌局，大家都出来乘晚风。我再没机会跟艾莉森单独说话了。

晚上回到"孵化器"（Incubator）。回家的路上我们几乎没说什么话；我们之间出现了我记忆中的第一次拘谨。睡觉还太早，于是我们在客厅里抽烟，试图聊些无关紧要的事。过了一会儿，连那些话题也聊不下去了，我们相对无言。最后还是麦克奈特挑起了话头。

"所以她根本就不在海豹港（Seal Harbor）。"

"不在。"

===Original===
“Do you know where she was, Lollie?”

“Somewhere near Cresson.”

“And that was the purse—her purse—with the broken necklace in it?”

“Yes, it was. You understand, don’t you, Rich, that, having given her my word, I couldn’t tell you?”

“I understand a lot of things,” he said, without bitterness.

We sat for some time and smoked. Then Richey got up and stretched himself. “I’m off to bed, old man,” he said. “Need any help with that game arm of yours?”

“No, thanks,” I returned.

I heard him go into his room and lock the door. It was a bad hour for me. The first shadow between us, and the shadow of a girl at that.

===Chinese===
"你知道她在哪儿吗，洛利？"

"在克雷森（Cresson）附近。"

"那只荷包——她的荷包——里面装着那条断了的项链？"

"是的。你理解的，对吧，里奇，既然我答应过她，我就不能告诉你？"

"我理解的事多了去了，"他没有带任何苦涩地说。

我们坐着抽了一会儿烟。然后里奇站起来伸了个懒腰。"我去睡了，老伙计，"他说。"你那条伤胳膊需要帮忙吗？"

"不用了，谢谢，"我答道。

我听见他进了自己的房间，锁上了门。对我来说，那是个糟糕的时刻。我们之间出现了第一道阴影，而且还是一个姑娘的阴影。

## XVII / 再访农舍

===Original===
McKnight is always a sympathizer with the early worm. It was late when he appeared. Perhaps, like myself, he had not slept well. But he was apparently cheerful enough, and he made a better breakfast than I did. It was one o'clock before we got to Baltimore. After a half hour's wait we took a local for M——, the station near which the cinematograph picture had been taken.

We passed the scene of the wreck, McKnight with curiosity, I with a sickening sense of horror. Back in the fields was the little farmhouse where Alison West and I had intended getting coffee, and winding away from the track, maple trees shading it on each sides, was the lane where we had stopped to rest, and where I had—it seemed presumption beyond belief now—where I had tried to comfort her by patting her hand.

===Chinese===
麦克奈特（McKnight）向来是早起的鸟儿的同情者。他到得很晚。也许跟我一样，他也没睡好。但他看上去精神不错，早餐吃得比我多。我们一点钟才到巴尔的摩（Baltimore）。等了半小时后，我们搭上了开往M——的慢车，活动电影拍到的那段画面就是在那附近拍的。

我们经过了车祸现场，麦克奈特带着好奇，我带着一种令人作呕的恐惧感。田野深处就是那栋小农舍，艾莉森·韦斯特（Alison West）和我本打算进去喝杯咖啡；从铁轨蜿蜒出去、两旁枫树成荫的那条小路，就是我们停下来歇脚的地方，也是我——现在回想起来简直不可置信——我曾拍了拍她的手试图安慰她的地方。

===Original===
We got out at M——, a small place with two or three houses and a general store. The station was a one-roomed affair, with a railed-off place at the end, where a scale, a telegraph instrument and a chair constituted the entire furnishing.

The station agent was a young man with a shrewd face. He stopped hammering a piece of wood over a hole in the floor to ask where we wanted to go.

"We're not going," said McKnight, "we're coming. Have a cigar?"

The agent took it with an inquiring glance, first at it and then at us.

"We want to ask you a few questions," began McKnight, perching himself on the railing and kicking the chair forward for me. "Or, rather, this gentleman does."

"Wait a minute," said the agent, glancing through the window. "There's a hen in that crate choking herself to death."

===Chinese===
我们在M——下了车，一个只有两三栋房子和一家杂货铺的小地方。车站是一间屋子的规模，尽头用栏杆隔出一块地方，里面一把秤、一台电报机和一把椅子，便是全部家当。

车站管理员是个面相精明的年轻人。他停下往地板洞上钉木板的活儿，问我们要去哪儿。

"我们不是走，"麦克奈特说，"我们是来。来根雪茄？"

管理员接过去，先打量了一眼雪茄，又打量了我们。

"我们想问你几个问题，"麦克奈特一边说，一边自己骑上栏杆，用脚把椅子踢到我面前，"或者更准确地说，是这位先生要问。"

"等一下，"管理员朝窗外瞥了一眼，"有只母鸡在箱子里快把自己噎死了。"

===Original===
He was back in a minute, and took up his position near a sawdust-filled box that did duty as a cuspidor.

"Now fire away," he said.

"In the first place," I began, "do you remember the day the Washington Flier was wrecked below here?"

"Do I!" he said. "Did Jonah remember the whale?"

"Were you on the platform here when the first section passed?"

"I was."

"Do you recall seeing a man hanging to the platform of the last car?"

"There was no one hanging there when she passed here," he said with conviction. "I watched her out of sight."

"Did you see anything that morning of a man about my size, carrying a small grip, and wearing dark clothes and a derby hat?" I asked eagerly.

McKnight was trying to look unconcerned, but I was frankly anxious. It was clear that the man had jumped somewhere in the mile of track just beyond.

===Chinese===
他一分钟后就回来了，站到一个装满锯末充当痰盂的箱子旁边。

"尽管问吧，"他说。

"首先，"我开口道，"你还记得'华盛顿号特快'（Washington Flier）在这下边出事那天吗？"

"怎么能忘！"他说，"约拿还记得鲸鱼呢。"

"第一列经过的时候你在站台上吗？"

"在。"

"你记得看到有个人挂在最后一节车厢的台子上吗？"

"她经过这儿的时候没有人挂在那儿，"他语气肯定，"我一直看到她消失在视线里。"

"那天早上你有没有看见一个跟我差不多高的人，拎着一个小旅行箱，穿深色衣服，戴圆顶礼帽？"我急切地问道。

麦克奈特在努力装出一副漫不经心的样子，但我已经掩饰不住焦虑了。很清楚，那个人就在前方那一英里铁轨的某处跳了车。

===Original===
"Well, yes, I did." The agent cleared his throat. "When the smash came the operator at MX sent word along the wire, both ways. I got it here, and I was pretty near crazy, though I knew it wasn't any fault of mine.

"I was standing on the track looking down, for I couldn't leave the office, when a young fellow with light hair limped up to me and asked me what that smoke was over there.

"'That's what's left of the Washington Flier,' I said, 'and I guess there's souls going up in that smoke.'

"'Do you mean the first section?' he said, getting kind of greenish-yellow.

"'That's what I mean,' I said; 'split to kindling wood because Rafferty, on the second section, didn't want to be late.'

"He put his hand out in front of him, and the satchel fell with a bang.

"'My God!' he said, and dropped right on the track in a pile.

"I got him into the station and he came around, but he kept on groaning something awful. He'd sprained his ankle, and when he got a little better I drove him over in Carter's milk wagon to the Carter place, and I reckon he stayed there a spell."

===Chinese===
"嗯，是的，我看见了。"管理员清了清嗓子，"出事以后，MX站的电报员沿线路往两个方向都发了电报。我收到了，当时差点急疯了，虽然我知道跟我一点关系也没有。

"我站在铁轨上往下看——我不能离岗——这时一个金发年轻人一瘸一拐地走过来，问我那边冒烟的是什么。

"'那是华盛顿号特快剩下的东西，'我说，'我猜那烟里有灵魂在往上升。'

"'你是说第一列吗？'他说，脸变得有点发青发黄。

"'就是那个，'我说，'因为第二列的拉弗蒂（Rafferty）不想迟到，第一列被撞成了劈柴。'

"他伸出双手，手提箱砰的一声掉在地上。

"'天哪！'他叫了一声，整个人就瘫倒了。

"我把他弄进车站，他醒过来了，但一直在痛苦地呻吟。他脚踝扭伤了，等他好一点以后，我赶着卡特家的牛奶车把他送到了卡特（Carter）家，我估摸他在那儿住了些日子。"

===Original===
"That's all, is it?" I asked.

"That's all—or, no, there's something else. About noon that day one of the Carter twins came down with a note from him asking me to send a long-distance message to someone in Washington."

"To whom?" I asked eagerly.

"I reckon I've forgot the name, but the message was that this fellow—Sullivan was his name—was at M——, and if the man had escaped from the wreck would he come to see him."

"He wouldn't have sent that message to me," I said to McKnight, rather crestfallen. "He'd have every object in keeping out of my way."

"There might be reasons," McKnight observed judicially. "He might not have found the papers then."

"Was the name Blakeley?" I asked.

"It might have been—I can't say. But the man wasn't there, and there was a lot of noise. I couldn't hear well. Then in half an hour down came the other twin to say the gentleman was taking on awful and didn't want the message sent."

"He's gone, of course?"
